| The loader's build is reproducible bit for bit, with a gate: TASKS item 31, CLAUDE.md §75 (2026-11-10, branch `claude/clever-robinson-751c44`) | Two release builds of the same loader source differed in 10 to 12 bytes; they are now the same bytes. Two causes, each shown by experiment with the pinned toolchain: the linker wrote the time of the link into the PE header, and it wrote a CodeView record whose identifier is a hash of a PDB that holds the path of a directory rustc names at random for each link. Two link arguments for the UEFI target in `.cargo/config.toml`, `/DEBUG:NONE` and `/TIMESTAMP:0`; neither is enough alone. The image then has a zero time stamp and no debug directory, which is nothing but what UEFI 2.11 §2.1.1 says firmware uses (the second argument was `/Brepro` until that section could be read). No line of the loader's source changed, and its `.text` is the same bytes. A gate in `cargo xtask check`, and `cargo xtask reproducible-gate`, builds the loader three ways and the kernel ELF twice from nothing in two target directories and compares the files. Found and not changed: both images hold the path the toolchain is installed at, so another machine cannot give the same bytes yet (item 32) | `cargo xtask check` (1,205 host tests, 0 failed, 8 ignored; Loom 39 models, 60 controls) and `cargo xtask boot-test` (11 of 11 scenarios, first attempt; 56 of 56 proofs) at `42391cd`, and the same at `32f1a94` for the first form; the gate passing in 31 s and failing on four controls planted in the real tree (no argument, each argument alone, and a random build ID in the kernel); 11 new host tests; the loader that booted has the digest the gate's two builds gave; the ledger `docs/research/2026-11-10-loader-reproducible-build.md`; `SOURCE_PROVENANCE.md`. The reproducible-build gate fails if an image holds the toolchain's directory, Cargo's home or the checkout. The owner chose the remap over removing `rust-src`. **Found during S3-2 (2026-11-10):**
- **Reading without room:** a reader cannot drop a message it does not want without making room for it (Zircon has a discard option). Neither B1 nor B3 needs it; a service domain may.

## Next, in order

**First, per ADR-0011 N14 (accepted 2026-11-09), before the items below add to the kernel:** the Framekernel framework crate and its gate — **step 1 is done (2026-11-09): `framekernel/frame/` holds every `unsafe` in the kernel and `cargo xtask check` fails on any outside it**; then the interface language and its generator, with the kernel's own calls as the first consumer — **the specification and the generator exist (see Done), and the owner accepted the format on 2026-11-09; the kernel's call table, decoding and statuses are generated from it (S3-0, see Done)**; then caller identity, the start record and the service directory, with memory objects and ports — **scoped and its thirteen interface decisions accepted (2026-11-09, ADR-0005 Amendment 4, decision sheet items 56–68); not built. Tasks, in order: S3-0 the kernel uses OIDL's generated call table (done); S3-1 process and thread objects, `TERMINATED`, process IDs (done); S3-2 caller identity and handle records (done); S3-3 the start record (done); S3-4 the service directory (done); S3-5a in the framework, address spaces that change while threads run, shared frames and the kernel half fixed at boot (ADR-0004 H2) (done, with its independent review's fixes); S3-5b memory objects (done); S3-6 events and ports; S3-7 the system-call threat model (begun with S3-2: `docs/engineering/THREAT_MODEL_SYSCALLS.md`; `docs/research/2026-11-09-n14-step3-scope.md`)**; then `origin.core` and one end-to-end service domain (Diagnostics), measured before a second is built.

1. **NVMe interrupts:** the PCI capability list, MSI-X, a vector per queue, and completions that wake a wait queue instead of polling; timed waits (deadlines on the monotonic clock).
2. **IOMMU (§32):** VT-d under QEMU's `intel-iommu` — **done 2026-11-08** (ADR-0007, Accepted): the DMAR table, DMA remapping for NVMe and virtio-net with blocked-DMA and fault proofs, and interrupt remapping (see STATUS and `SOURCE_PROVENANCE.md`). Delivery to an APIC ID above 255 is proven live since the CPU-index work (ADR-0008, `boot-x2apic`). I/O APIC entries in remappable format, through IRTEs that check the I/O APIC's source ID — **done 2026-11-08**. AMD-Vi under QEMU's `amd-iommu` — **done 2026-11-08** (ADR-0007's AMD-Vi section, Accepted): the IVRS, default-deny device tables, DMA and per-device interrupt remapping with x2APIC destinations, the same proofs and drivers, `boot-matrix` over both IOMMUs. Remaining: RMRR and IVMD identity maps, scalable mode; an interrupt table for the I/O APIC's DeviceID under AMD-Vi (its pins stay masked there until then); the keyboard IRQ1 to a handler through `iommu::route_ioapic_pin`; a live level-triggered pin (PCI INTx) with its EOI path; the source-ID check on I/O APIC interrupts on hardware (QEMU's I/O APIC sends no requester ID). Threat T4 (bus mastering cleared at enumeration, drivers enable it only after attaching, translation before SMP start; windows remain, threat model) — **done 2026-11-08** (merged 2026-11-09).
3. **Parser hardening (§72):** deterministic mutation runs over every firmware and device parser (ACPI, PCI, NVMe Identify, GPT, RTC), with a seeded generator and no fuzzing crates — **done 2026-11-08**: the whole inventory, with a soak per parser (about 1.3 million mutated inputs in the default 1 s run; `ORIGIN_MUTATION_SCALE` scales it). The soaks found 13 library bugs and several unchecked device values in the kernel; each is fixed with a regression test (see `SOURCE_PROVENANCE.md`, "Parser hardening"). The multi-fault soak over the ONBF parsers is **done 2026-11-08**: the container, OCBOR-1, the KRN manifest, the signature block, Merkle verification and signature verification, each judged by a model written from the specification, about 98,000 cases in the default 1 s; 5 bugs found, each fixed with a regression test (see `SOURCE_PROVENANCE.md`). Remaining: coverage-guided fuzzing and Kani bounds (§73); the malformed-device paths on a fault-injecting device model or hardware (§74).
4. **M3, scheduling proper** (ADR-0003 K7 is the baseline; ADR-0008 indexes its queues): priorities and a fairness policy (EEVDF to be evaluated, §26), load balancing; pad the per-worker counters. Per-CPU state sized at discovery instead of 64 static slots (ADR-0008 C6) and CPU hot-plug (MADT "online capable"). CPUID topology for §27 (dies, modules, per CPU) — **done 2026-11-08** (see Done): the next §27 step is using it for placement, and hybrid-core *capacity*, which no leaf here gives (`docs/research/2026-11-08-cpu-topology-leaf-1fh.md`).
5. **M4:** ADR-0005 is accepted. Done on 2026-11-08 (first slice, merged 2026-11-09): process objects, the kernel's system-call layer with copies against the calling process's tables, `wait_one`, and handles and channels through them; see the Done table. **Next:** the start record (P6), memory objects (P7), the Loader Service and `.ose` loading; PCIDs (ADR-0004 H5); events, ports and timers (P3, P9); termination through a `manage` handle and the crash record (P10, P11); memory and thread quotas (P11). ADR-0005 Amendment 1, accepted on 2026-11-08, covers what this slice needed and the ADR did not say. The slice that adds sender identity (B1) also makes `channel_read` report each handle's type and rights (Amendment 3, B3), so generated service stubs can check them.
6. **Signature block (ONBF §7.1):** host signing in original Rust (`onbf-sign`), the loader requiring a signed KRN outside developer mode, and a compiled-in trust store with a test key — **done 2026-11-08** (`docs/research/2026-11-08-krn-signature-verification.md`; errata V1–V6 and O2–O5 decided by the owner). A loader built without `test-trust-store` trusts nothing and refuses every kernel, until Origin has its own keys. Remaining: OKC chains (O3 makes them wait for the SYS profile's signed time), Origin's real root and kernel keys and the signing ceremony (HSM, §60), and B7's Secure Boot first stage. Anti-rollback and TPM measurement (§7.6, §8.1) stay with the SYS profile.
7. **Block layer and filesystem (§39–§41):** one I/O queue per CPU, a block cache, then a filesystem ADR.
8. **Formal and concurrency checks:** Loom models for `SpinLock`, `RwLock` and the wait-queue protocol (§73) — **done 2026-11-08** (`cargo xtask loom`, part of `check`): the real locks, the real wait-queue protocol and the real run queue, 18 models and 22 negative controls, the protocol moved out of the kernel binary into `origin_sync::wait` so it can run under Loom. What each model proves, what it leaves unproved and the approved crates are in `SOURCE_PROVENANCE.md`, "Loom models". Remaining: Kani bounds for `paging`, `frames`, `heap`.
9. **CPU-feature CPUID tests** (key registry bits 0–5) and XCR0 checks; **M1 follow-ups** (A/B deployment, boot-attempt recording).

10. **The pre-merge review's open list:** the Framekernel's trusted base, speculation controls, CET, TCP's acceptance checks, the open IOMMU threats, NVMe reset, scheduling and allocator scaling (`docs/engineering/PRE_MERGE_REVIEW_2026-11-09.md`, "Not fixed").

11. **IOMMU after the Rev 3.11 check (2026-11-09; AMD-Vi ledger §7, "Found, not changed"):** clear Enable No Snoop in each attached function where a VT-d unit lacks snoop control; IVMD semantics and placement, with question 4's identity maps; whether the IVRS parser stops one IVHD's walk instead of refusing the table; a precedence for several entries naming one DeviceID; decoding a type 10h block's feature field. On the first AMD machine: record which address form an interrupt fault carries, and that the SMI filter the firmware set is still on after boot.
12. **The public repository's files (decision item 79, CLAUDE.md §6):** `CONTRIBUTING.md` with DCO sign-off and an original-source declaration (§3), `SECURITY.md` for private vulnerability reports (§60), `CODEOWNERS` for the kernel and SDK interfaces (§78). Also: the README's status line still says "There is no kernel yet".

    - one table from vendor, family and model to core generation, built from Intel's and AMD's own publications (the numbers the rule uses are confirmed in `docs/research/2026-11-10-cpu-identification.md`; Diamond Rapids is not), and the home for errata keyed by family, model and stepping;

    **Built 2026-11-12 on `claude/t16-cpu-ident-621a3b`, not merged:** `origin_frame::cpuident`, the boot line, `boot-identity` in `boot-matrix`; the contract and its result are under "Task contracts".

**From the design review of 2026-11-10 (decision items 106–123), in order.** The findings and their evidence are in `docs/engineering/DESIGN_REVIEW_2026-11-10.md`. Each task writes its §78 contract before it starts (item 122).

17. **The assembly blocks' options, and four gates (items 107, 110, 111, 123), now — done 2026-11-10** (see Done, and the result below the contract):
    - remove `preserves_flags` from the five blocks in `framekernel/frame/src/cpu.rs` that use `or` or `and`;
    - for the seven that only move to or from a control register (six in `cpu.rs`, one in `boot/uefi/src/main.rs`), read Intel's and AMD's entries for that move (§16), and remove the option unless both say the status flags are unchanged;
    - four gates in `cargo xtask check`: an `asm!` block that declares the option holds only instructions known not to write flags; only listed files hold `unsafe`, and every other kernel file forbids it; no PDF or similar document is tracked outside an allowlist; §11's lint also refuses `cfg(unix)`, `target_family` and `target_env`.

    **Contract (CLAUDE.md §78, decision item 122), written on 2026-11-10 before any code:**

    | Field | |
    |---|---|
    | Task ID | TASKS item 17, on branch `claude/funny-torvalds-9a28a0`, cut from `docs/design-review-2026-11-10` at `2fbaea6` |
    | Objective | No assembly block of the kernel or the loader declares `preserves_flags` unless every instruction in it leaves the flags alone. Four gates in `cargo xtask check` keep that true, and three more rules with it: only listed files of the framework hold `unsafe`; no document is tracked outside an allowlist; no Origin code outside `developer-tools/` depends on `unix`, `target_family` or `target_env` |
    | Source ownership | This task alone edits: the `options(…)` of assembly blocks in `framekernel/frame/src/cpu.rs` and `boot/uefi/src/main.rs`, with the comments beside them, and no instruction; one added line, `#![forbid(unsafe_code)]`, in each framework file that holds no `unsafe`; `developer-tools/xtask/src/`; a ledger under `docs/research/`; this file, `SOURCE_PROVENANCE.md` and `STATUS.md`. It changes no kernel interface, no ABI, no boot proof and no ADR. Task 18 works in the same tree at the same time and may add framework files and touch `cpu.rs`: the shared notes folder tells it what the new gates will ask of a new file |
    | Architecture contract | CLAUDE.md §13's two rules for privileged code (items 110, 111), §11's lint (item 123) and §17's rule that restricted specifications stay out of the repository (item 107). ADR-0011 N6, N12 and N13: the framework is the one place with `unsafe`, and a gate says so. Nothing here is an ABI or format decision, so no ADR is needed (§84) |
    | Relevant specifications | Read on the day, with revision and date recorded in `docs/research/2026-11-10-asm-flags.md` (§16): the Rust Reference's rules for `preserves_flags`, as Rust 1.99.1 has them; Intel's SDM Volume 2 and AMD's APM Volumes 3 to 5 for the moves to and from control and debug registers, and for every instruction the gate's list names |
    | Required dependencies | None new. `xtask` stays `std` only. The document gate asks `git` which files are tracked, as `turns.rs` already asks it for the common directory |
    | Security invariants | (1) An assembly block's options are true of its instructions. Removing the option only makes the compiler assume less. (2) No instruction changes, so every processor's CR0, CR4, EFER and CR3 hold what they held before. (3) Each gate fails closed: a block it cannot read, an instruction off its list, a framework file neither listed nor forbidding, a tracked file it cannot read, or a scan that saw nothing, each fails. (4) No boot proof is weakened, and no bound changes |
    | Performance constraints | The kernel's cost may only rise by a comparison recomputed after a block that lost the option, beside a control-register move that costs far more. It is measured where it can be measured exactly: the kernel's and the loader's machine code are compared before and after. The context-switch and IPC costs the boots print are recorded as readings under an emulator and no more (§53). The gates together add under five seconds to `cargo xtask check` |
    | Required tests | For each gate, unit tests that plant a violation and show it caught, and a test that the real tree passes. Each gate also fails on a violation planted in the real tree, which is then undone. `cargo xtask check`. `cargo xtask boot-matrix`, because the changed blocks run on every processor at every boot |
    | Actual deliverables | The corrected options; the four gates with their tests; the ledger; the provenance entry; this item in the Done table with its evidence |
    | Acceptance conditions | `cargo xtask check` passes. `cargo xtask boot-matrix` passes with no proof or bound changed. No block that declares `preserves_flags` holds an instruction off the gate's list, and each instruction on the list has both vendors' entries cited for it. A boot that fails is reported with its output, not retried until it passes |

    **Result (2026-11-10): done.** The evidence is in the Done table and in `SOURCE_PROVENANCE.md`. Where it differs from the three bullets and the contract above:

    - **22 blocks lost the option, not 12.** The vendors disagree on the control-register moves: AMD says no flag is affected, and Intel says all six status flags are undefined. So the seven lost it. Intel says the same of the debug registers, which the review did not name: 2 more. The gate's list then holds only instructions that both vendors say change no status flag, and 8 more blocks used an instruction that is not one (`xrstor`, `fxrstor`, `xsave`, `fxsave`, `lfence`, `clflush`, `movq`). 18 blocks keep the option. No instruction changed in the source, and none in what is built: the kernel's and the loader's disassemblies are the same before and after.
    - **AMD's Volume 5 was not used.** AMD serves it as public, with a confidentiality marking on every page. The ledger says so; nothing rests on it.
    - **The document gate also reads the branch's history, and refuses archives.** A document deleted by a later commit is still published by a push.
    - **A tracked file missing from the working tree is judged by its name alone,** not failed as invariant 3 says: a file deleted and not yet staged would otherwise stop every check.
    - **The §11 lint also refuses `cfg(windows)`** (the other short spelling of `target_family`), and reads manifests and `.cargo/config.toml`.
    - **The `unsafe` gate also asks for the attribute at the library root of each kernel crate outside the framework,** where before it asked it of one.
18. **The kernel's random-number generator (item 113), before S3-5b lands:**
    - an ADR first: the SP 800-90A and 800-90B revisions pinned (§16), seeding from RDSEED, RDRAND and a firmware seed the loader passes, reseeding, state for each processor, health tests, a threat model;
    - then HMAC_DRBG over `origin-crypto`'s SHA-512, with NIST's vectors, as a framework service;
    - every consumer draws from it: `launch.rs`, `virtionet.rs`, the loader's KASLR draw and S3-5b's mappings;
    - "hardware entropy" becomes Withdraw in `bootproof.rs`, its host test and ADR-0013's inventory, in one commit (ADR-0013 Amendment 1).

    **State, 2026-11-10.** The first half, 18a, is built: the mechanism (HMAC-SHA-512 and HMAC_DRBG in `origin-crypto`, host-tested), the ledger (`docs/research/2026-11-10-kernel-random-numbers.md`) and ADR-0016, which the owner accepted on 2026-11-10 as proposed (decision sheet items 145 to 151). The second half, 18b, was built on 2026-11-11 on `claude/18b-kernel-random`; its contract and its result are under "Task contracts", and the contract answers the review note below as S1 to S7. **Left, as 18c:** the loader's call to the firmware's random-number protocol (ADR-0016 R10 step 1), for a session that has not read EDK II's declaration of it (decision item 92; the facts are in the ledger's §8), with the place for its 64 bytes ready in `boot/uefi/src/random.rs`. **Left to the branch that lands second (done on 2026-11-11 in the batch's merge of 18b, `c2245ef`; what follows is the plan as written):** two draws on `land/s3-part2` (read at `e9eae13` on 2026-11-11). `framekernel/src/memory_object.rs` places a mapping through `entropy::hardware_u64`, which becomes `origin_frame::random::next_u64`. `framekernel/frame/src/stack.rs` draws the stack region's base through `cpu::rdseed` and `cpu::rdrand`, and that is not a change of one call: `early.rs` draws it where the kernel's tables are made, before the heap and the clock, so before the kernel's generator exists. The owner decided how on 2026-11-11 (decision item 166; ADR-0016 Amendment 1): the loader's generator makes a third 8-byte request and hands the value over directly after its seed, and `draw_region` takes it through `origin_frame::random::take_stack_value`, which this branch provides and proves. `framekernel/src/stacks.rs` does not draw: its proof reads which instruction the framework drew from, and changes with it. What 18b built, in the order planned: the standard's known-answer self-test, with NIST's vectors (now in `tests/kat`) as its answers; the policy crate (`security/random`: the health tests, retries, seeding and reseeding, host-tested); the framework's service with a generator for each processor, the volatile overwrites and the stack overwrite; the gate that keeps `rdrand` and `rdseed` to two files; boot info 0.6 and the loader's generator; the three consumers; the Withdraw row with its controls; the cost of a request printed at boot.

    **For 18b's contract.** A review note from the coordinating session, checked against ADR-0016's text on 2026-11-10 and recorded in its "What this does not decide". It is not an owner decision and changes nothing accepted. The first three points are the reviewer's; the start-up figure and the fourth point were added on checking.
    - Say where a reseed's draws run. R6 reseeds before a request, R7 runs a request with interrupts off, and the ADR does not say whether the draws do. One reseed is up to 800 RDSEED tries (8 draws of 100 tries each, R4). A start-up test tried again at a reseed is up to 102,400 (1,024 draws; R4 item 8). Say also whether the root's draws are made while its lock is held.
    - Bound the longest time interrupts stay off, and print the measured worst case at boot beside a request's cost (P2; rules 21 and 22).
    - Say what backs off. While a ring-3 program keeps RDSEED dry (ADR-0016 T5), every due reseed would otherwise spend its whole retry budget before each request. Say how soon a reseed that got "not now" is tried again, and after how many tries it takes RDRAND instead: T5 names that fall-back, and R6 gives it only for an instruction that is not healthy.
    - Wherever the draws run, they are seed material, so R9's stack overwrite has to cover them.
    - An answer that changes R4, R6 or R7 as accepted is an amendment for the owner (§84) before it is built.

    - `docs/engineering/THREAT_MODEL_SYSTEM.md`: the attackers, what each edition defends, which component covers each, and each component model's exclusions covered or accepted by name;
    - the ADR, from Intel's and AMD's documents read under §16: `IA32_ARCH_CAPABILITIES`, enhanced and automatic IBRS, IBPB, the SMT policy, supervisor shadow stacks, FRED and LASS where CPUID reports them, and the compiler's options;
    - an index-masking primitive in the framework, for the handle table and the call table;
    - a row for a nested NMI in the system-call threat model.

(2) Z9: a framework primitive, `origin_frame::nospec`, for an index and for a user address that come from ring 3, in a masked and a fenced form chosen once at boot; used by the handle table and by the copies from and to user memory. (3) Z1: one framework module decodes what each processor reports and plans the controls; every boot prints what is on and what the processor does not offer; it never refuses to boot. (4) Z2 and Z5: enhanced IBRS with `BHI_DIS_S` and `RRSBA_DIS_S`, or automatic IBRS with STIBP, set on each processor as it starts; the entry path clears the general-purpose registers once it has saved them. (5) Z3: an IBPB before a processor runs a different process in ring 3 |

    | Security invariants | (1) The primitive's safe interface returns only an index below the length it was given, or nothing; no caller can make it return anything else. (2) A control bit is written only where the processor enumerates it, so no write can fault. Writes only narrow speculation: none can widen it. (3) A processor that offers nothing boots and says so. (4) Every general-purpose register is saved before it is cleared, and restored from the frame as before. (5) On a processor that has IBPB, no instruction of a different process runs in ring 3 after another's until the barrier has been issued on that processor. (6) No instruction of the entry path changes other than those added. (7) The form the primitive uses when the processor is not recognised is the fenced one |
    | Performance constraints | Measured, not budgeted (`PERFORMANCE_BUDGETS.md` is item 26's): every boot prints the cost of a masked and of a fenced lookup, and of one IBPB where the processor has it, beside the context-switch cost. The register clearing adds about fourteen register-to-register instructions to each entry. Figures under QEMU's software processor are readings under an emulator and no more (§53) |

Boot proofs, each with a negative control recorded in `SOURCE_PROVENANCE.md`: a lookup that stays in bounds in both forms; the controls each processor set, read back; one barrier for each change of process and none without one; the image gate on the real kernel. `cargo xtask check`, `cargo xtask boot-test`, `cargo xtask boot-matrix` |
    | Actual deliverables | The code above; the gate; the proofs and their controls; the provenance entry; the threat model's rows; this item in the Done table |
    | Acceptance conditions | `cargo xtask check` and `cargo xtask boot-matrix` pass with no existing proof or bound changed. The records say plainly what could not be shown: QEMU's software processor offers none of these controls, so on the development machine every write of a control is unexercised, and stays so until a boot on a processor that offers it (CI's, through KVM, when the branch lands). A boot that fails is reported with its output, not retried until it passes |
    **What changed from that contract while building, 2026-11-10.** The contract above is kept as written. Five things were done differently, each for the reason given:
    - **`userspace.rs` and `syscall.rs` were edited**, and `usercopy.rs` and the kernel's `lib.rs` were not. The address clamp belongs after the range check, which is in `UserSpace::copy_in` and `copy_out`: two lines each. Z9's rule also covers a count a program supplies that sizes a kernel buffer, and the three such places are in `syscall.rs`: one line each. No other branch changes those lines (checked against `land/s3-part2`, `claude/s3-5b-*`, `claude/h2-stacks` and task 17's branch on the day).
    - **The image gate cannot hold the toolchain's prebuilt `core`, `alloc` and `compiler_builtins`**, which the stable compiler cannot rebuild. It holds what the build compiles and prints the libraries' count. This left part of Z10 unmet; the owner accepted the gap by name (ADR-0017 Amendment 1, decision item 152).
    - **The gate reads one more thing:** the trap entry's register clearing (Z2), which no boot proof can see from inside.
    - **Three proof rows, not one each for five things:** the controls (Report), the clamps (Refuse) and the IBPB count (Refuse). The image gate is a host gate, not a boot proof.
    - **No negative control can show the IBPB rule "none without a change" on the development machine,** because QEMU's processor has no IBPB and the count of barriers is then zero by rule. The host test of `Ledger` covers it; the boot control shows the scheduler's call (C31).

    **Result of 19b, 2026-11-10.** Built and committed on the branch, unpushed: Z1, Z2, Z3, Z5, Z9 and Z10 of ADR-0017, as the contract and the five changes above say. The evidence is in `SOURCE_PROVENANCE.md` ("Speculation and processor hardening, the first code").
    - **Passed:** `cargo xtask check` (1,189 host tests, the image gate, the unsafe gate at 334 sites, Loom); `cargo xtask boot-test`, 11 of 11; every part of `cargo xtask boot-matrix`, but in two runs: the first stopped when the service directory's proof refused one console scenario, the race in that proof's own script that `claude/directory-proof-race` fixes and this branch's base does not have. The console scenarios then passed by themselves, 9 of 9. Four negative controls were caught (ADR-0013 C30 to C32, and the image gate's).
    - **Not shown, and it is most of what matters:** QEMU's software processor has none of the controls, so every boot printed `on: nothing`, issued no IBPB, and ended `degraded: 58 of 59 proofs passed; failed: speculation controls`. **No control has been written on a processor that has it.** CI's boots under KVM were expected to be the first and were not: On 2026-11-11 the review batch's CI ran its real-CPU boots at `55db1f1` on an AMD EPYC 7763 (family 25) and an Intel Xeon Platinum 8573C (family 6, model 207), each as a guest under KVM with `-cpu max`. The guests offered none of the controls: every boot printed `on: nothing; ... not offered: enhanced or automatic IBRS, IBPB, branch isolation between sibling threads` and `this processor has no IBPB to measure`. Nothing can test what a processor does on a wrongly guessed path.
    - **Decided by the owner while it was built** (relayed by the coordinating session): the toolchain's prebuilt libraries are outside Z10, by name (item 152, ADR-0017 Amendment 1); direct calls between the kernel's crates are a task of their own (item 153, TASKS item 33).
    - **Decided by the owner once the boots were in (item 154):** every boot under QEMU's software processor ends "degraded", because the "speculation controls" row fails on a processor that offers none (item 131 as written). That stays. A row that passes when the kernel set everything the machine offers, and only prints what is lacking, was offered and not chosen.
    - **Left for 19c, which writes its own contract:** Z4's per-thread `SPEC_CTRL` value and its interface, Z6 (FRED), Z7 (LASS), Z8 (shadow stacks) and Z12 (the nested-NMI guard; row E12 stays Open). The pass after a resume from sleep, and the typed interface for these facts, wait for sleep and for the Diagnostics interface.
    - **For whoever merges:** the `mod` list in `developer-tools/xtask/src/main.rs` and the end of `OWNER_DECISIONS.md` conflict with the review batch, both "keep both". `Proof::ALL` grows by three here and by one on `claude/h2-stacks`: the counts in `bootproof.rs`'s tests are summed by hand. `STATUS.md`'s "Updated" row is not touched here. After the merge with task 17, run its `unsafe-gate` once: no block this task added declares `preserves_flags`.
20. **The clock experiment (item 114):** `prove_consistent` with `LFENCE; RDTSC`, on the CI runners that showed the step, from a trial branch. The result goes in the TSC ledger. If it supports one, a fast-path amendment to ADR-0012 is put to the owner.
21. **The four statements (item 120):**
    - `origin-version.toml`'s comment says what the loader does today;
    - the kernel manifest's `cet.shstk` is false until the kernel has shadow stacks;
    - `xtask` passes the canonical flags to `onbf-pack`, so `flags_digest` records them;
    - the AP trampoline's frame is not writable through the direct map while it is executable, and `smp.rs`'s comment says what holds.
22. **Build settings and CI (item 123):** `overflow-checks = true` for the kernel and the loader, with the cost measured; the loader refuses a processor below x86-64-v3, and then the kernel is built with `+popcnt,+lzcnt,+bmi1,+bmi2,+movbe`; CI runs `developer-tools/toolchain/verify.sh`, builds with `--locked` and checks the compiler's commit. **The CI half (`verify.sh` in CI, `--locked`, the compiler's commit, and §10's `wcslen` test inside the loader) is task 22a, built on `claude/blissful-hopper-8ab31a` on 2026-11-12, not landed and not yet run in CI: its contract and result are under "Task contracts". The other half (overflow checks, the loader's refusal, the kernel's target features) needs boots and a measured cost, and is not started.**
23. **What lives in the kernel (item 111):** the ADR (a user-mode service by default; a stated reason for kernel placement; the no-panic rule for every in-kernel path that reads outside data). Then the modules that are not the kernel's move out of `framekernel` to the crates §81 names. Twelve are named by nothing today: `base64`, `json`, `glob`, `editbuffer`, `semver`, `deflate`, `dns`, `route`, `hashmap`, `utf16`, `originfs`, `klog`.
24. **64-bit handles (item 109; ADR-0005 Amendment 6, E1), after S3-5b and S3-6 land:** the kernel's table, `origin-abi`, `origin.kernel.oidl`, `docs/spec/OIDL.md` §10, every generated crate and the hostile corpus, in one `user-abi` minor. **Built as task S4-0, TASKS item 38 (contract below).**

(2) Every trend names its environment, the revision and the run, and its raw values are in the tree. (3) The ledger's table is there with ADR-0018 V5's columns. (4) The file claims nothing, and says so. (5) `cargo xtask check` passes |

    **What it found.** One CI run, 62 boots: 20 on QEMU's software processor and 21 each as KVM guests on an EPYC 7763 and an EPYC 9V45. A context switch prints 260 ns on the Zen 5 guest. The random request prints 7.0 µs on the older guest, over ADR-0016 R12's "under 5 µs", on a processor outside the declared scope. The paths that wake a processor are far over their budgets under KVM, where a halted virtual processor is woken through the host. A console section kept interrupts off for as long as 5.3 ms in a guest, where each access to the UART leaves the guest. No boot printed what an IBPB costs. None of it is a figure for a declared machine.

    **Not done, and whose it is.** The ledger has no row: its first fill and ADR-0018 V8's gate are a task the coordinating session numbers (TASKS item 54, below). Nothing collects the figures at each run: the script that read the logs is not in the tree. No tail latency, and nothing on a physical machine. No person has reviewed the budgets.
27. **ADRs that §18 requires (item 118):** locking and synchronization (lock classes in a fixed order, checked by types or by a debug-build checker; rules for interrupt context); the rest of the cryptographic profile (storage encryption, password hashing, transport; the generator is task 18's); trade-offs and migration consequences for ADR-0002, 0003, 0004 and 0005.
28. **ADR-0002 Amendment 2 (item 119), with the SYS profile:** measured boot, the release manifest and the rollback floor, firmware runtime calls. Addresses leave release logs before the Diagnostics interface exists.
29. **The SDK-only mechanism (item 108; ADR-0011 Amendment 1), with the Loader Service's interface at M6:** the launch capability, the manifest gate, the directory, a base image with no interpreter, the run-time test.
30. **The review's smaller points (finding 19), which were not put as decisions:** the stale lines in `README.md`, `STATUS.md` and the ADR headings; SAFETY text for the naked functions and the `global_asm!` blocks; `place.rs`'s skipped segment; a zero check for a named reserved field in OIDL; a register of specifications in use for §17.

**Found while comparing builds for item 17 (2026-11-10), under CLAUDE.md §75 and rule 28:**

31. **The loader's build is reproducible bit for bit, and a gate keeps it so, now — done 2026-11-10** (see Done, and the result below the contract):
    - two release builds of the same loader source, in the same directory, give `origin-boot.efi` files that differ in 10 to 12 bytes: the link time in the PE header, the same time in the debug directory, and the linker's debug identifier. The disassembly is the same. The kernel ELF rebuilds byte for byte;
    - find the cause, and the smallest change that removes it, from what Rust 1.99.1's linker is seen to accept and to write (§9, §16);
    - a gate in `cargo xtask check` that builds the loader twice and fails if the two files differ, and does the same for the kernel ELF.

    **Contract (CLAUDE.md §78, decision item 122), written on 2026-11-10 before any code:**

    | Field | |
    |---|---|
    | Task ID | TASKS item 31, on branch `claude/clever-robinson-751c44`, cut from `docs/design-review-2026-11-10` at `2fbaea6` |
    | Objective | Two builds of the same loader source on one machine give the same `origin-boot.efi`, byte for byte, whenever they are built and whichever target directory they are built in. A gate in `cargo xtask check` keeps that true of the loader and of the kernel ELF |
    | Source ownership | This task alone adds `boot/uefi/build.rs`, which holds link arguments for the loader's binary as `framekernel/build.rs` holds the kernel's, and changes no line of the loader's source. In `developer-tools/xtask/src/` it adds one module for the gate, and in `main.rs` the gate's call in `check`, its own command, and a target-directory parameter on the two build functions. It adds a ledger under `docs/research/`, and edits this file and `SOURCE_PROVENANCE.md`. It changes no instruction of the loader or the kernel, no interface, no ABI, no boot proof and no ADR. Item 17's branch (`claude/funny-torvalds-9a28a0`) edits the same three places of `main.rs` (the module list, the commands, `check`'s list of gates) and the same two records, so the lines added here sit apart from the ones it added |
    | Architecture contract | CLAUDE.md §75 (rebuild and compare), §60 (track timestamps, build paths and other nondeterministic inputs), §14 (build reproducibility is reviewed) and §19 (the loader is a UEFI image, a different thing from the kernel image). Under Secure Boot the loader's Authenticode hash covers the header's time stamp (ADR-0002 B7), so a signed loader can be rebuilt and checked against its signature only if that stamp is reproducible. The image stays a valid UEFI application. This is not an ABI or format decision, so it needs no ADR (§84) |
    | Relevant specifications | Read on the day and recorded in the ledger (§16): Microsoft's PE format specification, which UEFI 2.11 §2.1.1 makes the format of the loader's image, for the header's time stamp, the debug directory and its reproducibility entry; the rustc book and the Cargo book as published for 1.99.1, for `-C link-arg`, `-C strip` and a build script's link arguments; and what the `rust-lld` of Rust 1.99.1 itself lists and writes, found by experiment and not from memory (§9) |
    | Required dependencies | None new. `xtask` stays `std` and first-party crates only. The gate runs `cargo`, as every other build step of `xtask` does |
    | Security invariants | (1) The image's code and data are what they were: only bytes of the header and the debug directory change, shown by comparing the sections before and after. (2) The loader still verifies: it boots the signed kernel and refuses the unsigned, wrong-key and tampered ones, with no boot proof, marker or bound changed. (3) The gate fails closed: a build that fails, an image that is missing or empty, and two images that differ in one byte or in length each fail it; and it cannot pass by comparing a file with itself, because the two builds are made from nothing in two directories |
    | Performance constraints | The loader's and the kernel's machine code do not change, so nothing moves at run time. The gate's own cost is measured and recorded, and is under a minute of `cargo xtask check` on the development machine |
    | Required tests | Unit tests of the comparison that plant a difference (one byte, a length, an empty file) and show it reported with its offset. The gate passing on the real tree. The gate failing on the real tree with the fix taken out, which is then put back. `cargo xtask check`. `cargo xtask boot-test`, because the loader's header changes and the firmware reads it |
    | Actual deliverables | The link arguments; the gate with its tests and its own command; the ledger, with the cause and what each candidate argument was seen to write; the provenance entry; this item in the Done table |
    | Acceptance conditions | Two builds of the same loader source are byte-identical. `cargo xtask check` passes, and fails when a difference is planted. `cargo xtask boot-test` passes with no proof weakened. What could not be verified is listed with the result |

    **Result (2026-11-10): done.** The evidence is in the Done table, in `SOURCE_PROVENANCE.md` and in the ledger `docs/research/2026-11-10-loader-reproducible-build.md`. Where it differs from the three bullets and the contract above:

    - **Two arguments, not one.** `/Brepro` alone, the usual answer, left two builds different in 20 bytes, and `/TIMESTAMP:0` alone in 8. For this target rustc passes `/DEBUG` whatever `-C strip` says, so the linker writes a PDB and a record in the image whose identifier is a hash of the PDB, and the PDB holds the path of a directory that rustc names at random for each link. `/DEBUG:NONE` takes the record and the PDB away. Nothing in the repository read the PDB.
    - **The arguments are in `.cargo/config.toml`, not in `boot/uefi/build.rs`.** A build script for the loader changes the metadata hash Cargo gives its crates, and the image then differs in 3,755 bytes with `.text` among them: the same code in another order. Invariant 1 could not have been shown byte for byte. In the configuration file `.text` is the same bytes as before, and the image differs in 74 bytes: the stamp, the header's entry for the debug directory, `.rdata`'s size, and the debug directory and the record that are gone. The cost is that `RUSTFLAGS` in the environment replaces that file's list; the gate's report says so when it sees the time stamp differ.
    - **The gate builds from nothing in two target directories**, not twice in one, and builds four images: the loader with no feature (what a release builds), with `test-trust-store` and with `developer-mode`, and the kernel ELF. Two seconds pass between the directories, because a PE time stamp counts seconds.
    - **A fourth control, on the kernel:** linked with a random build ID, the kernel fails the gate after the three loaders pass. The contract asked only for the loader's.
    - **UEFI 2.11 was read after the first result, and it changed the second argument.** `uefi.org` answered HTTP 403 to the fetch tool and to the browser, so the first form of the change (`/Brepro`, tested at `32f1a94`) was chosen from the PE format alone. The owner then supplied UEFI 2.11. Its §2.1.1 is the section the contract names, and says what the contract took from memory: UEFI images are a subset of PE32+. It says firmware uses the header's `Subsystem` and `Machine`, the sections, the relocations and the entry point. A search of its text finds nothing about the header's time stamp or a debug directory, and the PE/COFF revision it cites is version 8.1. `/Brepro` leaves a debug directory entry of a type defined in today's PE format; `/TIMESTAMP:0` leaves no debug directory. So the tree now has `/TIMESTAMP:0` (`42391cd`), and everything was run again. The copy of UEFI 2.11 came through a third-party file store and was not compared with the publisher's file.
    - **`docs/toolchain/VERIFICATION.md` was not edited:** it is generated by `developer-tools/toolchain/verify.sh`. The evidence is in the ledger and in the provenance entry.
    - **Not verified:** a Linux build host, where CI runs the gate for the first time; two different machines; any firmware but OVMF; version 8.1 of the PE/COFF specification.
32. **The images hold the path the toolchain is installed at (found by item 31; CLAUDE.md §75, §60) — done 2026-11-11, but for the comparison with CI; brought up to the batch on 2026-11-12, with the ring-3 build; step 2 done the same day, which found that the images still follow the kind of host that builds them** (see Done, the result below the contract, and the two contracts under "Task contracts"):
    - the loader holds one path and the kernel eight of the form `/Users/<account>/.rustup/toolchains/1.99.1-aarch64-apple-darwin/lib/rustlib/src/rust/library/…`, from panics in standard-library code compiled into Origin's crates. Another account, or CI's Linux runner, has a path of another length, so two machines cannot give the same bytes; and a released image would carry its builder's account name;
    - the cause is the `rust-src` component (ledger §9). Two fixes give the same loader, byte for byte: no `rust-src` in `rust-toolchain.toml`, which changes §8's list and is the owner's; or `--remap-path-prefix` from the toolchain's source directory to `/rustc/<commit>`, computed for every build of both images. Either changes the kernel's and the loader's bytes;
    - with it: `RUSTFLAGS` in the environment silently drops `force-frame-pointers` (§14) and the loader's two link arguments, for every build. `xtask` could refuse to build with it set;
    - then compare the digests `cargo xtask check` prints on the Mac and in CI. They are the first cross-machine comparison the project has.

    **Contract (CLAUDE.md §78, decision item 122), written on 2026-11-11 before any code.** The owner chose the fix on 2026-11-11: the remap, added by `xtask` to every image build, and not the removal of `rust-src`; with it, `xtask` refuses flags from the environment.

    | Field | |
    |---|---|
    | Task ID | TASKS item 32, on branch `claude/item-32-toolchain-path`, cut from `main` at `f654d71`, which holds item 31 |
    | Objective | No image holds a path of the machine that built it. The kernel ELF and the loader are the same bytes wherever the toolchain is installed and whichever account builds them, as far as one machine can show it. `xtask` does not build with compiler flags that the environment has replaced or added to. The reproducible-build gate fails if an image holds a path of the build host |
    | Source ownership | This task alone edits `developer-tools/xtask/src/`: how the three functions that build images and their libraries run `cargo`, a check of the environment when `xtask` starts, and the gate's new check with its tests. It adds a comment to `.cargo/config.toml`, a ledger under `docs/research/`, and edits this file and `SOURCE_PROVENANCE.md`. It changes no source line of the kernel or the loader, no interface, no ABI, no boot proof and no ADR. `rust-toolchain.toml` and CLAUDE.md §8 are not touched |
    | Architecture contract | CLAUDE.md §75 (rebuild independently and compare), §60 (build paths are tracked), §14 (frame pointers are enforced, so nothing may drop them silently) and §61 (a released image must not carry its builder's account name). Not an ABI or format decision, so no ADR (§84). In revision 7.5's words, still a draft, this is the work of the toolchain engineer and of the build, release and supply-chain architect |
    | Relevant specifications | Read on the day and recorded in the ledger (§16): the rustc book as published for 1.99.1, for `--remap-path-prefix`; the Cargo book as published for 1.99.1, for where compiler flags come from and in what order, for `--config`, and for the `trim-paths` profile option; and what the pinned toolchain itself does with each, found by experiment (§9) |
    | Required dependencies | None new. `xtask` stays `std` and first-party crates only |
    | Security invariants | (1) The remap changes the text of source locations and nothing else: each image is byte for byte what the compiler writes when it has no local copy of its own source, shown by building both ways. (2) Frame pointers, the kernel's two hardening options and the loader's two link arguments reach every image build: `xtask` stops before it builds anything if the environment holds compiler flags. (3) Fail closed: if the toolchain's directory or the compiler's commit cannot be read, `xtask` stops and does not build without the remap; an image that holds a build-host path fails the gate. (4) No boot proof, marker or bound changes |
    | Performance budgets, resource limits | Nothing moves at run time but the length of some strings; the change in each image's size is recorded. The gate's new check reads images it already holds in memory and adds under a second. `xtask` asks the compiler for its directory and commit once in a run. No new files are kept |
    | Failure behaviour | `xtask` names the variable it refuses, or the value it could not read, and exits before any build. The gate names the path it found, the image and the offset |
    | Required tests | Unit tests: the flag for a directory with spaces and quotes in its name; the compiler's commit read from its own report, and a report without one refused; each refused variable; a build-host path planted in an image and found, and an image without one passing. On the real tree: the gate passing; the gate failing with the remap taken out, which is then put back; `xtask` refusing with each variable set; the same images from a second copy of the toolchain's files at another path. `cargo xtask check`, `cargo xtask boot-test`, and `cargo xtask boot-matrix` because the kernel's bytes change |
    | Actual deliverables | The remap and the refusal in `xtask`; the gate's check with its tests; the ledger; the provenance entry with the new digests; this item in the Done table |
    | Acceptance conditions | Neither image holds a path under the toolchain's directory, Cargo's home or the checkout. The gate fails when the remap is taken out. `cargo xtask check`, `boot-test` and `boot-matrix` pass with no proof weakened. The digests the Mac prints are recorded for comparison with CI's. What could not be verified is listed with the result |

    **Result (2026-11-11): done, but for the last bullet.** The evidence is in the Done table, in `SOURCE_PROVENANCE.md` and in the ledger `docs/research/2026-11-11-toolchain-path-in-images.md`. Where it differs from the bullets and the contract above:

    - **The digests have not been compared with CI's.** That needs this branch to reach a pull request. What one machine can show was shown: the toolchain's whole directory copied to another path, and run from there, gives the same four digests; and with the remap taken out, the gate fails naming whichever toolchain was in use.
    - **`trim-paths` was tried first and is not stable.** Cargo's own profile option for this would have let the manifest say it, for plain `cargo build` too. Cargo 1.99.1 refuses it. So an image built by plain `cargo build` still holds the path, and `.cargo/config.toml` says to build with `xtask`.
    - **Four variables are refused, and one is let through.** `RUSTFLAGS` and `CARGO_ENCODED_RUSTFLAGS` replace the repository's flags even when they are empty. The per-target variable of each image target adds to them, which the item's third bullet did not know. `CARGO_BUILD_RUSTFLAGS` reaches no image.
    - **The gate looks for three paths, with a floor.** The toolchain's directory, Cargo's home and the checkout. It does not look for the bare home directory, or for a path under eight bytes, and says which it set aside.
    - **Every kernel boot ends `degraded: 58 of 59 proofs passed; failed: speculation controls`,** as on `main`: QEMU's software processor offers none of ADR-0017's controls. This task changed no proof.
    - **Not verified:** another machine; a Linux build host; a configuration file above the checkout or in Cargo's home that adds flags.
33. **Direct calls between the kernel's crates (decision item 153). Not started; not a condition of ADR-0011 N14 step 4.** (Items 31 and 32 are on another branch.) Found by task 19b's image gate. More than half the kernel's calls are indirect: in the image of 2026-11-10 (19b's tip), 3,720 of 7,028 calls and 211 jumps go through the global offset table (the coordinating session counted 3,718 of 6,992 and 208 at `7c1770f`). The compiler emits `R_X86_64_GOTPCREL` for a call into another crate, about 4,600 of them in the kernel crate's object, and the linker cannot turn that form into a direct call. Each is a load and an indirect branch where a direct call would do: a cost (P1), and indirect branches nothing asked for (ADR-0017 Z2). The task:
    - find a way on stable Rust 1.99.1 to make those calls direct. Link-time optimization is the candidate the owner was given; `-C relocation-model=pie` is a second. **Neither has been tried.** `rustc -C help` lists `relocation-model`, `code-model`, `lto`, `linker-plugin-lto` and `jump-tables`, and nothing for the PLT, for relaxable relocations or for default visibility;
    - measure before and after (rule 21): the image's size, the count of each kind of call, and the costs the boot prints;
    - check that the loader still has only `R_X86_64_RELATIVE` relocations to apply, and that `onbf-pack` still accepts the image;
    - then have the image gate (`developer-tools/xtask/src/image.rs`) hold the count of calls through the table, so it cannot grow back unnoticed.

    It writes its own contract (§78) before any change. The toolchain's prebuilt `core` and `alloc` keep their own calls, as they keep their returns (ADR-0017 Amendment 1).
34. **The APIC timer's proof on a starved emulator (found 2026-11-10 and 2026-11-11). Open: not built. Its design comes to the coordinating session before any of it is built, and to the owner if it changes ADR-0013's table.**
    - **What was seen.** Two boots under QEMU's TCG on the development Mac, with the host heavily loaded, were refused at the APIC timer's proof (`framekernel/frame/src/timer.rs`): "50 interrupts in 971801 us, expected 50 in 500000 us: APIC timer is wrong" (the console scenario, the host's one-minute load at 26.7) and "50 interrupts in 1314698 us" (the kernel boot, 2026-11-10, load not recorded). Both had calibrated right: 62,504 kHz against the PIT, which is QEMU's 1 GHz APIC clock divided by 16, and a 999 MHz TSC. The proof counts interrupts against the TSC and accepts 95 % to 150 % of the nominal 500 ms. QEMU raises one interrupt for each expiry it gets round to, so the periods a starved virtual processor misses are merged into one: the proof measured delivery, not the rate. `timer.rs` records the same effect at 1 kHz, which is why `HZ` is 100. This is a reading of the two logs and of how QEMU delivers the timer; nothing was loaded on purpose to repeat it.
    - **The logs** stay where they are, outside the repository, on the development Mac: `~/Library/Application Support/Claude/side-session-notes/local_ae64ddf9-9cfc-4e13-aa1e-73ab3cf83acd/2026-11-11-apic-timer-under-load/` (`load-26.7-console-com1.log`, `load-26.7-round.tsv`, `2026-11-10-kernel-boot-load-unrecorded.log`).
    - **The task: two checks where there is one.**
      - *The rate, from the timer's own count, on every machine.* A one-shot with a known initial count, its current count (`X2APIC_TIMER_CURRENT`, MSR 0x839) read against the TSC across a window. Each read sits between two TSC reads and is taken again when they are far apart, as the calibration discards a disturbed window. The count runs down whether or not an interrupt is delivered, so a starved processor does not move it; a wrong divisor, or an APIC rate that disagrees with the TSC's, does. Proposed bound: the calibration's own spread, which the design measures across the matrix, and never wider than ±5 %, the tolerance the fast side has today. Today the slow side allows +50 %.
      - *Delivery, on its own:* that the timer's interrupts arrive on their vector, period after period. On a processor that reports no hypervisor (CPUID.01H:ECX[31], which `speculation.rs` already reads), every period is delivered: 50 interrupts within the rate's bound of 500 ms. Under a hypervisor, merged periods are allowed but silence is not. Proposed bound: all 50 within the two seconds the proof already waits, four times the nominal time. The slowest kept log took 2.6 times, so the design narrows that bound if its starved runs show it can be.
    - **CI's real-processor boots run under a hypervisor too:** KVM (`ci.yml`'s `boot-real-cpu`, and `processor-sweep.yml`). So the hypervisor's rule applies there as on the Mac, and what is still checked there is the rate from the count against the TSC, at the same narrow bound as everywhere, and delivery at the hypervisor's bound. The design checks that those processors report the hypervisor bit. No machine without a hypervisor has run this proof (CLAUDE.md §74), so the bare-metal bound is untested until one does.
    - **A starved run without loading the machine** for other sessions: stop and continue the QEMU process (SIGSTOP, SIGCONT) at a known duty cycle while the proof runs. Such a run should show merged periods, a rate that holds, and where the delivery bound can sit.
    - **Negative controls:** a wrong divisor; an APIC rate off by more than the bound, in each direction; the timer's interrupt masked; one-shot mode in place of periodic. Each must fail under TCG and under KVM.
    - **ADR-0013.** Its row for the APIC timer says a correct kernel fails this proof only when no calibration source works (since F3). The kept logs show it failing with one that works, under a starved emulator. R1(a) already gives the remedy: a better proof, not a looser rule. A change to that row, or to what the proof refuses, is the owner's (R7: the table is the policy).
35. **The requirements matrix (CLAUDE.md 7.5 §1; decision items 165, 168 and 191 to 196). Built on 2026-11-12 on `claude/dazzling-jackson-9eca1b`, brought up to the second step-3 batch the same day, and not landed: see "Result" and "Brought up to the second step-3 batch" below.** §1 sets 100% coverage of approved requirements as the completion target and measures it against a versioned requirements matrix, which role 45 owns. No such matrix exists, so coverage cannot be stated today. The task:
    - one row for each approved requirement of CLAUDE.md, the accepted ADRs and the decision sheet, with its scope, owning role, implementation, acceptance criteria, verification evidence, state and remaining limitations;
    - kept where a gate can read it, beside item 25's provenance table and `docs/ROADMAP.md`, and repeating neither;
    - a gate in `cargo xtask check` that fails when a row marked done names evidence that is not in the repository, and prints the coverage figure;
    - approved scope changes recorded in the matrix with the decision item that made them.

    It writes its own contract (§78) first. The contract says how a requirement is split into rows and where the matrix lives; both are put to the owner before the matrix is filled.

A lead-in (a paragraph ending in a colon) is joined to each item of the list after it, or to the one paragraph after it; a paragraph that is only a bold label is joined to the paragraph after it. Code blocks and headings are not statements |

    **The shape, put to the owner on 2026-11-11 and accepted on 2026-11-12:** "Accept all 30", which is decision items 191 to 196 for these six and items 197 to 220 for step 4. The coordinating session added one rule to item 191 before it was put: every answered decision item is named by at least one row, or listed as asking for nothing, and the gate checks it. Each question had one recommendation, and each recommendation is what was accepted.

    1. **What one row is.** Recommended: one row for each statement of CLAUDE.md and one for each decision of an accepted ADR: 1,603 rows today. A row quotes its statement, so the gate can check that every statement has exactly one row and that no row outlives its statement. Decision-sheet items are not rows: most accept an ADR whose decisions are already rows, and some are downloads and merges. Each row names the item that approved or changed it instead. A statement that asks for nothing (a permission, a definition, a milestone's description) keeps its row, marked as information and not counted. Specifications, threat models and ADR-0013's list of boot proofs are cited by rows and not split into rows. One row for each section (125) was rejected: almost every section would read "partly done" for years, and the figure would say nothing.
    2. **Where it lives, and in what form.** Recommended: Markdown tables under `docs/requirements/`, one file for each PART of the directive and one for each ADR, with a `README.md` that holds the rules, the directive's revision and the scope changes. The tables are the only copy: GitHub shows them as they are, and the gate reads them with first-party code. Rejected: one file (about 1,600 long lines, too large to show or to read); a data file with a generated page (two copies, and the generated one conflicts at every merge).
    3. **What "done" means, and how "QEMU only" is carried.** Recommended: two states where today's records have one. `built` means implemented, with its evidence named, but short of the row's scope: proven under QEMU where the scope is declared hardware, for instance. `done` means the acceptance criteria pass within the declared scope. Each row says where its evidence ran, in §1's four words (host tests, emulation, hardware-assisted virtualization, physical devices), and the gate refuses `done` on less than the scope asks. The coverage figure counts `done` alone and prints `built` beside it. **What this means today:** no row of the kernel, the loader or a driver can be done, so the first figure is small and holds only host tools, records and gates. The other choice, counting QEMU-proven work as done with a note, gives a figure that could reach 100% with nothing run on a real machine.
    4. **What counts as evidence, and how "no person has reviewed this" is carried.** Recommended: evidence is a reference the gate can find in the tree, of four kinds: a file, a test by its file and name, a boot proof by its name in `framekernel/src/bootproof.rs`, and a `cargo xtask` command. A row that is `built` or `done` names at least one. The gate checks that the evidence exists; `cargo xtask check` and the boots are what check that it passes. Each row also says who reviewed it: nobody, an AI reviewer, or a named person. The gate prints how many rows a person has reviewed, which is none today.
    5. **Acceptance criteria for work that has not started.** Recommended: a row may stay `not-started` with no criteria, and cannot leave that state without them: the task's §78 contract writes them before the work begins, and the gate holds the rule. Most of the matrix is work that has not begun, and writing its criteria now would be guessing.
    6. **Scope changes.** Recommended: a table in the matrix's `README.md` with one line for each approved change: the decision item, the date, what was added, narrowed or removed, and the rows it touched. A row taken out of scope stays in the matrix, marked `removed` with its item, and is not counted. The gate checks that each item named is in the decision sheet. It starts with the changes already approved, the Server edition (items 71 to 76) and the stricter hardware scope (item 88) among them.

    Left out of the first version, and said so in its report: which requirement depends on which; a milestone for each row (the roadmap's exit tests are item 25's); the date each piece of evidence last passed; rows for the 24 decisions that are only on the draft batch at `15baed9` (ADR-0004 Amendment 2, ADR-0005 Amendment 7, ADR-0016 Amendments 1 and 2), which get their rows when the batch and this branch meet: until then the gate would fail there, by design.

Not pushed, and no pull request: the session that keeps the landing batch is told the commit.

    **Hardest to split into rows:**

    - **§1's roles.** Each is one statement with five to eight duties, and one row has one state. A role's row is done when every row it owns is done. Duties that no other statement carries have no row of their own: role 54's machine checks and ECC, role 42's data lifecycle, role 43's migrations, role 44's backups, role 47's firmware inventory, role 53's quotas.

    - **Decisions without titles.** ADR-0005's Amendments 2 to 5 are table rows with no title, so each is quoted by its first sentence. A decision is quoted by its title only, so a change to its body under the same title is not seen.

37. **Task 18b's follow-ups, from its second review and its landing (2026-11-11). Not started, except (c).** None blocked the landing. Each is a finding; none has a remedy decided.

    - **(a)** A processor's start still records a give-up in a later step (`collect`). That is true to the rule only because a start cannot move.
    - **(b)** A processor's own draws wait in its store when a gathering is interrupted. Someone who reads memory meanwhile (ADR-0016's T7) knows them, and against that reader the next reseed is worth only its newer draws.
    - **(c)** The randomized model of the generator's policy as a host test. **Built:** `security/random/src/tests/model.rs` and nine negative controls in `security/random/tools/plant_faults.py`, on `claude/18b-random-model` at `dabe70b`, merged into the second step-3 batch at `fe457f2` (decision item 190). No person has read it.
    - **(d)** From the landing (`c2245ef`): run the memory-object proof on a machine with no generator and show `not_supported` for a map at a place the kernel chooses. Today that proof does not run there, and only a host test of `mapping::place_at_random` shows the status. *An observation from the interface session (2026-11-11), not a remedy:* on such a machine no process can be launched, since a start record's place is drawn too (threat row P3), so the proof cannot be a ring-3 program there. A remedy would have to call the map path from the kernel, with the kernel choosing the place, in a process that is never started.

Done when `cargo xtask check` passes; every recorded digest changes from its format-1 value; the description's handle width comes from the same constant the generator emits handles with; and a planted width of 32 changes every minor's digest (a negative control). `origin.kernel` 0.7's fingerprint still equals 0.6's after it: every minor is recorded again from today's definition, which states one width for all of them. Only a width the definition versions by minor could tell them apart, a grammar change and a question of its own for the owner, not asked. Both had an AMD EPYC 7763, the slowest processor these runners have; pull request 21's had an EPYC 9V45, the fastest (`PERFORMANCE_TRENDS_2026-11-13_ci-boot-step.md`). 49. **ADR-0017 says nothing of ring-3 code: a trap after a return in `origin.core`'s stubs and in compiled ring-3 code (found by task S4-2 and by the independent review of its build half, 2026-11-13). Open: not decided and not built. It comes to the owner with ADR-0017's next amendment, before a ring-3 program that takes hostile input runs.** Z10's table of options is headed "Kernel and framework", and its rule for hand-written assembly names the framework's own returns. The userspace flags (`.cargo/userspace.toml`) carry no `harden-sls-ret`. So no accepted text asks for an INT3 after a return in ring 3, and none says ring 3 goes without one. In `origin.core` as the batch builds it after S4-2's landing (Rust 1.100.0), its 20 stubs are aligned to 4 bytes and one ends in `ud2`: of the 19 `ret`s, 12 are followed at once by the next stub's first instruction, 6 by the linker's `int3`, and the last by `int3` to the end of the page, which the kernel writes at install (finding F11). Read from the built bytes on 2026-11-13; the review counted the same at S4-2's first review point. By reading, a stub loads nothing from memory: what could run past a `ret` is the next stub's extensions, the load of its number and its `syscall`. Nothing was measured, and nothing has run on a processor that speculates. The choices to put: (a) nothing in ring 3, accepted by name in the system threat model; (b) an INT3 after each stub's `ret`, one byte a stub and never executed, held by `cargo xtask core-gate`; (c) the kernel's target feature for every first-party ring-3 image, with a gate that reads each image, and the toolchain's prebuilt libraries outside it as ADR-0017 Amendment 1 has them for the kernel. Step 4 runs first-party programs only (decision item 213), so this holds no step-4 task up.

51. **A frame that is executable in any address space cannot be written through the kernel's direct map (found by the independent review of task S4-2's build half, finding F7, 2026-11-13). Open: not designed and not built; a task of its own, after S4-2 lands.** The framework refuses a writable mapping of a frame that is executable anywhere. It does not refuse the kernel's own write: `SharedFrame` is `Clone`, and `write_u64` writes through the direct map whatever mappings the frame has. Today nothing writes such a frame after it is filled: the launch path fills a program's code before its first thread exists, and `core_image::shared` keeps `origin.core`'s frames to itself. So the rule holds by each module's privacy and by nothing in the framework, for every program's code frames and not only `origin.core`'s. Two shapes for the design to weigh: a frame that is sealed after its first fill and offers no write (no cost at run time), or a refusal inside the write (a check on every write, whose cost ADR-0018 asks to be stated first). **Two paths, not one (the independent review of S4-2's kernel half, finding K1, 2026-11-13).** Besides `SharedFrame::write_u64`, `UserSpace::write_frame` writes through the direct map into any frame a space maps, whatever the mapping's access, and `Process::frame_of` hands kernel code the frame of any mapped page. The rule has to cover both. It also has to settle the launch path, which today maps a program's code page read-execute and then fills it: either private frames stay writable by the kernel until the first thread, or the launch path fills a page before it maps it, which would let the rule hold for every executable frame with no exception. Until this task is built, task S4-2's `Process::write_page`, the kernel's one way into a process's pages, refuses any frame of `origin.core`, and the names gate in `cargo xtask check` lets `UserSpace::write_frame` be named only in `framekernel/src/process.rs`, for `write_page`, and in `framekernel/src/aspace.rs`, whose proofs hold a bare space and no process. That closes the path for the shared image and not for a program's own code. The design comes to the coordinating session before any of it is built, and to the owner if it changes an accepted ADR.

55. **Task S4-3 of ADR-0011 N14 step 4: the kernel-wide bound, and O2 for step 4 (decision items 206, 209 and 210; ADR-0005 Amendment 8, G2, G5 and G6). Assigned by the coordinating session on 2026-11-13, on `claude/elegant-benz-1983ff` at `793299a` (`land/s4-part2`, which holds S4-2). Built on 2026-11-13, with its gates passed under QEMU on `f5f1829` (Rust 1.100.0); not landed and not pushed; an independent review is to come. Its contract, the rulings that changed it and its result are under "Task contracts".** A set of limits is worth a figure in bytes, the most kernel memory and frames they let a process make the kernel hold; making a process pays it out of a root the kernel sizes at boot less a reserve, and the account gives it back when everything it covers is gone. A process the root cannot pay for is refused with `limit`, with nothing made. The figure's thread part needs a `threads` count in each process's limits, held inside the kernel. Row O2 gains its citation of `origin.kernel` at `user-abi` 0.7, held by a test over the definition.

57. **ADR-0005 Amendment 10, I1: a port's creator pays for its index (decision item 282, accepted on 2026-11-13). Assigned by the coordinating session on 2026-11-13, on `claude/elegant-benz-1983ff` from `land/s4-part2` at `1b8688a` (which holds S4-3) with `docs/decision-282` merged in. Its contract is under "Task contracts"; nothing is built yet.** A process's limits gain `bindings_per_port`; a port is made bounded by its creator's value, a bind past it is `limit`, and the port's index at that bound is in its creator's figure. It closes row K7 of the system-call threat model, and comes before S4-4.

40. **Identity maps for firmware-reserved memory: VT-d RMRRs and AMD-Vi IVMDs (ADR-0007 questions 4 and 8; decision items 8, 12 and 229; TASKS item 11's IVMD semantics and placement). Started 2026-11-12 on `claude/rmrr-ivmd-maps`, from `land/s4` at `5871257`; not merged.** The owner's deadline: they land before real hardware, and rental B's tool waits for them. Today a device a reserved range names is refused at attach (I11, A9), which fails closed but leaves USB controllers and integrated graphics without a driver on real machines (threat T14). Research ledger `docs/research/2026-11-12-reserved-memory-identity-maps.md`; five shapes the ADR leaves open (R1–R5) were put to the coordinating session on 2026-11-12 with recommendations; it numbered them decision items 247 to 251 and put them to the owner, who accepted all five as recommended on 2026-11-13 (ADR-0007 Amendment 1, matrix rows A07-R1 to A07-R5). On 2026-11-13 the branch merged `main` (`803755c`, Rust 1.100.0, then `6368f02`) and those records, and `check`, `boot-test`, `boot-matrix` and `boot-reserved` passed again under Rust 1.100.0 (provenance entry). An independent AI review the same day found two Medium and seven Low points (`docs/engineering/REVIEW_T40_2026-11-13.md`); all are answered there, with decision items 291 to 293 built (a never-map type-20h IVMD for every device; the similarity check in place of §3's split; boot info 0.7). No person has reviewed the code. Its contract is under "Task contracts".

61. **`boot-matrix` runs its scenarios in one process, each scenario's QEMU settings passed as typed values rather than through the environment (ADR-0011 Amendment 4, T1's notes; decision items 300, 310 and 311). Open: not started; after TASKS items 58 and 59 land, since both add settings to the same code.** Today `boot-matrix` and its nine scenario commands (`boot-x2apic`, `boot-bridge`, `boot-reserved`, `boot-legacy-free`, `boot-random`, `boot-identity`, and TASKS item 58's `boot-sparse`, `boot-many` and `boot-listed`) re-run `xtask` through `current_exe()` with `ORIGIN_QEMU_*` variables set, which items 300, 310 and 311 allow until this task. Done means: no such re-run is left, `host-tools-gate`'s `SELF_RUNS` (TASKS item 60) keeps only `exclusive`'s command and the tests that start a binary to test it, and every scenario prints the markers it prints today.

Proposed by the coordinating session on 2026-11-14 as a session of its own; not started.** A session that writes no Origin code reads today's ACPICA release through GitHub's web pages, under its BSD licence option, for those gaps alone, and writes the facts, in Origin's words, into a ledger of its own, `docs/research/2026-11-14-aml-gaps-acpica.md`, with each file, its release and the date read. TASKS item 63 reads that ledger and never ACPICA (CLAUDE.md §3). G10, what deployed firmware expects of `_OS`, then comes to the owner as its own item.

Open: not started; after TASKS items 58 and 60 land, which both change the checker.** The checker stops QEMU at a panic's first line (`panicked at file:line:col:`), so no `boot-test` log holds the message on the lines after it; TASKS item 58 read its control AC-9's panic message only through `cargo xtask run`. Done means: after a panic's first line the checker reads on, for a bounded time or until the kernel's halt, and the log keeps the message; a planted panic shows it (CLAUDE.md §1, "Protect diagnostic data"; §69).

### 18a. The kernel's random-number generator: the research, the ADR and the host-tested generator

Written 2026-11-10, before any of it was built. It is the first half of item 18 above (decision item 113; finding 8 of `docs/engineering/DESIGN_REVIEW_2026-11-10.md`). The second half, 18b, wires the generator into the kernel and the loader, and writes its own contract.

| Field | |

| Task ID | 18a |
| Objective | Three things, and no more. (1) A research ledger: the revisions and dates of NIST SP 800-90A and SP 800-90B as NIST's own pages give them on the day, what each requires of an HMAC_DRBG and of health tests, and what was read and what was not. (2) `docs/adr/ADR-0016-kernel-random-numbers.md`, **Proposed**: seeding and reseeding, state for each processor, what happens with no healthy seed, zeroization, where the code lives, how the loader passes a firmware seed, a threat model, and numbered questions for the owner. (3) HMAC-SHA-512 and HMAC_DRBG in `origin-crypto`, tested on the host. **Not in this task:** any change to the kernel, the framework, the loader, the boot-info structure, `bootproof.rs` or ADR-0013's inventory; the health tests' code; any consumer |
