New files, owned by this task: `docs/research/2026-11-10-kernel-random-numbers.md`; `docs/adr/ADR-0016-kernel-random-numbers.md`; in `security/crypto/`: `src/hmac.rs`, `src/hmac_drbg.rs`, their test modules, the reference generator under `tools/` and its vector files under `tests/vectors/`. Shared files it may touch, and how far: `security/crypto/src/lib.rs` (the module list, the re-exports and the crate's description of itself); `security/crypto/src/sha512.rs` and `src/block.rs` (crate-private additions only: no public item and no existing behaviour changes); `security/crypto/Cargo.toml` (the description); this file (this contract and item 18's rows); `docs/engineering/SOURCE_PROVENANCE.md` (one entry); `docs/STATUS.md` (one paragraph). It touches nothing under `framekernel/`, `boot/`, `runtime/`, `sdk/`, `services/` or `developer-tools/`, and neither `CLAUDE.md` nor `OWNER_DECISIONS.md`: the questions go to the owner in the ADR, and the session that relays the answers gives them their item numbers |
| Architecture contract | CLAUDE.md §59 as revision 7.4 has it (one generator, an established deterministic one, seeded from more than one source and reseeded, health tests on the raw source); decision item 113; ADR-0013 Amendment 1 (the entropy proof becomes Withdraw with the generator); ADR-0004 H3 and its note of 2026-11-10 (the firmware's generator becomes one seed); ADR-0002 B4 (boot info is the one interface between loader and kernel, and a change to it is the owner's, §84); ADR-0011 N6 (the kernel's `unsafe` lives in the framework only); `origin-crypto`'s own contract (`no_std`, no allocation, `forbid(unsafe_code)`, the no-panic lints and the no-panic object check, ONBF §12); ADR-0001 D3 (the OS holds no signing code: unchanged) |
| Relevant specifications | NIST SP 800-90A Rev. 1 (June 2015), the generator. NIST SP 800-90B (January 2018, with NIST's errata note of 2025-05-29), the health tests. NIST SP 800-90C (September 2025), for how a generator and its sources are put together: §17 does not list it, and the ADR says whether it should. FIPS 198-1 and RFC 2104, HMAC. RFC 4231, HMAC-SHA-512 test cases. FIPS 180-4, SHA-512 (already implemented). UEFI 2.11's random-number protocol, for the firmware seed. Intel's and AMD's documents on RDSEED and RDRAND. The ledger pins each revision with the date it was checked and says which text was read (§16, decision item 96) |
| Required dependencies | `origin-crypto`'s SHA-512 and `core`. No new crate, in the shipping code or for the tests (§5). Python 3's standard library for the host-side reference that writes the differential vectors; it is a development tool and never ships. NIST's DRBG vector files and the standards' PDFs are downloads: each needs the owner's word first, with its name, source and size. The task is built so that it stands without them and says what it then lacks |
| Security invariants | 1. The generator is SP 800-90A's HMAC_DRBG and HMAC is FIPS 198-1's, unchanged: no step added, removed or reordered (§4, §59). 2. No output before a successful instantiation; a missing, short or failed seed is an error and never a default. 3. Past the reseed interval a request returns "reseed required" and no bytes. 4. A request or an input over the standard's limits is refused whole; nothing is truncated silently. 5. On any error the caller's buffer holds no generator output. 6. No `unsafe`, no allocation, no panic path. 7. No branch and no memory index depends on a secret (the key, V, an entropy input, an output); lengths and counters are public. 8. The state cannot be copied or cloned, and `Debug` prints none of it (§61). 9. Every named buffer that held secret material is overwritten before it goes out of use, and the code says what that overwrite does and does not guarantee without `unsafe` (§59) |
| Performance constraints | No allocation and a fixed, stated state size. The cost of one HMAC and of one request is measured on the host and recorded as indicative only (the target is x86-64; rule 21). The kernel's budget for a draw belongs to 18b, where it can be measured; the ADR gives the cost model it will be held to |
| Required tests | HMAC-SHA-512: RFC 4231's test cases, from the RFC's text; streaming against one-shot at every split around the block boundaries; keys shorter than, equal to and longer than a block; differential vectors against Python's `hmac`. HMAC_DRBG: known answers from a published text where one exists; a differential corpus against an independent reference covering instantiate, reseed, generate, additional input, personalization and prediction resistance; the state machine (counter, interval, limits, every refusal, the cleared buffer, uninstantiation); planted faults, one at a time, each of which must fail a test; the no-panic object check and clippy on the host and both freestanding targets, through `cargo xtask check`. NIST's own DRBG vectors are added when the owner allows the download |
| Actual deliverables | The ledger; the Proposed ADR with its questions; the code and its tests, vectors and reference tool; one entry in `SOURCE_PROVENANCE.md`; this file and `STATUS.md` brought up to date; small commits that each build |
| Acceptance conditions | `cargo xtask check` passes on the final tree. The ADR is Proposed, not Accepted, and nothing it proposes for the kernel, the loader or boot info is built. No file was downloaded without the owner's word. The final report gives the questions for the owner, the commands run with their results, and what is not verified |

Nothing it decides for the kernel, the loader or boot info is built: no file under `framekernel/`, `boot/`, `runtime/`, `sdk/`, `services/` or `developer-tools/` changed |

The fetch tool saved two copies of one PDF unasked; they were never opened and were deleted. The ledger's §11 gives the order of events |

One thing the contract did not foresee: SP 800-90C, which CLAUDE.md §17 does not list, describes the design the ADR proposes, a root generator with a child for each processor, and the ADR was corrected against it.

### 18b. The kernel's random-number generator in the kernel and the loader

Written 2026-11-11, before any of it was built. It is the second half of item 18: it builds what ADR-0016 decides and 18a left unbuilt. The owner's word to start it was "Start 18b" (2026-11-11, relayed by the coordinating session). The branch is cut from `main` at `f654d71`, which holds ADR-0016, 18a's mechanism, CLAUDE.md 7.4 and the gates of tasks 17, 19b and 31.

| Field | |
|---|---|
| Task ID | 18b |
| Objective | Seven things. (1) The policy crate `security/random` (`origin-random`): the health tests, the retries, how a seed is put together, when a generator reseeds and what backs off, and the standard's known-answer self-test, all tested on the host. (2) The framework's service, `framekernel/frame/src/random.rs`: the two instructions, the root and a generator for each processor, the overwrites of R9, and R11's three functions. (3) Boot info 0.6 and the loader's own generator: two slides from two requests, and 64 bytes handed to the kernel. (4) The consumers on `main` moved to the service: `launch.rs` and `virtionet.rs`; `entropy.rs` removed. (5) The proof row moved to Withdraw, with `bootproof.rs`, its host test, ADR-0013's inventory and the boot test's markers in one commit. (6) R2's gate on the two mnemonics. (7) The cost of a request and the longest time interrupts stayed off, measured and printed at every boot. **Not in this task:** see "What 18b leaves" below |
| Source ownership | Branch `claude/18b-kernel-random`, one session. **New, owned by this task:** `security/random/`; `framekernel/frame/src/random.rs`; `boot/uefi/src/random.rs`; `developer-tools/xtask/src/mnemonics.rs`. **Removed:** `framekernel/src/entropy.rs`. **Shared files it changes, and how far:** `Cargo.toml` and `Cargo.lock` (one member); `origin-version.toml` (boot info 0.6); `boot/info/src/lib.rs` (one field, its checks and tests); `boot/uefi/src/main.rs` (the two slides, the seed, the overwrite; `entropy()` goes) and `boot/uefi/Cargo.toml` (one dependency); `framekernel/frame/Cargo.toml`, `src/lib.rs` (one module), `src/cpu.rs` (`rdrand` and `rdseed` move out), `src/early.rs` (where the loader's 64 bytes are, checked), `src/smp.rs` (each processor's generator at its start); `framekernel/src/kernel.rs` (the boot wiring; `prove_entropy` replaced), `lib.rs`, `launch.rs`, `virtionet.rs`, `bootproof.rs`, and the two proofs that launch a process (`ipc_proof.rs`, `ipc_proof/directory.rs`: "did not run" with no generator); `developer-tools/xtask/src/main.rs` (the crate lists, the markers, two boots), `unsafety.rs` (two leaves, one listed file); `security/crypto/tools/convert_kat_vectors.py` (it also writes the self-test's constants); `.github/workflows/ci.yml` only if the new boots need a line; `docs/adr/ADR-0013-boot-proof-policy.md` (the row, the controls), `ADR-0016` (its status and what is built), `ADR-0002` and `ADR-0004` (their "not built" notes); this file, `STATUS.md`, `SOURCE_PROVENANCE.md` (the crate's row and entry). **It does not change** `security/crypto/src/`, `CLAUDE.md`, or any file that is only on another branch |
| Architecture contract | ADR-0016 R1 to R13 as accepted (items 146 to 151), and S1 to S7 below, which settle what the review note found open and change none of them. ADR-0013 R1 to R6 and Amendment 1. ADR-0002 B4 and ADR-0004 H3 as ADR-0016 amended them. ADR-0011 N6 and N13: the kernel's `unsafe` only in the framework's listed files; dependencies one way. CLAUDE.md §13 (decision items 110, 111), §14 (item 123), §59 (item 113), ADR-0017 Z10 (no jump tables, an INT3 after every return), task 31's reproducible build. ADR-0008 (processor indexes). `origin-crypto`'s contract (ONBF §12): `no_std`, no allocation, no `unsafe`, no panic path; the policy crate takes the same |
| Relevant specifications | NIST SP 800-90A Rev. 1 §9, §10.1.2 and §11.3; SP 800-90B §4.3 and §4.4; SP 800-90C §7 and Appendix A.3.4; Intel's DRNG guide and AMD-SB-7055. All as the ledger `docs/research/2026-11-10-kernel-random-numbers.md` records them, read on 2026-11-10 at the revisions it pins. No new reading is planned. If a question comes up that the ledger does not answer, the source is read on the day and the ledger gains a dated section first (§16, item 96) |
| Required dependencies | `origin-crypto` and `core`; `alloc` in the framework only. No third-party crate (§5). Python 3's standard library for the tool that writes the self-test's constants from NIST's committed vectors; it never ships. No download: nothing beyond the six files of item 145 |
| Security invariants | 1. Every random value in the kernel and the loader comes from the generator; the mnemonics `rdrand` and `rdseed` are in two files, and a gate holds them there (R1, R2). 2. No output without an instantiated generator: every function returns a `Result`, none returns zero for "none", and no consumer has a fall-back (R8). 3. No draw is used before its instruction has passed a start-up test on that processor, and every later draw passes the continuous tests first; a failure stops that instruction on that processor (R4). 4. No draw goes into two seeds (R5). 5. A generator's state is only in the framework's static storage; uninstantiation overwrites it with volatile writes (R9 Z1). 6. Every step that handles a draw, a seed or the state runs with interrupts off on a stack of the generator's own, and that whole stack is overwritten and the scratch registers cleared before interrupts come back on (R9 Z3; S5). 7. The loader's 64 bytes are checked to lie inside the boot-info region, taken once, and overwritten before another processor starts; neither side prints them (R10). 8. No draw, seed, state or output is printed or put in an error: reports are counts and yes-or-no (R13, §61). 9. No safe caller can break memory safety through the service, from any processor, in any order, an interrupt handler included; a request that arrives in the middle of another on the same processor is refused, not served (ADR-0011 N6). 10. `origin-random` holds no `unsafe` and no panic path; the framework's new `unsafe` is in one listed file, each block with its argument. 11. No assembly block that holds `rdrand` or `rdseed` declares `preserves_flags` |
| Performance constraints | A request touches no hardware unless a reseed is due. **The generator never keeps interrupts off for longer than one step** (S1): one try of one instruction, one piece of a request of at most 128 bytes, or one reseed. Provisional budgets, hypotheses for `PERFORMANCE_BUDGETS.md` when it exists (decision item 115), on a declared target: a request of up to 64 bytes under 5 µs (R12), and no step after a processor's start keeping interrupts off for more than 10 µs. Both are measured at every boot and printed; under QEMU's software processor the figures are trends and claim nothing (§53). A request does a bounded amount of gathering: at most 16 draws (S2). No lock is shared between processors on a request; the root's lock is held for one computation and never across a draw (S4). Static storage for each processor index: R7's 576 bytes for the state, and beside it the tests' values and the store (under 512 bytes more), the whole aligned so that no two processors share a cache line. One 8 KiB stack with a guard page for each processor that is online |
| Required tests | **Host, `origin-random`:** each health test against hand-made sequences (a repeat; the window's first draw again; a stuck bit in each position; zero and all ones; "not ready" for 99 and for 100 tries); the cutoffs against the ledger's worked figures; the start-up test's count, and that a failure discards what was gathered; seed assembly for a root and a child byte for byte against R5's table, through an independent reference; the reseed schedule (2^20 requests, 60 s), the fall-back and the back-off of S3 with a scripted source and clock; a request that moves between processors between its steps; the 16-draw bound; the self-test's constants equal to NIST's vectors in `tests/vectors/acvp`, and a planted wrong constant failing it; planted faults, one at a time, each failing a test. **Host, the framework and the kernel library:** `bootproof`'s pinned table; boot info 0.6's layout and checks. **`cargo xtask check`:** clippy on the host and both freestanding targets, the no-panic object check with `origin-random` in it, the `unsafe` gate, the assembly gate, the new mnemonic gate with its own tests, the image gate, the reproducible build, Loom. **Boots under QEMU:** `cargo xtask boot-test` and `boot-matrix` with the new lines; a boot with RDRAND only and one with neither instruction, the second ending degraded with no program run; controls in exported copies, each built and booted once and recorded in ADR-0013 from C37: a stuck source, a stuck bit, a failing known-answer test, the stack overwrite removed, the loader's seed left in place |
| Actual deliverables | The crate, the service, the loader's generator, boot info 0.6, the consumers, the proof row, the gate, the boots; ADR-0013's row and controls; ADR-0016, ADR-0002 and ADR-0004 saying what is now built; `STATUS.md`; `SOURCE_PROVENANCE.md` with a row for `origin-random` and an entry; this file. Small commits that each build and pass their tests |
| Acceptance conditions | `cargo xtask check` passes on the final tree. `cargo xtask boot-test` passes on it, and the two new boots end as stated. Each control fails as recorded. Nothing is pushed, merged or opened as a pull request. The final report gives the commands run with their results, what QEMU showed and what it cannot show, and what is not built |

**What the contract settles (the review note in item 18).** None of S1 to S7 changes R4, R6 or R7 as accepted: each fills something those rules leave open, and each says which words it rests on. If building shows that one cannot be kept without changing them, the work stops there and the change goes to the owner as an amendment first (§84).

- **S1. Where draws run, and how long interrupts stay off.** The generator's work is done in steps, each with interrupts off, and interrupts are restored between steps to what the caller had. There are three kinds of step: one try of one instruction, with the continuous tests on its value and the value put into the processor's own store; one piece of a request, at most 128 bytes (R7); one reseed or instantiation from a full store. Counting tries, pausing between them and deciding the next step happen between steps and touch nothing secret. So the longest time the generator keeps interrupts off is one step, whatever the retry budget is. R6's "before a request" and R7's "a request runs with interrupts off" both hold: the reseed's steps come before the request's first piece.
- **S2. How much one request may do.** At most 8 draws for a reseed that is due, and at most 8 more towards a start-up test that is being run again: 16 draws, each with R4's budget of tries. A start-up test that has to be run again (R4 item 8) starts when the processor's generator next falls due for a reseed and goes on over the following requests, 8 draws at a time, until 1,024 consecutive draws have passed or one fails. R4 says the test "is tried" then; it does not say one request carries all of it.
- **S3. A dry RDSEED: what backs off, and when RDRAND takes over.** One draw whose 100 tries were all "not ready" is R4's "not now". When a reseed that is due meets it, RDSEED on that processor is left alone for one second, and the reseed is made at once from 8 RDRAND draws where RDRAND is healthy; the RDSEED draws already gathered go in as additional input, so no draw is used twice or thrown away. It counts as a reseed, and a counter the boot report shows says how many were made that way. With no healthy RDRAND, or RDRAND "not now" too, the reseed stays due and nothing draws again until the second has passed. So a program that keeps RDSEED dry (T5) costs one draw's tries a second on each processor, not on each request. A request for prediction resistance is refused with "not now" where its 8 RDSEED draws cannot be had (R6). These are T5's words, "delayed" and "fall back to RDRAND", given numbers.
- **S4. The root's lock.** The processor that needs the root gathers its 8 draws into its own store first, as in S1. It then takes the root's lock for one step: the reseed and the generation. No instruction is tried while the lock is held, and a processor that finds the lock taken waits with its interrupts restored.
- **S5. The stack overwrite covers the draws.** Every step of S1 runs on a stack that belongs to the generator, one for each processor, 8 KiB with a guard page below it. Before interrupts come back on, the whole of that stack is overwritten and the scratch registers are cleared. A draw therefore never rests on a thread's stack, and the overwrite does not depend on an estimate of depth: this is R9 Z3 with "a fixed depth" made the whole stack. The store a draw goes into and the tests' few remembered values (the last draw, a window's first draw, two masks) are in the processor's static storage with its state.
- **S6. A caller that arrives with interrupts off** (an interrupt handler, R7) gets R6's reseed before its request like any other, with one try for each draw in place of R4's "up to" 100 and 10, so that it never waits in a pause. That caller's own time with interrupts off then grows by at most 16 tries and one reseed.
- **S7. What a non-maskable interrupt can leave.** An NMI or a machine check that arrives in the middle of a step saves the interrupted registers on its own stack, which is not overwritten. Neither handler asks for random values (R7). This is named, not closed: it is a limit of R9 that T11 does not state, and it goes into ADR-0016's "cannot show" list with this task.

**What 18b leaves, and to whom.**

- **The loader's call to the firmware's random-number protocol** (R10 step 1). This session read EDK II's declaration of that protocol on 2026-11-10 (ledger §12), and decision item 92 has the code written by a session that did not. The loader's generator is built here with the place for those 64 bytes and the one-line report; a separate session adds the call from the ledger's §8. Until then the loader reports that it did not ask.
- **Consumers that are not on `main`:** the draws in `framekernel/src/memory_object.rs` and `framekernel/frame/src/stack.rs` on `land/s3-part2`. *(Corrected 2026-11-11: the contract first named `framekernel/src/stacks.rs` as a third. It does not draw; it reads what the framework drew from, for its proof. Item 18's state says what converting the two takes.)* Whichever of the two branches lands second converts what the other added; `cpu::rdrand`, `cpu::rdseed` and `entropy::hardware_u64` no longer exist after this task, so that code does not compile until it calls `origin_frame::random`. *(2026-11-11: both are converted in the batch's merge of 18b, `c2245ef`.)*
- **A processor going offline** (R7's last point): the kernel has no such path today, so nothing uninstantiates a running processor's generator.
- **Reseeding after a resume or a restored snapshot** (R6, T10), **an interface for programs** (R11), and **`PERFORMANCE_BUDGETS.md`** itself: each is another task's.
- **Anything on hardware.** No machine exists to run this on. QEMU's software processor shows that the code paths run and that the tests catch planted failures. It cannot show what RDSEED and RDRAND cost or how often they are not ready, how sixty-four processors asking at once behave, or that any budget above is met.

**What building changed or found.**

- **A real fault, found by planting faults.** Collecting a start-up test's draws, and gathering for a request with prediction resistance, counted an instruction's failed tries across draws. Two draws that each waited sixty tries were taken for one that had waited a hundred, and a healthy instruction was left out. Fixed, with two tests.
- **A due request always moves its processor forward.** With a thread that moved to another processor after every step, one processor's reseed could wait for ever. The step that finds a processor due now does the first piece of its maintenance itself.
- **A failed proof withholds randomness.** ADR-0016 R8 speaks of a machine with no generator. The row also fails when a processor has no generator, a value repeats, the generator's stack is not all zeros or the loader's seed is still there; `random::withhold()` then refuses every later request, so the row withholds what it says (ADR-0013 R2).
- **"Runs no program" is "launches no process".** With no generator the kernel's fixed ring-3 proof programs, which ask for no random value, still run. Nothing is launched through the launch path.
- **The loader runs its generator as the kernel does:** interrupts off for a step, a stack of its own, registers cleared. The contract had the loader overwrite the firmware's stack below its frame; that needs a fact about the firmware's stack that no document read here gives.
- **Where the contract and the work differ.** The self-test's constants are written by `security/random/tools/gen_selftest.py`, not by `convert_kat_vectors.py`, which is unchanged. The "did not run" condition is in `sched.rs`, not in the two proof files. `framekernel/frame/src/boot.rs` and `framekernel/src/report.rs` gained the new field in their test headers. `.github/workflows/ci.yml` is unchanged: `boot-matrix` runs the two new boots.

**Not verified, and not built.** Nothing has run on a real processor: the costs, how often the instructions are not ready, many processors asking at once, and both provisional budgets. Under QEMU's software processor a 64-byte request took about 22 µs and the longest step after a processor's start about 0.2 ms; those are an emulator's figures. An NMI or a machine check in the middle of a step leaves registers on its own stack (S7). No processor goes offline, so no generator is uninstantiated while the kernel runs. The firmware's protocol is not asked (18c). No human has reviewed any of it.

Boot info stays 0.6. On this branch:

- the loader's third request, both parts handed over or neither;
- `origin-boot-info`: the secret area is 72 bytes (`BOOT_SECRET_LEN`), and `boot_seed()` checks all of it;
- `origin_frame::random::take_stack_value`: the value once, with no instruction and no generator, and nothing after the generator's start has overwritten the area;
- the kernel overwrites and reads back all 72 bytes, and a boot proof takes the value once, is refused a second time, and is refused again after the start;
- `finish_start` takes its lock outright, where it tried it and went on silently if it failed (a review point from the coordinating session; nothing contended for the lock).

A request that moves begins again where it arrives; one that ends unanswered overwrites what it gathered. A processor's own reseed does not take such draws over. The root's gathering never takes or overwrites a processor's own draws, and makes at most eight | `schedule::the_root_is_reseeded_on_the_processors_schedule`; `schedule::a_processor_that_could_not_reseed_the_root_looks_again`; `schedule::a_processor_that_starts_when_the_root_is_due_reseeds_it_first`; `schedule::the_roots_gathering_leaves_a_processors_own_draws_alone` |

**What the fixes change in the contract's words.** S2's bound stands: a request makes at most 16 draws. What the 16 can be has one more case: eight for the processor's reseed and eight for the root's. When a start-up test that is being run again has used eight, the root's eight wait for the next request. S4's "a processor that finds the lock taken waits" still holds for a processor that needs the root for itself; a processor that only looks at the root for the root's sake never waits. Eight rounds of patience at a start is a new figure, a bound like the others in `drive.rs`; whether it is enough is a question for hardware.

**For the owner** (nothing here is built on an answer):

1. **T5's row in ADR-0016.** It says a program that keeps RDSEED dry makes reseeds "delayed" and has them "fall back to RDRAND". Where RDRAND is not healthy on a processor there is nothing to fall back to, and that processor's reseed waits for as long as RDSEED gives nothing. **Recommended:** amend T5's row to say so, and build nothing now. The alternative is to send such a processor to the root once its reseed has been delayed for some periods, which changes R6. On a machine whose processors are alike that is a reseed in name only: the root's own reseed needs the same instruction. It would help only where another processor has a healthy RDRAND and this one does not, and nothing read says such a machine exists. **Answered 2026-11-11, in the session that built the generator: "Yes, amend T5"** (decision item 188). ADR-0016 Amendment 2 and T5's row carry it; nothing is built.
2. **R6's sentence on the root**, "by whichever processor next uses it and has a healthy instruction". Built as: a processor that has just reseeded itself looks at the root. The review gave this as one of two ways to carry R6 out; the other was to reword R6 to what was built before, which would have left the root on its first seed. **Recommended:** nothing to decide; if the wording should match the code, one clause in R6 when the ADR is next amended.

**Evidence for the fixes.** 72 host tests of `origin-random` (55 before). `plant_faults.py` holds 107 faults (69 before): **105 fail a test; the other two are listed as not observable** (a local variable's bytes; the root being told of a change twice). The first full run after the fixes left three more uncaught, which led to three of the tests named above. The Python reference follows the root's reseed and still agrees byte for byte. Control C43 in ADR-0013. `cargo xtask check` passed on `fb88326` (2026-11-11, 12:54 to 12:59, a clean tree when it began; the code is `53ee743`, and the commits after it change Markdown only): every crate's clippy and tests, the no-panic check with `origin-random` in it (9 external symbols, all allowed), the image gate (1,270 returns, an INT3 after each), the `unsafe`, assembly and random-instruction gates, the loader built three ways and the kernel twice with the same bytes, and Loom. `cargo xtask boot-matrix` passed on the same code (13:00 to 13:29): `boot-test`'s 11 scenarios under Intel VT-d and AMD-Vi with 1, 2 and 4 processors, the sparse, bridge and legacy-free machines, both `boot-random` machines and the 9 console scenarios. The planted faults were run on an exported copy of `fb88326` beside the check. Every boot is QEMU's software processor.

**Still not verified.** Nothing on hardware: how often RDSEED gives nothing, which decides whether a start's patience and the proof's three requests are generous or mean, is unmeasured. The review read the policy crate; the coordinating session is still to read the kernel's wiring and the gate. No human has reviewed any of it.

**Added to 18b on 2026-11-11: a second independent review, of the fixes above, and its seven findings.** The coordinating session had the fixes read at `a6ab315` by a session that wrote none of them. It confirmed the eight, each by planting the old behaviour and seeing tests fail, and its randomized machine found no draw older than its request and no draw in two seeds over 79,000 pieces with prediction resistance. The coordinating session's own read of the kernel's wiring, the instruction gate and item 166's hand-over found no defect. The review reported seven more things. **None lets output be predicted or repeated.** Three of them need a thread that changes processor between two steps of one request, which today's scheduler never does. Two sentences of the table above were wrong in variants the review reproduced: "a request that has given an instruction up does not try it again on any processor" and "a stuck value seen on one processor is not held against another's". They are true now, by other means.

**The choice for a thread that moves (findings 2, 4 and 5): the move is handled.** A request is not held to one processor, so nothing in R6 or R7 changes and nothing goes to the owner. Three rules do it:

- **A draw's tries are counted by the processor that makes them,** in that processor's state. What follows the last try, a rest or a failure, happens in that same step. The driver no longer counts tries and no longer records a give-up in a later step.
- **A request's upkeep belongs to the processor it began on.** Every later step of it does nothing on another processor, and the upkeep ends at the first that finds the thread elsewhere. The answer still comes from whichever processor the thread is on (R7).
- **Draws gathered for the root are lent under a number** that names the request and the processor. Only the holder is given them, and every gathering begins by overwriting the store.

| # | The finding | Judged | What is built now | Fails without it |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | The root's reseed can slip a period: a processor looked once, after its own reseed, and a root seeded a few steps later than the processor was "not due" at that look | A defect. Fixed | A processor notes when the root was last seeded, at its start and at every look, and looks again when the root's own period has passed since then. That is the only thing that makes it look: the flag that its own reseed set is gone | `schedule::the_root_does_not_wait_for_the_processors_next_reseed`, with a clock that moves at every step |
| 2 | A give-up was recorded on whichever processor the thread was on by then: processor 0's RDSEED gave zero a hundred times, and processor 1's was failed | A defect, latent. Fixed by the first rule | The step that makes a draw's last try ends the draw, on the processor that made it | `requests::what_follows_a_draws_last_try_falls_on_the_processor_that_made_it` |
| 3 | The root's gathering could not fall back to RDRAND: its cap of eight counted RDSEED draws that were later set aside | A defect. Fixed | The gathering is bounded by the draws the request has left of its sixteen. A look that follows the processor's own reseed has eight; a look a second later has all sixteen, which covers seven RDSEED draws and eight of RDRAND | `schedule::the_roots_reseed_falls_back_to_rdrand_when_rdseed_goes_dry` |
| 4 | Lent draws stayed on a processor the thread had left, and a later gathering went on from them | A defect, latent. Fixed by the third rule | A gathering begins by overwriting. Draws left behind stay until that store is next used and are never gone on from. "Draws that do not reach the root are overwritten" holds at once where the thread still is, and at the store's next use where it has moved | `schedule::draws_left_on_a_processor_the_thread_moved_away_from_are_never_gone_on_from`; `schedule::a_lending_number_names_its_processor` |
| 5 | Tries across moves: a new budget on the next processor, a count that began again when the instruction changed, and 3,264 steps under the root's lock telling the root of a change | A defect, latent. Fixed by the first two rules | Each processor counts its own instruction's tries; a request that has moved makes no further try; the root is told only for the processor the step runs on | `requests::a_request_that_has_moved_makes_no_further_try`; `requests::the_root_is_told_once_however_the_thread_moves` |
| 6 | Requests with prediction resistance overwrite each other when threads on one processor are switched between every few steps: 2,785 of 3,356 refused in the review's model | **Recorded, not changed.** No answer is wrong by it. A request is refused "not now" after 3,264 steps at the latest, and each beginning costs at most eight draws. It needs a switch inside the ten or so steps of a gathering, and nothing asks for prediction resistance today. The cure, a newcomer that waits for a gathering under way, is for the typed interface's design | ADR-0016's "cannot show" list names it, with the bound | — |
| 7 | Six of the review's sixteen mutations survived | Right. Each now fails a test, or its code is gone | The processor checks around a request with prediction resistance are gone: the number names the processor. Two tellings of the root that changed nothing are gone. The test for a period having passed lost a term that changed nothing. A processor with no healthy instruction not looking at the root has a test | `schedule::a_processor_with_no_healthy_instruction_does_not_look_at_the_root`; `schedule::a_lending_number_names_its_processor`; the faults in `plant_faults.py` |

**What this changes in the contract's words.** S2 stands: sixteen draws a request. S3's "one draw's tries a second on each processor" is now exact, because the count lives on the processor: a request that leaves in the middle of a draw leaves the count, and the next request there goes on from it. A request that has moved is answered by the processor it is on without that processor's upkeep, even if that processor is due: its reseed is made at its own next request. That was so before these fixes, and it is the "delayed" of T5's row.

**Two notes from the coordinating session's read, which block nothing.** The instruction gate reads literal assembly templates only; a check of the linked images would cover the rest. `fill_fresh` says "not now" both for a dry RDSEED and for a processor with no healthy RDSEED. Both are in ADR-0016's "cannot show" list now; neither is built.

The first run on this round's fixes caught 112 and showed that one test had been deleted by mistake while its neighbours were rewritten; it is back as it was. The Python reference agrees byte for byte, unchanged. Nothing in the kernel or the loader changed in this round. `cargo xtask check` passed on `d739b93` (2026-11-11, 15:20 to 15:26; the commit after it changes Markdown only): every crate's clippy and tests, the no-panic check with `origin-random` in it (9 external symbols, all allowed), the image gate (1,259 returns, an INT3 after each), the `unsafe`, assembly and random-instruction gates, the loader built three ways and the kernel twice with the same bytes, and Loom. `cargo xtask boot-matrix` passed on the same code (15:26 to 15:53): `boot-test`'s 11 scenarios under Intel VT-d and AMD-Vi with 1, 2 and 4 processors, the sparse, bridge and legacy-free machines, both `boot-random` machines and the 9 console scenarios. The planted faults were run on an exported copy of `d739b93` beside the check. Every boot is QEMU's software processor.

**Still not verified.** As above: nothing on hardware, and no human has read any of it. The second round's fixes have been read by nobody but the session that wrote them.

**Added to 18b on 2026-11-11, from another session: the randomized model is in the repository (item 37 (c)).** The machine the second review ran, with the checks the coordinating session added to it, is `security/random/src/tests/model.rs`, on `claude/18b-random-model`, cut from `a11202a`. The branch changes tests and `plant_faults.py`: nothing that ships. The model makes requests nest, threads move and take their steps in turns, and instructions go dry, fail or run out, all from fixed seeds, and it holds every seed the policy makes against the rules at the head of that file. **No violation on `a11202a`.** `cargo test --package origin-random` runs 100 tests in 3.8 s (79 in 2.7 s before), 21 of them the model's; 17 more are marked `#[ignore]` and run the same configurations at length in 56 s. The 13 runs of 2026-11-11 are among them, and the seven on one thread of control give that day's figures exactly. `plant_faults.py` holds 120 faults (116 before): **118 fail a test, 1 does not compile and 1 is not caught**, the one listed as not observable. A test of the model fails for 40 of them, its nine negative controls among them, and for one no other test does. Of the 116 at `a11202a`, 114 failed a test and one, a reseed of seven draws, only stopped the crate compiling: the "115" above counted both. `cargo xtask check` passed on `f9df059` (2026-11-11, 18:18 to 18:25; the commit after it changes Markdown only). What the model cannot show is in `SOURCE_PROVENANCE.md`: it runs the policy crate on a scripted platform, not the kernel's wiring, and nothing on hardware. No human has read it. Item 37's own text is for the session that lands 18b.

It ran under QEMU on the development Mac only.

| Field | |
|---|---|
| Task ID | S3-6, of ADR-0011 N14 step 3 (`docs/research/2026-11-09-n14-step3-scope.md`, §3 row S3-6). S3-6a is its part inside the framework. |
| Objective | `event_create`, `object_signal`, `port_create`, `object_wait_async`, `port_wait`, `port_cancel` and `clock_read`; signal delivery that never allocates; Loom models for bind, raise, cancel and close. `origin.kernel` and `user-abi` go from 0.5 to 0.6 in one step. |
| Source ownership | The interface session: `sdk/interfaces/origin.kernel.oidl` with its generated code and fingerprint lock; `origin-abi`; `framekernel/src/syscall.rs`; new kernel files for events and ports; `ipc.rs` and `process.rs` as far as the task needs; the proofs and their bookkeeping; xtask's boot markers; `THREAT_MODEL_SYSCALLS.md`; ADR-0013's rows; STATUS, TASKS and provenance. The framework session: everything under `framekernel/frame/`, with the bind, raise, cancel and close protocol in `origin-sync` (S3-6a). The coordinating session: ADR-0005, the decision sheet, and every merge into the batch. |
| Architecture contract | ADR-0005 C6, C9, C10, C11, C13, A2, D1, D4, D5, E1 and Amendment 7 (F1 to F19). |
| Relevant specifications | The ledger's sources: the Intel SDM and the AMD64 manual for what ring 3 reads of time; other systems' public documentation only, at the day's version. No source code of any other system was read. |
| Required dependencies | The second step-3 batch (`land/s3-part2`); the framework's wait queues; ADR-0012's clock floor; ADR-0017 Z9's `origin_frame::nospec`. |
| Security invariants | (1) No allocation on any delivery path; storage is reserved and charged at bind. (2) One binding yields at most one packet, only on its port, and none after `port_cancel` has returned for that object and key. (3) No lost delivery: a signal raised before, during or after the bind queues the packet exactly once. (4) The lock order is extended and written down; no lock is held across a wake-up; closing never recurses. (5) The wait queue's 16 waiters do not bound bindings; the binder's quota does. (6) Rights are checked on every handle and pointers before anything is consumed. (7) Every lookup by a value ring 3 chose goes through `origin_frame::nospec` and is listed in the threat model. (8) Lifetimes, **as corrected by item 179**: when a port's last handle closes, every binding and queued packet goes and every charge is refunded; a binder that exits leaves no armed binding, and no charge once the port is read, cancelled or closed; an object that dies with bindings on it leaves none; the IPC leak check stays exact. (9) `#![forbid(unsafe_code)]` on every new kernel file. (10) No kernel address in any packet or status. (11) `clock_read` reads `time::monotonic_nanos` and never steps back. (12) From item 172: the threat model gets a row for processes that share a port's `write`. |
| Performance constraints | QEMU figures, no budget: event signal to `port_wait` round trip; bind plus delivery; `clock_read`; the channel ping-pong figure before and after. |
| Required tests | Host tests of the mechanism, one or more per rule of C9. Nine Loom models in S3-6a, each with a committed control. A table of hostile calls for each new call. Two boot results run from ring 3, each with negative controls and a row in ADR-0013's table: one for events and ports, which fails the boot when it fails, and one for the clock as ring 3 reads it, which is reported. `clock_read`'s hostile calls include a bad out pointer (`fault`; **item 178 corrects the contract's "no errors"**). |
| Actual deliverables | The interface at 0.6, the kernel's side of the seven calls, the tests above, the records. Small commits, each of which builds and passes its tests. Nothing pushed by the task's sessions. |
| Acceptance conditions | All of the above; `cargo xtask check` and `cargo xtask boot-test` pass on the merge, run by the coordinating session after it has read the piece; the interface and image gates pass; costs reported; what is not shown is said plainly. Nothing here runs on physical hardware. |

The contract's full text as sent, with its starting list of undecided points, is in the coordinating session's transcript and is not kept in the repository; the table above is its content with the two corrections made.

### S4-0. 64-bit handles (TASKS item 38)

Sent by the coordinating session to the interface session on 2026-11-12, after the second step-3 batch reached `main` (`04ec842`). Written here on 2026-11-12, before any code. It is task S4-0 of ADR-0011 N14 step 4 (`docs/research/2026-11-11-n14-step4-scope.md`, section 4, whose exit tests were written before this), and it builds TASKS item 24.

| Field | |
|---|---|
| Task ID | S4-0, of ADR-0011 N14 step 4; TASKS item 38. It builds item 24, ADR-0005 Amendment 6's E1 (decision item 109). |
| Objective | A handle is a 64-bit value everywhere a program meets one: as a system-call argument, as a result, inside `received_handle` (16 bytes) and `start_entry` (24 bytes), and in the handle lists `channel_write` and `channel_create` take and fill. The kernel's table gives a process no practical limit on the handles it creates in its life. `origin.kernel` and `user-abi` go from 0.6 to 0.7 in one step. |
| Source ownership | The interface session: `framekernel/src/handles.rs`; `runtime/abi` (`origin-abi`); `sdk/interfaces/origin.kernel.oidl` and both locks; `docs/spec/OIDL.md` §10; the generator (`developer-tools/oidl`) as far as a handle's width and its register check go; every generated crate (`framekernel/syscalls`, `sdk/directory`); `origin-version.toml`'s `user-abi`; the kernel files that hold a handle's value (`syscall.rs`, `ipc.rs`, `launch.rs` and the proofs under `ipc_proof`); `services/directory`; the threat model's row H2; ADR-0013's evidence; STATUS, TASKS, provenance and the requirements rows E1 changes. The framework session: `framekernel/frame/src/proof_programs.rs`, the interpreter's handle load and a 64-bit copy step, the only assembly the task changes. The coordinating session: ADR-0005, the decision sheet, and the merge into the third batch. |
| Architecture contract | ADR-0005 P1, P2, P4, B3, C4 and Amendment 6, E1 (which governs P1, B3 and C4 where they differ); ADR-0011 N9 (a handle is opaque) and Amendment 2, L5; `docs/spec/OIDL.md` §3.1, §5, §9, §10, §13 and §14. How the kernel divides a handle's bits between a slot and its generation is the kernel's, not the ABI's (E1). |
| Relevant specifications | None outside the repository: the change is Origin's own ABI. |
| Required dependencies | `main` at `04ec842`, which holds S3-5b and S3-6 (E1: "after S3-5b and S3-6, which change the same calls"). No new answer from the owner (the ledger's section 4). |
| Security invariants | (1) A handle is never 0. (2) A stale handle never names a reused slot (P1, row H2): a slot whose generations are used up is retired. (3) Each process's handles name only its own table's entries (row H1); a value from another process, or a live value with its generation changed, is `bad_handle`. (4) No path keeps 32 bits of a handle: a live handle's low 32 bits alone are `bad_handle`. The kernel places the slot above bit 32, so any truncation left in the kernel, the generated code or the proofs names no slot and fails loudly. (5) Every padding byte of `start_entry` is written as zero and refused when not zero (OIDL §5.2). (6) Every lookup by a value ring 3 chose still goes through `origin_frame::nospec` (ADR-0017 Z9). (7) No `unsafe` outside `framekernel/frame/`; `origin-abi` and every generated crate keep `#![forbid(unsafe_code)]` where they have it. |
| Performance constraints | None set. A handle lookup does the same work on wider fields; the IPC ping-pong figure the boot prints is reported before and after, as a QEMU figure, with no budget (§53, item 115). |
| Required tests | The ledger's exit tests for S4-0. Host: `received_handle` is 16 bytes and `start_entry` 24, field by field against the generated layout; the decoder no longer checks the upper half of a handle's register (OIDL §10, revised), in the generated code and in the corpus's independent model; one slot driven past 2^16 generations is not retired, and a stale handle still never names a reused slot; a slot is retired when its generations are used up; the fingerprints re-recorded, citing Amendment 6. Boot: every existing proof passes, with the interpreter loading 64-bit handles; the hostile calls include a live handle's low 32 bits alone and a handle with its generation changed, each `bad_handle`. Negative controls, each a temporary edit booted once and put back byte for byte: the kernel's handle argument cut to 32 bits; a lookup that ignores the generation. Each must fail the boot. |
| Actual deliverables | The interface at 0.7 and both locks re-recorded; `origin-abi` and the generated crates at 64 bits; the kernel's table; the proofs at 64 bits with the new hostile steps; OIDL §10 revised; the records. Small commits, each of which builds and passes its tests. Nothing pushed. |
| Acceptance conditions | On the task's branch: `cargo xtask check` (the requirements gate included), `cargo xtask boot-test` and `cargo xtask boot-matrix` pass; the exit tests above pass; both negative controls fail the boot; provenance, STATUS, TASKS and the matrix rows S4-0 changes are current. Then the coordinating session reads the piece, runs the gates on the merge and lands it in the third batch. Nothing here runs on physical hardware. |

### 22a. The toolchain checks in CI, and §10's `wcslen` test inside the loader (the first half of TASKS item 22)

Assigned by the coordinating session on 2026-11-12. Written here on 2026-11-12, before any of it was built. It builds the CI half of TASKS item 22 (decision item 123; the fourth point of finding 18 in `docs/engineering/DESIGN_REVIEW_2026-11-10.md`), which is what the roadmap's tests M0-1, M0-2 and M0-3 ask for (decision item 252).

Three things were tried first, in a scratch copy of `0550226` that has no git and is not kept, to choose between designs. Each is a required test below and is run again on the task's own commits:

- A length loop over a NUL-terminated `CHAR16` string, added to the loader's library and called by nothing: the library's object holds a call to `wcslen`, and the loader's image is the same bytes as before (SHA-256 `d6b62ba2…` both times).
- The same function calling a symbol that nothing defines: the loader's link fails with "undefined symbol", naming the function. So the link needs every symbol such a function names, though the linker then drops the function, and the loop does not have to run for the test to be a link test.
- `Cargo.lock` with one dependency line taken out: `cargo --locked xtask cfg-lint` stops with "cannot update the lock file", and `cargo xtask cfg-lint` rewrites the file and passes, which is what CI does today.

| Field | |
|---|---|
| Task ID | TASKS item 22, first half (22a), on branch `claude/blissful-hopper-8ab31a`, cut from `land/s4` at `0550226`. The worktree was made at `main`'s `418aeee`; the branch was moved to `land/s4` before its first commit, as the assignment asks. |
| Objective | (1) CI runs `developer-tools/toolchain/verify.sh` on every pull request and on `main`, in a job of its own, with its report written outside the checkout and onto the run's summary page. (2) Every cargo command CI gives carries `--locked`, so a lock file out of step with the manifests fails a job before anything is built. (3) Every CI job fails unless the compiler reports the pinned commit, which is written once, in a file both `verify.sh` and CI's install script read. (4) §10's `wcslen` test is inside the loader: the loop is in the loader's library, and `verify.sh` builds the loader as a release would (no feature, `--release`, the workspace's flags, `--locked`) and fails unless that library's object calls `wcslen`, the image links, and it is a PE32+ EFI application. `verify.sh` runs all three of §10's tests. (5) `docs/toolchain/VERIFICATION.md` is recorded again, from a tree with nothing uncommitted. **Not in this task:** `overflow-checks`, the loader's refusal below x86-64-v3 and the kernel's target features (the second half: they need boots and a measured cost); `--locked` on the cargo commands `xtask` starts itself (see "Security invariants", 5); a check of cargo's own commit; any change to what the loader does when it runs; marking M0 or any milestone done. |
| Source ownership | This task alone edits: `developer-tools/toolchain/verify.sh`; `developer-tools/toolchain/rustc-commit-hash` (new, one line); `tests/toolchain/uefi_smoke.rs` (its comment only); `boot/uefi/src/uefi.rs` (one function beside `copy_char16_string`, and its unit tests); `docs/toolchain/VERIFICATION.md`. Shared, and kept narrow: `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (the flag on the existing cargo lines, one new job, one last step in the `check` job) and `.github/ci/install-rust.sh` (one check). **No file under `developer-tools/xtask` changes.** Records: this contract and item 22's line in this file; one entry appended to `SOURCE_PROVENANCE.md`, and a cell of the loader's row in its table only if this makes one stale; the matrix rows named under "Acceptance conditions", and the README's list of done rows if one becomes done; in `docs/ROADMAP.md` only the three cells for M0-1 to M0-3 under "Marked against the tests"; `docs/STATUS.md` only where it says what CI runs. The keeper of `land/s4` merges and pushes. The coordinating session: the decision sheet and every number. |
| Architecture contract | CLAUDE.md §8 ("Use an exact compiler version in CI", "Verify target installation rather than assuming it succeeded"), §9, §10, §60 (dependency inventory, build provenance), §75 (toolchain hashes recorded), §80 (no spending: GitHub's standard runners only, decision item 32), §7 (no padding). Decision items 123 and 252. ADR-0018 V1 and V3 for the cost (accepted as items 238 to 240; the ADR is on `docs/adr-0018` and not in this tree). ADR-0002 for the loader: a firmware string stays behind the bounded copy. Nothing here is an ABI or format decision, so no ADR is needed (§84). |
| Relevant specifications | None outside the repository. The three regressions are as `docs/research/2026-11-07-onbf.md` records them (R2, T5; rust-lang/rust issues 163614, 163911 and 163947), and were not read again today. Three behaviours of the pinned toolchain are relied on, each observed on 2026-11-12 with rustc 1.99.1 (`58cf8f9c3`, LLVM 23.1.1) and held by a test here, not taken from documentation: LLVM turns the loop into a call to `wcslen` for `x86_64-unknown-uefi`; `rust-lld` refuses an undefined symbol named by a function nothing calls; cargo accepts `--locked` in front of an alias. |
| Required dependencies | `land/s4` at `0550226`, which holds the requirements matrix, the provenance gate and `docs/ROADMAP.md`. No new crate, no new tool (`llvm-nm` is in the pinned `llvm-tools`), no new action in the workflow. No answer from the owner. The proof of M0-3 waits for the keeper's push: this task cannot push, so it cannot run CI. |
| Security invariants | (1) **No image changes.** The loader's three builds (no feature, `test-trust-store`, `developer-mode`) and the kernel are the same bytes with and without this task's diff, compared between two copies of the tree without git that differ only by it. (2) **No unbounded scan of a firmware string.** Nothing calls the new loop, the linker drops it (1 shows that for the image), and its documentation sends firmware strings to `copy_char16_string`. (3) **The workflow gains nothing:** `permissions: contents: read`, no secret, and the same pinned actions as before. (4) **The checks fail closed.** A commit file that is missing or is not 40 hexadecimal digits fails both scripts. `verify.sh` fails, and does not merely note, when the loader's library holds no call to `wcslen`: a regression test that has stopped testing is a failure. An argument `verify.sh` does not know is a usage error. (5) **The lock file CI builds with is the committed one.** The first cargo command of each job resolves the whole workspace under `--locked`; the cargo commands `xtask` starts afterwards resolve the same workspace against the same file and so have nothing to change; and the `check` job's last step fails if the job changed any tracked file. (6) **`verify.sh` writes only its report.** In check mode it installs nothing, and with its report sent outside the tree it leaves `git status` empty. |
| Performance constraints | In ADR-0018 V3's forms. **Every image: none, by construction.** Nothing the task adds to the loader is called, the linker removes it, and invariant 1 checks that by digest. **The build host:** `verify.sh` gains one release build of the loader from nothing, in its own temporary directory; its time on the development Mac is measured and reported, with no budget. **CI: unmeasured, owed.** One more job on a free standard runner, and the flag and one `git diff` on the existing ones; the model is the toolchain's install plus one build of the loader, a few minutes, and the figure is read from pull request 21's run. |
| Required tests | **Host:** unit tests of the loop (an empty string, one unit, several, and that it stops at the first NUL); `verify.sh` on a clean tree with no FAIL; `cargo xtask check` on the last commit, in a turn (`cargo xtask exclusive`). **Negative controls,** each planted in a copy of the commit under test that has no git and is thrown away, so the tree is never edited for a control: (a) another hash in the commit file: `verify.sh` fails, and CI's install script stops at its commit check; (b) one dependency line out of `Cargo.lock`: `cargo --locked xtask cfg-lint` fails and leaves the file as planted, and without the flag cargo rewrites it and passes; (c) the loop given a bound: `verify.sh` fails, with no call to `wcslen` in the library; (d) the loop replaced by a call to a symbol nothing defines: the loader's link fails, and `verify.sh` with it; (e) `ffi_bool.rs` returning the byte unmasked: fails; (f) the path of `rust-lld` pointed at nothing: fails. **Also:** the install script run on the Mac with stand-ins for `lscpu` and `nproc`, to show it passes with the right hash; the workflow file parsed as YAML; the image digests of invariant 1. |
| Actual deliverables | The function and its tests; `verify.sh` with the commit check, the loader test and a `--report` option; the commit file; the workflow and the install script; `VERIFICATION.md` recorded again; the records and the matrix rows. Small commits. `cargo xtask check` is run on the last one, and what was run on each earlier one is said in the result. Nothing pushed. |
| Acceptance conditions | `verify.sh` exits 0 on a tree with nothing uncommitted, and that run is the committed record. `cargo xtask check` passes on the last commit. Controls (a) to (f) each fail as said, with their output in this task's provenance entry. Invariant 1's digests are equal. **Matrix rows.** S10-04 takes a criterion of its own: "The loop is in the loader's library, and `verify.sh` fails unless that library's object, built for the loader in release, calls `wcslen` and the loader's image links"; it can be shown on the build host, which is its scope. S8-07 and S8-10 take: "Every CI job installs the compiler `rust-toolchain.toml` names, fails unless it reports the pinned commit, and passes `--locked` on each cargo command it gives." S8-05, S8-09, S9-01 to S9-10, S10-01 to S10-03, S84-02, S84-03 and S84-05 keep their criteria, which end "and CI runs it" or "in every build environment". **Every row here except S10-04 stays `built` until a CI run exists,** and its limitation says so: the run on pull request 21 after the keeper pushes is the proof, and of M0-3. **What this does not show:** any CI run; `verify.sh` on Linux (it was last run there on 2026-11-07, under another compiler); §10's `rust-lld` test for macOS in CI, whose runners are Linux; that the compiler is the one Rust's project built, since the commit is what the compiler says of itself and rustup's check of the download is the only other; and no person has reviewed any of it. The coordinating session checks the result. |

**Result (2026-11-12): built on the branch; not landed, and not shown in CI.** The evidence is in `SOURCE_PROVENANCE.md`, under "TASKS item 22a". What the contract asked for and what was seen:

| Asked | Seen |
|---|---|
| `verify.sh` exits 0 on a tree with nothing uncommitted, and that run is the record | PASS 20, FAIL 0, INFO 5 at `b17dc8d`; `docs/toolchain/VERIFICATION.md` is that run |
| `cargo xtask check` passes on the last commit | Passed at `b52c36c`, the commit that completed the code, in a turn on the machine (546 s; 1,587 host tests passed, none failed). The run on the last commit is reported to the coordinating session with that commit: a commit cannot hold the result of its own check |
| Controls (a) to (f) each fail | Each fails as said, and `verify.sh` exits 1 in every one. Two plants needed a second attempt (below) |
| Invariant 1: no image changes | The loader's three builds and the kernel are the same bytes at `0550226` and at `b52c36c` (four SHA-256 digests, in the provenance entry). No boot was run, and none was needed |
| The matrix rows | S10-04 is `done`, with a criterion of its own. S8-07 and S8-10 have theirs. The other rows named keep their criteria. All but S10-04 are `built`, and say that the CI run is owed. One row the contract did not name was stale and is brought up to date: S82-01, M0's own row, whose limitation said M0-2 and M0-3 fail; it stays `built` |

**What changed from the contract while building:**

- **`verify.sh` checks more on every run** than the contract's fourth objective said. The coordinating session asked, on 2026-11-12, that the test notice when it stops testing. So before the link is trusted, the code of `char16_len` in the library's object must name `wcslen` (read from its disassembly, not only from the object's list of undefined symbols, which another loop could satisfy), and the library must be one object. A control (d2) was added for the second by itself: the loop kept, and another uncalled function naming a symbol nothing defines.
- **`verify.sh` also checks that the version is written the same in three places** (`rust-toolchain.toml`, `Cargo.toml`'s `rust-version`, and the script), and its header lists the four places a compiler upgrade changes together, the commit file among them. A control (g) leaves `rust-version` behind, and the script fails. The coordinating session asked that an upgrade could not leave the new file behind; no file in the tree says what an upgrade changes (§15's rows are not started), so the script says it and holds it.
- **The loop counts with `wrapping_add`.** With overflow checks on, which the second half of item 22 will turn on for the loader, `n += 1` loses the call to `wcslen` and the test would have gone quiet.
- **Two files the contract did not name.** `.github/ci/checkout-unchanged.sh` is new: the "checkout as committed" step is in two jobs, the new one and `check`, so it is a script and not a line of the workflow. And `.github/workflows/processor-sweep.yml` gives its two cargo commands `--locked`: the contract named `ci.yml` alone, and the second workflow had not been read when it was written. **Changing that file starts the workflow's short sweep on the pull request that carries the change,** by the workflow's own design; it is a commit of its own, so the keeper of `land/s4` can leave it out.
- **Controls.** (a) was also run with a commit file that is not a hash and with none. (e)'s first plant, a `transmute`, failed the check on `x86_64-unknown-uefi` only, because rustc 1.99.1 still masks the byte on the two System V targets; the body Rust 1.99.0 produced, written in assembly, fails all three. (f)'s first attempt planted nothing and passed; it was planted again with an asserted replacement.
- **`VERIFICATION.md` was recorded three times,** each time from a clean tree, so that the record comes from the script the tree holds.
- **`land/s4` moved while the task was built, and was merged in at `61aae61`:** `main` with ADR-0018, and TASKS item 26's budgets file. Documents only, with no conflict. The contract says ADR-0018 is not in this tree; since the merge it is. The cost ledger in `docs/engineering/PERFORMANCE_BUDGETS.md` is owed no row by this task: it lists what the design pays on each operation or pays unmeasured on a path, and nothing here is on any path.

**What was run on each commit** (the contract: the full check on the last one, and this list for the others):

|---|---|---|

### 16. The CPU identification table (ADR-0009 Amendment 1, A2; decision items 88 and 89)

Sent by the coordinating session on 2026-11-12; written here on 2026-11-12, before any code. It builds TASKS item 16 and CLAUDE.md §24's statement that the kernel names the core generation (requirements rows S24-18, S24-19, S24-20 and A09-A2).

| Field | |
|---|---|
| Task ID | TASKS item 16, on branch `claude/t16-cpu-ident-621a3b`, cut from `land/s4` at `5ea97ef`. |
| Objective | (1) One table, inside the framework, from CPUID's vendor, family and model to a core generation and to whether that generation is in the declared scope (ADR-0009 A1, A2). Each row cites the vendor publication it rests on, with its revision and the date it was read. A row Intel or AMD has not confirmed says so, and never puts a machine in scope that the rule alone would not. (2) The table's column for the form `origin_frame::nospec` uses (ADR-0017 Z9, which says the table "gains a column"): `nospec::choose` moves into it, with the same answer for every maker and family. (3) The home for errata, keyed by vendor, family, model and a range of steppings, holding only entries a vendor documents and the kernel acts on, or none. (4) One line at boot, from the boot processor's CPUID: the vendor, family, model and stepping, the generation the table names, and whether the machine is in the declared scope or a bench machine, and why. The boot never stops on it, no proof's verdict depends on it, and instructions are still chosen from feature bits. (5) Two QEMU boots that set `family=` and `model=`, one in the scope and one outside it, in `boot-matrix`. **Not in this task:** the installer's choice (item 90, M9); any erratum workaround the kernel does not already need; any change to an accepted decision's text: corrections go to the coordinating session as proposals. |
| Source ownership | This task alone, in this worktree. New: `framekernel/frame/src/cpuident.rs` (the table, the errata home, decoding, the line's text, host tests), with `#![forbid(unsafe_code)]`; `docs/research/2026-11-12-cpu-identification-table.md` (the sources read today). Shared, and how far: `framekernel/frame/src/lib.rs` (the module list); `nospec.rs` (`choose` and `Maker` move to the table; `Form` and every lookup stay as they are); `speculation.rs` (`Reported`'s maker and family read through the table's decoding; the plan's form from the table); `cpu.rs` (one safe function that reads leaves 0 and 1, and no `unsafe` added); `framekernel/src/kernel.rs` (the line after the CPU features line, and `prove_nospec`'s comparison); `developer-tools/xtask/src/main.rs`: a `boot-identity` command and function of its own, the generic marker for the line in the default boot, the exact line for the two identities, and one call in `boot_matrix` (S4-1 edits xtask in another session: no other hunk of xtask is touched, and `launch.rs` and `.cargo/config.toml` not at all); records: this file, `docs/STATUS.md`, `SOURCE_PROVENANCE.md` (one entry), the requirements rows for §24 and ADR-0009, and dated as-built notes under ADR-0009 Amendment 1, ADR-0013's evidence and ADR-0017's Z9 that change no decision. The coordinating session merges it into the batch. |
| Architecture contract | ADR-0009 Amendment 1, A1 and A2 (items 88, 89): the rule, the line, no refusal, features from feature bits, both answers tested under QEMU, not a security control. ADR-0017 Z9 (item 132): the form by maker and family, Fence for anything unknown, and the table as its home. ADR-0013 R3 and R8: the line is not a proof and never stops the boot. ADR-0011 N6 and N13: no `unsafe` outside the listed framework files. CLAUDE.md §3 (items 91–97): vendor documents only, read today; no operating system's table is read for this. §14, §16, §24. Nothing here is an ABI or format decision (§84): the line is console text, and no interface changes. |
| Relevant specifications | Read on 2026-11-12, each recorded with its revision and date in the new ledger (§16, item 96): Intel's SDM Volume 2A, CPUID leaves 0 and 1 (the vendor string, the signature, how the display family and model are formed); AMD's APM Volume 3 (24594), CPUID Fn0000_0000 and Fn0000_0001_EAX; Intel's microcode release notes and perfmon `mapfile.csv` at today's commit; AMD's uProf guide and its PPRs for family 1Ah; Intel's own product pages for the launch of Clearwater Forest (to check A1's "March 2026"); Intel's and AMD's errata documents only as far as deciding whether any entry belongs in the home. PDFs are fetched with `curl` and read in memory, never opened in the browser and never committed. |
| Required dependencies | None new. `land/s4` at `5ea97ef`. No answer from the owner is needed to build it; any correction found to A1's or A2's text is sent to the coordinating session. |
| Security invariants | (1) The table decides only the scope line, Z9's form and, where it has entries, an erratum's workaround: no feature is enabled or instruction chosen from it (§14, §24). (2) The boot never stops on the scope, and no verdict counts it (A2, ADR-0013 R3). (3) Z9's form is unchanged for every maker and family, Fence by default and for anything unknown (item 132). (4) Fail closed for the claim: a vendor, family or model the table does not place is outside the scope; an unconfirmed row cannot put a machine in scope. (5) The errata home holds only entries with a vendor's document, identifier and revision, keyed by vendor, family, model and stepping. (6) No `unsafe` in the new file, and the unsafe gate's list is unchanged. (7) The line claims only what CPUID reported: a hypervisor can report anything, so it is not a security control (A2), and no matrix row claims more. |
| Performance constraints | None set. The table is read once at boot, and the form is still chosen once, by the boot processor; no lookup's cost changes. The nospec figures the boot prints are reported before and after as QEMU readings, with no budget (§53, item 115). |
| Required tests | Host, in `origin-frame`: the signature's decoding by each vendor's rule (the extended family only under base family 0Fh; the extended model under Intel's base family 6 or 0Fh and AMD's 0Fh), against signatures the vendors publish; every row's answer and each boundary (AMD 19h out, 1Ah and above in; Intel's listed family-6 models in and their neighbours out; Intel families 15 to 17 out, 18 and above in; another vendor out); the table's form (keys unique, every row sourced, no unconfirmed row in scope that the rule would leave out); Z9's form equal to the old rule's for every maker and every family up to 10Eh; the errata home's lookup over a test-only list, and the shipped list well formed; the line's exact text for each answer. Planted faults in the rule and the table, one at a time, each of which must fail a test. Boot: the two identities, each with its exact line, its verdict and the halt line, so a refusal fails the boot. Negative controls, each a temporary edit booted once and put back byte for byte: the AMD threshold moved down to 19h (the outside boot must fail); model CCh taken out of Intel's list (the inside boot must fail); the kernel halting after an outside line (the outside boot must fail). |
| Actual deliverables | The table and errata home with their tests; the scope line; `nospec::choose` moved into the table; `cargo xtask boot-identity` in `boot-matrix`; the ledger; the records above. Small commits, each of which builds and passes its tests. Nothing pushed. |
| Acceptance conditions | On the task's branch, on the final commit: `cargo xtask check` (the requirements gate included), `cargo xtask boot-test` and `cargo xtask boot-matrix` pass, every boot run as `cargo xtask exclusive -- <command>`; the host tests and both identity boots pass; each planted fault and each negative control fails as stated; requirements rows S24-18, S24-19, S24-20 and A09-A2 move to `built` with criteria of their own (not `done`: their scope is hardware, and the evidence is QEMU's); provenance, STATUS and TASKS are current; corrections to accepted text, Clearwater Forest's date among them, sent to the coordinating session with a recommendation. Then the branch, the commit and the results go to the coordinating session. Nothing here runs on physical hardware. |

### 43. The progress page: `docs/PROGRESS.md`, generated from the matrix and the roadmap, and a gate that keeps it current

Assigned by the coordinating session on 2026-11-12, at the owner's request: "give me clear progress status table, which need to be updated for every PR is merged". Written here before any of it was built, from `docs/engineering/TASK_CONTRACT_TEMPLATE.md`.

| Field | |
|---|---|
| Task ID | TASKS item 43, on branch `claude/t43-progress-page`, cut from `claude/t42-roadmap-gate` at `5e80911` (TASKS item 42's last commit). |
| Objective | `cargo xtask progress` writes `docs/PROGRESS.md` from the requirements matrix (`docs/requirements/`), the headings of CLAUDE.md and the ADRs, and the placements and state words of `docs/ROADMAP.md`. A gate in `cargo xtask check` regenerates the page in memory and fails if the committed file differs by a byte, naming the command to run. CI runs `check` on every pull request, so a pull request with a stale page fails. The page holds, in this order: one paragraph saying in plain words what done, built, in progress (`partial` plus `not-verified`) and not started mean (items 193 and 221); the overall counts of counted rows in those four states, with where evidence ran (host, emulation, virtual machine, hardware); each milestone M0 to M13 with the rows placed in it, the four counts, a labelled percentage done and a labelled percentage done or built, and the state word its roadmap section gives; the fourteen feature groups of `build` rows by directive section, then each section under its group, with the section's title read from its CLAUDE.md heading; one line per accepted ADR; one line for the standing rules. Every figure is a count of rows. One line goes into the matrix README's "Changing the matrix". **Not in this task:** weighting of any state; any figure that is not a count of rows; a change to any row's state or to the roadmap's text; the README's stale status line (TASKS 30); a change to the CI workflow, which already runs `check`. |
| Source ownership | This task alone edits: a new module `developer-tools/xtask/src/progress.rs`, its subcommand and one line in `check` in `main.rs`; `docs/PROGRESS.md`; the narrow changes to `requirements.rs` and `roadmap.rs` that let the page use their parsers (the matrix's rows read by one function both use, the directive's section titles and the ADRs' titles kept, the roadmap's placements and state words returned) with no change to what either gate accepts; one line of `docs/requirements/README.md`; this contract and its result here; this task's entry at the end of `SOURCE_PROVENANCE.md` and `xtask`'s row of its table; the matrix rows the result changes, if any. The coordinating session owns the decision sheet and the numbering of any proposal. |
| Architecture contract | CLAUDE.md §1 ("Maintain complete traceability", "Report verified completion"), §75 (the same tree gives the same bytes), §82; decision items 122, 193 (done is the completion target's measure), 221 (built and in progress are not coverage), 255 and 256, 264. ADR-0018 V3 (state the cost). `docs/requirements/README.md` for what a counted row is (not `info`, not `removed`). No ABI or format decision, so no ADR (§84). |
| Relevant specifications | None outside the repository. |
| Required dependencies | `5e80911`. No new crate: `xtask` stays `std` only. No owner answer awaited: the fourteen groups and the page's order are the coordinating session's brief. Once TASKS 22a reaches `land/s4` and is merged into the base, the page is regenerated; its counts change with the rows, not with this task. |
| Security invariants | (1) The gate fails closed: no matrix row, no milestone, no group section or no title read is a failure, not an empty page. (2) The page depends on nothing but the tree: no clock, host, path, user or commit hash, so two runs on one tree give the same bytes (§75). (3) Every figure on the page is a count of matrix rows, and the four states of a table add up to its count of rows. (4) No state is weighted: a `partial` row counts as one row in progress. (5) `progress` writes one file, `docs/PROGRESS.md`, and the gate only reads. (6) Neither existing gate accepts anything it refused before. |
| Performance constraints | None, by construction (ADR-0018 V3, items 238 to 246): the task adds a host tool and a gate that run on the build host. The gate adds under one second to `cargo xtask check`; measured and recorded. |
| Required tests | Unit tests on a small fixture of matrix rows, milestones and sections: the four counts, the two percentages, the groups and sections, the ADR and rule lines, rows outside the groups, a milestone with no state word refused, a section with no heading refused. A test that the tree's committed page equals the generated one. Planted faults on the real tree, each refused by `cargo xtask check` and put back byte for byte (SHA-256 before and after): a page edited by hand (stale), and a row's state changed without regenerating the page. Both existing gates' tests pass unchanged. |
| Actual deliverables | `cargo xtask progress`; `docs/PROGRESS.md`; the gate and its tests; the README line; this task's provenance entry. Small commits, each of which passes `cargo xtask check`. Nothing pushed. |
| Acceptance conditions | `cargo xtask check` passes on the task's last commit, with the gate in it. The two planted faults are refused, with their output recorded. The page's figures agree with the requirements gate's own figure (counted rows and each state) and with an independent count by script, both recorded. No row of the matrix asks for this page by name: it reports the matrix that S1-03 (done) asks for and changes no row's state, so it completes no row. Then the branch, the commit and the results go to the coordinating session. Nothing here runs on QEMU or on physical hardware. |

**Result (2026-11-12): done, on the branch, not landed.** The evidence is in `SOURCE_PROVENANCE.md`, under "TASKS item 43". Where it differs from the contract above:

- **The planted faults ran through `cargo xtask progress-gate`,** the function `check` calls, one run each, and not through a full `check` each: the machine's turn lock was held by other worktrees' boots. A full `check` ran once, on the task's last commit.
- **The roadmap gate's tests changed in their fixture only:** the rows are written in the matrix's thirteen columns and file names, so that the requirements gate's reader reads them. Every assertion is as it was.
- **Row S1-52** was corrected in a commit of its own, at the coordinating session's request (`5556e4a`). Its limitation was made untrue by TASKS items 25 and 42. Its state is unchanged.
- **The milestone state word** is read from the two forms the roadmap uses: "State, <date>: <word>" and "Marked against the tests, <date>: <word>". Nothing checks that a word matches its milestone's tests; S1-52's limitation says so.

It adds, removes and reorders no instruction, and nothing reads those strings but a panic's report. What is measured and reported: each image's size, its count of instructions and of each mnemonic, with and without the remap, on the development Mac. |

### 32, step 2. The gate builds and reads each carried program by itself, and the userspace triple's flags variable is refused

So step 1's matrix rows, which say the kernel "with the program it carries" is built twice and compared, said more than was shown: the kernel was built twice, the program once.
