**Result (2026-11-12): built and gated on the development Mac; the comparison with CI gave a finding.** The evidence is in `SOURCE_PROVENANCE.md`, under item 32's entry ("Step 2"), and in the ledger's §12. Nothing is pushed; the keeper merges the branch.

- **The gates passed on `9648878`,** the last commit that changes code, in one turn: `cargo xtask check` (1,673 host tests, 0 failed, 27 ignored; Loom 53 models, 78 controls), `cargo xtask boot-test` (11 of 11) and `cargo xtask boot-matrix` (124 of 124 scenarios, first attempt, none repeated).
- **The gate reads ten files** and passes in 38 s (37 s before this step): the three loaders, the program as the linker wrote it, its four segment files, its description, and the kernel, whose image must hold each segment. With a second copy of the toolchain at another path it prints the same ten digests. Invariant 4 held: the four images' digests are the ones `9e4f79f` gave before any of this code.
- **The control fails where it is aimed.** With the remap out of the ring-3 build alone, the gate stops at the program as the linker wrote it, before any kernel is built.
- **Thirteen variables are refused,** each before any build: the flags variables, the compiler and wrapper variables, and the freestanding targets' linker variables. Two are let through, and a configuration file outside the repository is a recorded limit (ledger §12.3).
- **A test of step 1's failed on CI** (run 43230979284 at `9e4f79f`): it read the toolchain directory's name, which holds the userspace triple on a Linux host. Fixed in `9648878`. Because of it, CI has run nothing after `xtask`'s own tests on a tree that holds item 32: not the other host tests, not Loom, not this gate.
- **The comparison with CI, stated image by image.** Of a tree that holds item 32: none of the ten files was compared, since CI's gate has not run on one. Of `f5ab54f`, the last commit CI printed digests for, which is before item 32, with the Mac's build given CI's toolchain path and CI's host line:
  - the loader with no feature: the same bytes as CI's, with the path alone;
  - the loader with `test-trust-store`, and with `developer-mode`: the same bytes as CI's, with both;
  - the kernel: not the same, with both. Why is not known;
  - the carried program's files: CI printed nothing for them at that commit.
- **The finding: an image's bytes still follow the kind of host that builds it.** Cargo's `-C metadata` value for a build script takes in the host the compiler reports, and every crate that depends on a build script takes that in. `origin-version` and `framekernel` have one each. So CI's digests for the batch are expected to differ from the Mac's for the two feature loaders and the kernel. The ledger's §12.5 gives the two loader digests CI should print if this is the whole of it.
- **Where it differs from the contract.** The contract's comparison was to be of one commit on both machines as they are. That was not possible without a CI run that reaches the gate, so it was made on `f5ab54f` with the Mac given CI's two inputs, and the direct one is left as a follow-up. One source outside the contract's list was read: one file of Cargo's own source, recorded in the provenance entry.
- **Proposed to the coordinating session:** the two build scripts out of the images' dependency graphs, which it made TASKS item 44; and CI keeping the gate's files, so a differing image can be compared byte by byte, which it asked for at once and which is built (ledger §12.8). **Follow-up:** once CI's `check` passes on a head that holds `9648878`, read its ten digests against §12.7's and §12.5's and record the result in the matrix rows.
- **The follow-up, done on 2026-11-12** (ledger §13; the batch pushed at `c34fdab`, CI's run 43248605239). CI's `check` passed, the gate with it, and the artifact was kept. Four of the ten files are the same bytes on the Mac and on CI, and six are not. The three loaders came out as predicted. The rest has a third cause, found in CI's kept files: the linker writes its own name for itself into each ELF's `.comment`, and its Linux build gives it differently from its Mac build; through the section table's offset in the ELF header that moves one loaded byte of the carried program and of the kernel. With CI's host line and CI's linker string the Mac writes all ten of CI's files bit for bit, so there is no fourth. Item 44 removes the host line's way in; the linker's string is open, and a way to end it is put to the coordinating session.

### 44. No unit of an image depends on a unit built for the host

Assigned by the coordinating session on 2026-11-12, as the first of the three proposals item 32's step 2 made; it found that no accepted text names a build script and that this needs no owner decision. Written before any of it was built. Step 2 showed that an image's bytes follow the kind of host that builds it (ledger `docs/research/2026-11-11-toolchain-path-in-images.md`, §12.5 and §12.6): Cargo's `-C metadata` value for a unit built for the host takes in the `host:` line of `rustc -vV`, and each unit takes in the values of the units it depends on.

| Field | |
|---|---|
| Task ID | TASKS item 44, on branch `claude/item-32-toolchain-path`, after `41c03c2`, which sits on `origin/land/s4`'s tip `9e4f79f` and holds item 32's step 2. |
| Objective | No unit of an image depends on a unit built for the host: the loader each way it is built, each carried program, and the kernel. Then Cargo's value for each of their crates takes in nothing of the host, and an image's bytes do not follow the kind of host that builds it. The two build scripts in those graphs go: `runtime/version/build.rs`, whose constants come from a generated file in the tree, and `framekernel/build.rs`, whose link arguments move to `.cargo/config.toml`. A gate keeps it so. **Not in this task:** any change to what the reproducible-build gate requires; narrowing what "two machines" means in a row's criterion; a change to any constant's value, to the kernel's layout or to what any image does. If CI's kernel still differs afterwards, the cause is looked for in CI's kept file and reported, and the gate is not changed to pass. |
| Source ownership | This task alone edits `runtime/version/` (the script removed, the generator's text moved into `manifest.rs`, `src/generated.rs` added, one line of `src/lib.rs`), removes `framekernel/build.rs` and corrects the comment that names it in `framekernel/Cargo.toml`, adds the kernel's link arguments to `.cargo/config.toml`, corrects the one comment in `.cargo/userspace.toml` that names the script, and adds to `xtask` one module with the two gates and the command, wired into `main.rs`. No other line of the kernel, the loader or the version crate changes. In the matrix it edits S62-16, which names the removed script, and item 32's six rows. It edits its own entry in `SOURCE_PROVENANCE.md`, the ledger and this file. It never commits to `land/s4` and pushes nothing. |
| Architecture contract | CLAUDE.md 7.6 §62 (the constants are generated from `origin-version.toml`, the one place a version is written by hand), §75 and §60, §14 (W^X, full RELRO, segment permissions), §13; ONBF §6.1, §6.1.2 and §6.5 (the shape of the kernel's ELF that `onbf-pack` converts); ADR-0001 default 9 (full RELRO); ADR-0018 V3 for the cost. Not an ABI or format decision (§84): no constant, no layout and no interface changes. |
| Relevant specifications | The Cargo book as published for 1.99.1: "Build Scripts" (what makes a package have one, and the `build` key), "Configuration" (`target.<triple>.rustflags`) and the `cargo metadata` reference (a target's `kind`), read on 2026-11-12. Cargo's own source file already recorded as R3 in the ledger. None else outside the repository. |
| Required dependencies | `41c03c2`. No new crate or tool (§5). No owner answer is awaited. The comparison with CI waits for the keeper's push and a CI run that passes `check`. |
| Security invariants | (1) Every generated constant has the value it has today, and `cargo xtask check` fails when `runtime/version/src/generated.rs` is not what the generator writes from `origin-version.toml`. (2) Nothing in the generated file comes from the host or the time of the build: it is a function of `origin-version.toml` alone. (3) The kernel is linked with every argument `framekernel/build.rs` passes today, in the same order: the same entry, no `PT_INTERP`, no text relocations, each loadable segment on its own page with its own permissions, full RELRO, no executable stack. (4) `cargo xtask check` fails if any package in an image's dependency graph has a build script or is a procedural macro. (5) No boot proof, marker or bound changes. (6) `xtask`'s remap and its refusals are unchanged. |
| Performance constraints | None set. Performance cost, in ADR-0018 V3's terms: none is expected, since no code generation flag changes; this is stated in the result only if the comparison of the kernel before and after shows the same code apart from its order and the hashes in symbol names. Reported: the gate's time, and each image's size before and after. |
| Required tests | **Host tests:** the generated text for a known `origin-version.toml`; a generated file that is out of step, refused with the command that rewrites it; the packages with a build script or a procedural macro read from `cargo metadata`'s output, from a sample and from this workspace; a graph that holds one, refused by name. **On the real tree, in order:** (a) the baseline: the ten digests the gate prints at `41c03c2`. (b) A transitional commit: the constants read from the generated file and the link arguments in `.cargo/config.toml`, with both build scripts left in place and empty. Cargo's values do not change then, so the ten digests must be exactly the baseline's. If they are not, the work stops there until the reason is found. (c) The scripts removed: the bytes change once. The kernel before and after is compared by its program headers, its sections, and its symbols with their sizes, and by the count of each instruction; the loaders by their sections and instruction counts. Old and new digests are recorded. (d) With the compiler's reported host changed to CI's, by the stand-in of the ledger's §12.5 and plain `cargo` at two jobs, each image is the same bytes as with the host as it is. That is a prediction of what CI prints and is recorded as one. **Negative controls,** each put back with `git checkout`, everything else committed first: `framekernel/build.rs` put back, and the gate fails naming the package; a value changed in `origin-version.toml` without the file rewritten, and the gate fails naming the command. **Gates:** `cargo xtask check`, `cargo xtask boot-test` and `cargo xtask boot-matrix` on the last commit that changes code, the boots in the machine's turn. |
| Actual deliverables | The generated file, its command and its gate; the link arguments in the configuration file; the gate on the images' dependency graphs; their tests and controls; the comparison of the images before and after with both sets of digests; the comparison with CI when a pushed commit allows it, and with it whether CI's jobs on two runner images write the same bytes, as evidence; a plain list of whatever still follows the build host afterwards, each with what was tried; the matrix rows; the provenance entry. Nothing is pushed by this session. |
| Acceptance conditions | The transitional digests are the baseline's. The three gates pass on the last commit with code changes, on the development Mac under QEMU's software processor. Each control fails where it is aimed. With CI's host line the Mac's ten files do not change. S62-16 stays `done` with its criterion in its new words: the identity is generated from `origin-version.toml`, and `check` fails when the generated text is out of step with it. Rows S60-03, S60-11, S75-05, S75-06, S75-07 and F-28 become `done` only if every one of the ten files is the same bytes on the Mac and on CI for one pushed commit; otherwise they stay `partial` and say which files were the same and which were not. The coordinating session checks the result. |

**What changed from that contract while building.** Two things were added, each at the coordinating session's word, and one was found.

- *The linker's line about itself.* The first comparison with CI (ledger §13), made while this was being built, found a third thing a build took from its host. The coordinating session put ending it into this task: one entry in `.cargo/config.toml`, and a check in the reproducible-build gate. The contract's objective names the build scripts alone.
- *Which gates on which commit.* For the commits after `a81af90` the coordinating session set `cargo xtask check` and `boot-test`; the keeper runs all three on the merge. The contract asks for all three on the last commit with code changes.
- *An existing target directory.* Cargo does not link a file again when the entry appears, and `boot-test` on `3761692` booted a kernel one byte off the gate's. `68ed2b1` makes `xtask` link such a file again. Not in the contract. It was written expecting the keeper's checkout to hold such a file; it did not. The merge itself changed what the kernel and `first-program` are built from, Cargo linked both again, and the keeper's build printed no relink note. So this path has been exercised by a host test and by one run on one stale directory in this session's checkout, and nowhere else.

**Result (2026-11-12): built and gated on the development Mac; not yet compared with CI.** The evidence is in `SOURCE_PROVENANCE.md`, "No image is built from a unit built for the host", and in the ledger's §13 and §14. Nothing is pushed; the keeper merges the branch.

- **Nothing was lost.** At the transitional commit `62d045a`, with the constants in the generated file, the link arguments in the configuration and both scripts left empty, each of the gate's ten files was the same bytes as at `41c03c2`.
- **The scripts went** (`da2b82e`). The kernel before and after: the same 20 section headers and 9 program headers, field for field; 1,644 symbols with the same names, kinds and sizes; 245,551 instructions, the same count of each; every function the same instructions with branch targets and `%rip` displacements masked; 132 functions at another address. Performance cost, in ADR-0018 V3's first form: none, by construction.
- **The linker's line is fixed** (`3761692`). It changes `.comment`, one header byte of the kernel and of the program, and the copy of the program's header byte that the kernel carries. The loaders do not change.
- **With CI's host line the Mac's ten files do not change.** Before this task four did. A prediction, not a comparison.
- **The gates, each on its commit.** On `a81af90`: `check` (1,683 host tests, 0 failed, 27 ignored), `boot-test` (11 of 11), `boot-matrix` (124 of 124, first attempt, none repeated). On `68ed2b1`, the last commit that changes code: the gate with the ten digests below, `check` (1,688 host tests, 0 failed, 27 ignored; Loom 53 models, 78 controls) and `boot-test` (11 of 11); the kernel that booted is the gate's, byte for byte.
- **Seven controls, each failing where aimed:** the kernel's script put back; a script given to the loader's package; the two original scripts in place, named under the loader, the kernel and the carried program; a value changed in `origin-version.toml` without the file rewritten; the linker's entry taken out; the entry not forced; and the check run by hand on the Mac's and CI's kernels from before the entry.
- **Two faults of this task's own, each found by a gate or a comparison and fixed:** clippy over `origin-version`'s tests at `da2b82e` (`a81af90`); and the kernel that booted on `3761692` one byte off the gate's (`68ed2b1`).
- **The ten digests CI should print for the merged head,** the Mac's at `68ed2b1`: loader `b3ee60b9173235fdc38d708a2c95e14e3c065abdc8d89b28231c693a787dd6d2`; with `test-trust-store` `a9f7ddf8536b6cef49e81af38bc3dd70b2788e9baedd6d26cde2c85013830905`; with `developer-mode` `ee2a063f093fbbf4135f2b29155165d901d4c5b7bb29c20e915d36024e7e3eaf`; `first_program` as linked `8a6658ab4662300661306886431348de8a1346c084bd61acda1901b30052b627`; its segments `9e35a5739dc0e0133499a77bd12430720f0788a30c50e7f39e27b4b3c68dbf16`, `01f7addb33cac76c6aa8bf93d7607b2ab3ed30eb8455b684846fdf920fbad38c`, `592ee8a478796bb0cc4274c7cb9336f57a6fa4f5c834b24389944e553bccdb14` and `3341bcae4c97d7ba73e0728c783373725905eab0417cf220037920f0960679cb`; its description `364739ab7e1b18462e7fffeefe1a0e33282e1d795a19cd271fdef08487991bc5`; the kernel ELF, 1,680,400 bytes, `897a6d0fc43a2cae92342f69da35a8b3d34bda30df3f60637bbf66c9858b178a`.
- **The matrix.** S62-16 stays `done`, with the generated file and its gate in place of the script. S60-03, S60-11, S75-05, S75-06, S75-07 and F-28 stay `partial`: no file of a tree with this task has been built on CI.
- **Next on this branch, as its own commits** (item 44, step 2): the image that boots, `onbf-pack`'s conversion of the kernel's ELF, in the reproducible-build gate. Its contract and result follow.

### 44, step 2. The reproducible-build gate compares the image that boots

Asked for by the coordinating session on 2026-11-12, after this session reported that the gate compares the kernel's ELF and that what boots is `onbf-pack`'s conversion of it. Written before any of it was built. Its own commits, after item 44's final hash `a2a2700`; it does not hold item 44.

| Field | |
|---|---|
| Task ID | TASKS item 44, step 2, on branch `claude/item-32-toolchain-path`, after `a2a2700`. |
| Objective | The reproducible-build gate converts the kernel's ELF from each of its two builds with `onbf-pack krn`, as `cargo xtask build` and the boots do, and treats the converted image as it treats every other file: the two must be the same bytes, neither may hold a path of the machine or the linker's own line, its digest is printed, and a copy of each is kept, so CI's artifact holds it. The comparison with CI then covers what boots. **Not in this task:** any change to `onbf-pack`, to the image's format, or to how anything is signed; a signed image in the gate. |
| Source ownership | This task alone edits `developer-tools/xtask/src/reproducible.rs`, and in `main.rs` the one function that runs the conversion, to give it an output path. It edits its own records. Nothing under `developer-tools/onbf-pack/`. |
| Architecture contract | CLAUDE.md 7.6 §75, §60, §59 (signing runs in host tools; Origin OS verifies); ONBF §6.1.2, §6.5 and ONBF-R9 (the image is a function of its inputs; the claimed signing time is an input). Not an ABI or format decision (§84). |
| Relevant specifications | None outside the repository. `onbf-pack`'s own account of what is and is not deterministic (`developer-tools/onbf-pack/src/sign.rs`), read on 2026-11-12. |
| Required dependencies | `a2a2700`. No new crate or tool (§5). No owner answer is awaited. |
| Security invariants | (1) The converted image the gate reads is made by the same command, with the same arguments, as the one the boots start. (2) The gate fails if the two builds' images differ, naming both kept copies. (3) Nothing about signing changes: the gate signs nothing and reads no key. (4) No image's bytes change. |
| Performance constraints | None set. Reported: the gate's time before and after. Performance cost: none, by construction: no image changes. |
| Required tests | Host tests: the gate's list of what it builds holds the converted image after the kernel; the check that the kernel carries its programs' segments still reads the ELF. On the real tree: the gate, with the image's digest; that digest against the image the boots used; a control in which the conversion is made to depend on where it is written (a one-line edit of `xtask`, put back with `git checkout`), which the gate must fail at the converted image. Two signings of one image, compared, to say what the signature is a limit on. `cargo xtask check` and `cargo xtask boot-test` on the last commit that changes code. |
| Actual deliverables | The gate's eleventh file with its tests and control; the record of what signing does to an image and why it is not in the gate; the Mac's digest for the image, for the comparison with CI. Nothing is pushed by this session. |
| Acceptance conditions | The gate, `check` and `boot-test` pass on the last commit with code changes, on the development Mac under QEMU's software processor. The control fails at the converted image. The image the boots used has the digest the gate prints. Rows S60-03, S60-11, S75-05, S75-06, S75-07 and F-28 name the image among what is compared and stay `partial` until CI has built it. The coordinating session checks the result. |

**Result (2026-11-12): built and gated on the development Mac; not yet compared with CI.** The evidence is in `SOURCE_PROVENANCE.md`, under item 44's entry ("Step 2"), and in the ledger's §15. Nothing is pushed.

- **The gate reads eleven files.** The eleventh is the kernel image that boots: 1,545,848 bytes, `239f308b539b85e2b28d14ee066ca9652cc8c021600f2f7490421bba9a6f5c92` on the Mac. The other ten digests are item 44's. It passes in 35 s (34 s before).
- **The control fails where it is aimed:** with the gate's reading of the converted image made to follow its directory, the gate fails at that image, after the ten others had passed.
- **The gates on `3fe5aef`,** the last commit that changes code: the reproducible-build gate, `cargo xtask check` (1,689 host tests, 0 failed, 27 ignored; Loom 53 models, 78 controls) and `cargo xtask boot-test` (11 of 11). After the boots the image they used is the gate's, byte for byte. No boot matrix: no image changed.
- **Signing.** No signed image is in the gate and nothing about signing changed. Two signings of one image differ only within one span the length of an ML-DSA-87 signature, which is hedged with fresh randomness by design; everything outside it is the same bytes. The signature's own bytes are the limit.
- **Where it differs from the contract:** nothing.
- **The digest CI should print for the image** is the Mac's above. Not seen when this was written: `onbf-pack` is a host tool, and its Linux build had converted nothing for this comparison yet.
- **Compared with CI on the merged head, 2026-11-12** (ledger §16; `origin/land/s4` at `8ae9e9b`; run 43275652672, job 131383211984). CI's `check` passed (1,690 host tests, 0 failed, 27 ignored), with the three gates of item 44 passing on a Linux host. **All eleven files are the same bytes on the Mac and on CI,** compared file by file with CI's kept files: the three loaders, the carried program's six files, the kernel's ELF and the kernel image that boots. Each digest is the one written down before CI ran. That is eleven files, two hosts and one commit, compared by hand. The six rows say what it does not show. The coordinating session decided the states the same day: S60-03, S60-11 and S75-05 are `built`, each row saying why it is not `done`; S75-06, S75-07 and F-28 stay `partial`. S60-11 is not `done` because nothing yet would notice a fourth input that differs between kinds of host: the comparison across two of them was made once, by hand.

### 45. A command that compares a directory of kept files with the reproducible-build gate's own

Assigned by the coordinating session on 2026-11-12, as the second of the three things item 44's comparison with CI put to it (ledger §16.4). Written before any of it was built. The reproducible-build gate leaves a copy of every file it read, from each of its two builds, under `target/xtask/reproducible/files/`, and CI's `check` job keeps that directory as an artifact. Comparing two such directories was done by hand, with a script in a scratch directory.

| Field | |
|---|---|
| Task ID | TASKS item 45, on branch `claude/item-32-toolchain-path`, after `5dd8876`. |
| Objective | `cargo xtask reproducible-compare <directory>` compares a directory of kept files, CI's artifact unpacked or another checkout's `files/`, with this checkout's own, file by file. For each of the gate's two builds the two sides must hold the same set of names, and each file must be the same bytes. Every difference is named with its first differing offset; a file missing on either side is a failure and never a skip. It prints each file's size and digest, and how many of how many agree. **Not in this task:** a gate in `cargo xtask check`; any change to CI's workflow; fetching an artifact; knowing which commit either side was built from; anything about a signed image. |
| Source ownership | This task alone edits `developer-tools/xtask/src/reproducible.rs`, and in `main.rs` the command's one line and its usage text. It edits its own records and row S75-07. |
| Architecture contract | CLAUDE.md 7.6 §75 ("Compare artifacts", "Investigate differences") and §60; ONBF-R9. The directory compared is another machine's output, so the rule for untrusted input applies (§13: checked parsing; §58). Not an ABI or format decision (§84). Decision item 275, with the owner, is about a comparison that runs in CI; nothing of it is built here. |
| Relevant specifications | None outside the repository. |
| Required dependencies | `5dd8876`. No new crate or tool (§5). No owner answer is awaited. |
| Security invariants | (1) The directory is read as untrusted data. Only regular files directly inside its two build directories are read; a symbolic link, a directory or any other kind of entry there is a failure, and is not followed. Nothing in it is run. (2) No more of a file is read than this checkout's own file is long, and one byte: a file of any size on the other side costs no more memory than the gate's own. (3) The command writes nothing and builds nothing. (4) It never reports agreement when a name is on one side only, when a build's directory is missing, or when either side holds no file at all. |
| Performance constraints | None set. Performance cost: none, by construction: no image and no gate changes. |
| Required tests | Host tests, each on two scratch directories: two that agree; a planted difference, named with its first differing offset; a file that is a prefix of the other; a file missing from the other side; an extra file on the other side; a symbolic link in the other side, not followed; a subdirectory there; a build's directory missing; both sides empty. On the real files: this checkout's own against CI's artifact of run 43275652672 (`8ae9e9b`), which the comparison by hand found the same; as a control, against CI's artifact of run 43248605239 (`c34fdab`, before item 44), which must fail naming the six files that differ and the one that is missing. `cargo xtask check` on the last commit that changes code. |
| Actual deliverables | The command with its tests; the two comparisons above, made with it and recorded; row S75-07 naming its tool and what it still lacks. Nothing is pushed by this session. |
| Acceptance conditions | `cargo xtask check` passes on the last commit with code changes, on the development Mac. The comparison with run 43275652672's artifact finds every file the same, and the control fails as described. Row S75-07 stays `partial`: it names the command, and says that nothing runs it, that it has compared a development commit and no release candidate, and that it does not know which commit either side was built from. Its own criterion, when a task finishes it: for a release candidate, the unsigned images, which are everything a signature covers (ONBF-R9), are the same bytes from two independent builds, compared by this command; a signature's own bytes are outside it, since ML-DSA signing is hedged with fresh randomness (FIPS 204 §3.4). The coordinating session checks the result. |

**Result (2026-11-12): built and gated on the development Mac.** The evidence is in `SOURCE_PROVENANCE.md`, "The comparison of kept files is a command", and in the ledger's §17. Nothing is pushed.

- **The command** is `cargo xtask reproducible-compare <directory>`, in `reproducible.rs`. It is not a gate, changes no workflow, builds nothing and writes nothing.
- **Against CI's artifact of run 43275652672** (`8ae9e9b`): all 22 files, eleven from each of the two builds, are the same bytes. The comparison made by hand, made again with the command, before `check` and after it.
- **The control, against run 43248605239's artifact** (`c34fdab`): it fails; ten files the same, and for each build `kernel-image` missing and five files that differ, each named with its first offset.
- **Where it differs from the contract:** the contract expected six files to differ in the control; five do. Six is the count for the Mac's files of that artifact's own tree. This checkout's files are of the later tree, and two of them happen to be the bytes CI's older tree gave. I wrote the expectation without working it out for the two trees actually compared.
- **The gate:** `cargo xtask check` passes on `e78c65c` (1,694 host tests, 0 failed, 27 ignored; Loom 53 models, 78 controls). No boots: nothing that is built changed.
- **Row S75-07** names the command and its test, says what it still lacks, and stays `partial`. Its own criterion, for the task that finishes it, is in this contract's acceptance conditions.

### 46. CI compares the images across two kinds of host on every run

Assigned by the coordinating session after the owner accepted decision item 275 ("Accept all 5", 2026-11-13): CI compares the images across two machines on every run, with a job on GitHub's standard macOS runner beside the Linux one, and the two sets of kept files compared. Written before any of it was built. It lands in the batch after the third, which is frozen; the work stays on this branch from `ce279b8`.

**Checked before anything else, in GitHub's own documentation, on 2026-11-13** (19:18 UTC on 2026-11-12):

- *Free for this repository.* "GitHub-hosted runners reference" (`docs.github.com/en/actions/reference/runners/github-hosted-runners`), under "Standard GitHub-hosted runners for public repositories": "Use of the standard GitHub-hosted runners is free and unlimited on public repositories." "GitHub Actions billing" (`docs.github.com/en/billing/concepts/product-billing/github-actions`) lists standard runners in public repositories as free, and says larger runners are billed for public repositories too. The repository is public (`gh repo view`: `PUBLIC`).
- *Which labels are standard and Apple-processor.* The same reference table: macOS, 3 processors (M1), 7 GB of memory, 14 GB of storage, arm64, labels `macos-latest`, `macos-15`, `macos-26` (and `xcode-27`, which the runner images' own list marks as a preview). The Intel ones are `macos-15-intel` and `macos-26-intel`. The larger ones end in `-large` or `-xlarge` and are not used. The runner images' list (`github.com/actions/runner-images`) has `macos-latest` pointing at macOS 26 and says the macOS 14 images were retired on 2026-11-02.
- Both are as item 275 assumed. **The label used is `macos-26`:** an exact label, as the workflow has for Linux; the newest image that is not a preview; and the one with the longest time before it is retired.

| Field | |
|---|---|
| Task ID | TASKS item 46, on branch `claude/item-32-toolchain-path`, after `ce279b8`. Decision item 275. |
| Objective | On every run of the `ci` workflow, a job on a standard macOS runner builds the reproducible-build gate's files twice from nothing, keeps them, and compares them with the files the Linux `check` job kept, with `cargo xtask reproducible-compare`. The run fails on any difference, on a file missing from either side, and on a missing artifact. So a new input that differs between the two kinds of host fails CI on the commit that brings it. **Not in this task:** a third kind of host; a release candidate or its independent rebuild; a signed image; any change to the gate, to the command, or to what the Linux jobs do. |
| Source ownership | This task alone edits `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (one job added, and the header's first lines, which say the gates run on Linux alone) and `.github/ci/install-rust.sh` (its last lines, the machine's description, get a branch for macOS; nothing above them changes). It edits rows S60-03, S60-11, S75-06 and S75-07, its own records, and this file. For the trial only, and reverted: one line of the loader's source. |
| Architecture contract | Decision item 275; CLAUDE.md 7.6 §75, §60 and §80 (no spending: a standard runner, free for a public repository, and nothing else); §8 (every job builds with exactly the pinned compiler). TASKS items 44 and 45 for the gate and the command. Not an ABI or format decision (§84). The owner was told the job needs a trial before it is relied on: if the trial shows the runner unreliable, that goes to the coordinating session before the job is made required. |
| Relevant specifications | GitHub's documentation, read on 2026-11-13: the two pages above, and the runner images' list. The workflow syntax for `needs`, `runs-on` and artifacts as the workflow already uses it. |
| Required dependencies | `ce279b8`. No new crate or tool (§5). Two actions the workflows already pin by commit: `actions/upload-artifact` (v7.0.2) and `actions/download-artifact` (v8.0.2). The trial needs this branch pushed and a draft pull request, which is this session's user's to allow. |
| Security invariants | (1) The macOS job has no secrets and reads the repository only, as the others. (2) Every cargo command it starts first carries `--locked`. (3) The Linux job's artifact is read by `reproducible-compare` as untrusted data (item 45). (4) The comparison is of the macOS job's own gate output with the Linux job's, of one commit, in one run. (5) A difference, a missing file or a missing artifact fails the job; the step is not allowed to fail quietly. (6) No image's bytes change. |
| Performance constraints | None set. ADR-0018 V3 does not apply (no ADR); said anyway: nothing at run time. The cost is one more CI job on each push, and its measured minutes are reported from the trial. |
| Required tests | **The trial,** on a draft pull request against `land/s4` that is titled as a trial, says it will be closed, and is never merged: (a) the job passes with all eleven files, from both builds, the same on the two hosts; (b) how long it takes, and what the runner is; (c) that the macOS image sets none of the variables `xtask` refuses, which `xtask` itself shows by running; (d) one negative control, as a commit that is then reverted: a value of the build host's environment compiled into the loader (`RUNNER_OS`, which GitHub sets to the host's kind). Each host's two builds then agree, and the value is no path and no linker's line, so each host's own gate passes; the two hosts' loaders differ, and the comparison must fail naming them. It is a fair stand-in for a new host-dependent input because it is one: something of the host reaching an image by a way no gate on one machine looks at. **Locally:** `cargo xtask check` on the last commit that changes code; the workflow parsed and its new job's steps read back; `install-rust.sh`'s new lines run on the development Mac. No boots: nothing that is built changes. |
| Actual deliverables | The job; the script's branch; the trial's results with the run's and job's numbers; the rows with a criterion of their own; the provenance entry. The trial's pull request is closed afterwards, and the keeper lands the branch as always. |
| Acceptance conditions | The trial shows (a) to (d). `cargo xtask check` passes on the last commit with code changes. The record says plainly what the job is not: one Linux host and one macOS host, both GitHub's; the macOS one the same kind of host as the development Mac, so no third kind; development commits, not a release candidate; unsigned images. **Rows, each with a criterion of its own from here** (proposed states go to the coordinating session with the evidence): S60-03, "Implement: Reproducible build checks": on every run of CI the gate's files are built twice from nothing on a Linux host and twice on a macOS host, and the run fails unless the unsigned images, which are everything a signature covers (ONBF-R9), are the same bytes in all four builds; a signature's own bytes are outside it, since ML-DSA signing is hedged with fresh randomness (FIPS 204 §3.4). S60-11, "Track … nondeterministic build inputs": every input found to differ between builds or between hosts is recorded, normalised and held by a gate, and a comparison across two kinds of host runs on every push, so that one not yet found fails CI. S75-06 and S75-07 take item 275 in "Decided by" and say what the job gives them and what a release candidate still asks. The coordinating session checks the result. |

**Result (2026-11-13).** The job is `6ef3023`; the trial ran in draft pull request 26, which is closed and was never merged. The full account is the ledger's §18 (`docs/research/2026-11-11-toolchain-path-in-images.md`).

- **(a)** Run 43341445989 (`7001b44`): every job passed. The macOS job (131613843125) found "all 22 files, from both builds, … the same bytes", with the development Mac's eleven digests.
- **(b)** The macOS job took 2 min 0 s and 2 min 42 s in its two runs, starting when `check` had passed (7 min 42 s and 9 min 22 s). The runner: `macos-26`, image `macos-26-arm64` 20261102.0473.1, "Apple M1 (Virtual), 3 processors, Darwin 25.6.0".
- **(c)** `xtask` ran on the runner in both runs, so its environment sets none of the variables `xtask` refuses.
- **(d)** Run 43355957632 (`6b83691`, the control): `check` passed (job 131657950161); the macOS job's own gate passed and its comparison failed (job 131661454789), naming the three loaders in both builds, 6 of 22, with the other 16 the same. Both hosts' loader digests had been built on the Mac beforehand and came out.
- **Where it differs from the contract,** by the coordinating session's word: the pull request was against `main` (the third batch had merged), and the control was a commit on a trial branch alone, deleted afterwards, in place of a commit and its revert here. This task's branch never held it.
- **Two things I got wrong on the way.** The control's first form, a `#[used]` static, was dropped by the linker and changed nothing; I had called it the control before building it. And I told the coordinating session that no gate reads history; the document gate does, and it was then run on both of the trial's commits.
- **The gate:** `cargo xtask check` passed on `6ef3023` on the development Mac (1,694 host tests, 0 failed, 27 ignored). The trial's merge of `main` changed no file (the same tree, `566ba614…`). No boots here: nothing that is built changed. CI's own boots passed in run 1; run 2's were cancelled by hand, not being needed.
- **What the trial does not show:** the comparison under Rust 1.100.0; rustup on the macOS runner installing 1.100.0; a third kind of host (the runner is an Apple-processor Mac, like the development machine); a release candidate; a signed image. One run of each kind is one run: nothing here says how often the macOS runner fails for reasons of its own.
- **Rows.** By the coordinating session's decision of 2026-11-13, S60-03, S60-11, S75-06 and S75-07 take their own criteria, item 275 and their states from the job's run under Rust 1.100.0, in TASKS item 52. They are not changed here.

(4) `cargo xtask check` passes on the branch's last commit. **Result (2026-11-13): built on the development Mac.** Nothing is pushed; the coordinating session reads it and forwards it to the batch's keeper.

- **The ledger:** 36 rows. 29 are owed; 7 carry a trend from CI run 43131676181, from QEMU's software processor and two KVM guests; none has a figure from a declared machine. ADR-0004, ADR-0006 and ADR-0009 are listed with "none" and the reason. CL16 (the per-call pin, ADR-0011 L7) and CL17 (L13's widening) take their figure from TASKS item 47 when it lands, and CL16's model takes that task's line on the out-of-line pin check.
- **What it found:** the largest named cost, IBPB at each change of process (CL28), has no figure anywhere. Two costs may be avoidable and no ADR has looked at either: IRETQ on every return from a system call (CL5), whose hazard E5's unmapped page may already exclude, and the walk of the tables before a user copy (CL36), which runs before a copy that already recovers from a fault. Two rows are boot time spent by proofs written for development (CL13, CL20), which ADR-0013's question 5 already settles before M9.

ADR-0017 Amendment 2, on pull request 28's branch, was read and would be held and pass. - **The plants in the tree,** on `a7b89f4`, one at a time, each run through `cargo xtask cost-gate` and undone with `git checkout` before anything was committed; each was refused (exit 1), the gate passed before and after, and the tree held only the uncommitted records throughout: ADR-0005 Amendment 9's statement removed; that statement in no form; a new accepted ADR-0019 with none; ADR-0011 Amendment 3's reference to CL17 removed; that reference changed to CL99; CL28 naming ADR-0017 Z33; CL33's environments without their backticks; ADR-0013's text naming CL77; ADR-0006 left out of the list; CL4 listed under ADR-0002.

On 2026-11-13 the coordinating session ruled that the cost statement names the row, as V8 says, that this one reference needs no owner item, and gave its words; they are in a commit of their own.

### S4-1. Rust in ring 3, with no ABI change (TASKS item 41)

Sent by the coordinating session on 2026-11-12. Written here on 2026-11-12, before any code. It is task S4-1 of ADR-0011 N14 step 4 (`docs/research/2026-11-11-n14-step4-scope.md`, section 4, whose exit tests were written before this), and it builds decision item 197 (ADR-0011 Amendment 2, L1; the note to ONBF §8.3).

| Field | |
|---|---|
| Task ID | S4-1, of ADR-0011 N14 step 4; TASKS item 41, on branch `claude/s4-1-rust-ring3-fde097`, cut from `land/s4` at `5ea97ef`. |
| Objective | Rust code runs in ring 3, with no change to any ABI. (1) CLAUDE.md §11's userspace build: `x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu` used only as a code generator, `no_std`, `-C target-cpu=x86-64-v3`, `panic=abort`, frame pointers, a PIE linked by `rust-lld` with the same `-z` options as the kernel. The flags live in a configuration file of their own, `.cargo/userspace.toml`, which xtask passes with `--config` to the ring-3 builds only: in `.cargo/config.toml` they would reach every host build on CI, whose host is that same triple. (2) A runtime crate, `runtime/rt` (`origin-rt`): `_start`, which takes the start record's address in RDI and calls the program's `main` with the record decoded by `origin-abi`; a panic handler that ends the thread with a fixed exit code; and `memcpy`, `memmove`, `memset`, `memcmp` and `bcmp`. (3) Programs carried in the kernel's image and described at build time (L1): xtask builds each program, then `onbf-pack carried` reads the linker's output with `onbf-pack`'s existing ELF reader (no new profile), refuses each of ONBF §6.1.2's hard errors, and writes the kernel-private description: each segment's bytes with every relocation's addend in place, its permissions and size, the entry, and the relocation slots. The kernel compiles it in. Nothing is parsed at run time. (4) A launch path in `launch.rs` that places a program at a random base from `origin_frame::random`, maps each segment with its own permissions (read-only after relocation for RELRO), applies each relocation through the child's frames before any thread exists, gives it a stack at another random place with an unmapped page on either side, places its start record as today and starts its first thread. (5) The first program, `tests/kernel/first-program`: a boot proof that links today's generated stubs for `origin.kernel` into itself and runs under A6, the system-call range being its one read-execute segment. **Not in this task:** `origin.core`, imports bound from it, the per-call pin or any change to the generator (S4-2); the EXE and LIB profiles and the Loader Service (M6); thread or process creation (S4-3, G2); any change to `origin.kernel.oidl`, `origin-abi`'s layouts or the start record. |
| Source ownership | This task's session, on `claude/s4-1-rust-ring3-fde097`. New files, owned by it: `runtime/rt/`; `runtime/kernel-stubs/` (its committed files hold no generated code: xtask generates today's full `origin.kernel` tree into the target directory from the definition at each build, so no committed crate holds a shim, which xtask's interface test requires); `tests/kernel/first-program/`; `.cargo/userspace.toml` (not `.cargo/config.toml`, as the ledger's row said: CI's host is the userspace triple, so a section for that triple in `config.toml` would reach every host build and test there; xtask passes this file with `--config` to the ring-3 builds alone; agreed by the coordinating session on 2026-11-12); `developer-tools/onbf-pack/src/carried.rs` and its tests; `developer-tools/xtask/src/ring3.rs`; `framekernel/src/program.rs` (the description's types, and the descriptions compiled in for the kernel's target); `framekernel/src/program_proof.rs` (the boot proof). Shared files it touches, and how far: `framekernel/src/launch.rs` (S4-1's until it lands: the program launch, added beside today's functions, which do not change); `framekernel/src/bootproof.rs` (one row, the count, and the tally widened to 128 bits in a commit of its own, since the new row is the 64th and fills today's 64-bit tally); `framekernel/src/sched.rs` (one call, inside the block that runs only with randomness); `framekernel/src/lib.rs` (two module lines); `developer-tools/onbf-pack/src/elf.rs` (one more §6.1.2 check, an undefined symbol, which KRN's conversion then also makes) and `main.rs` (the `carried` subcommand); xtask's `main.rs` (the kernel's builds and clippy steps get the programs' description; the boot markers; the verdict counts), `unsafety.rs` (a list of the ring-3 files that may hold `unsafe`), and the §11 lint's file list; the workspace `Cargo.toml` (members only: the ring-3 crates are not default members, so no host build reaches them); `docs/adr/ADR-0013-boot-proof-policy.md` (one Refuse row and its controls); `THREAT_MODEL_SYSCALLS.md` (the rows the launch path touches); STATUS, this file, `SOURCE_PROVENANCE.md` and the requirements rows it changes. It touches nothing under `framekernel/frame/`, `sdk/`, `services/` or `developer-tools/oidl/`, and neither `CLAUDE.md`, an ADR's decisions nor `OWNER_DECISIONS.md`. |
| Architecture contract | ADR-0011 Amendment 2, L1 (programs in the kernel's image, described at build time, nothing parsed at run time), L8 (proof processes keep A6's range) and L9; ADR-0005 P4, P6, A6, C4, C7, E2 and Amendment 8, G2 (the kernel is the only creator) and G6 (each program's limits written beside its image, the least it needs); ONBF §6.1 (segments, permissions, relocations), §6.1.1 (`panic=abort`, frame pointers), §6.1.2 (the hard errors and the determinism test) and the note to §8.3; ADR-0004 H1 (the lower half is user space); ADR-0016 (every random place comes from `origin_frame::random`; with no generator nothing is launched); ADR-0013 R1, R6 and R7; ADR-0011 N6 and N13 (the kernel's `unsafe` stays in `framekernel/frame/`; the launch path is safe code); CLAUDE.md §11, §13, §14 and §75. The program layout chosen here (bases and stacks at random pages of `[0x1000_0000, 0x1000_0000_0000)`, below the start records' region) is the kernel's own until the Loader Service decides it (M6), and is not an interface. |
| Relevant specifications | The ELF gABI and the x86-64 psABI as `onbf-pack`'s reader already cites them (program headers, the dynamic section, `R_X86_64_RELATIVE`, the symbol table's `SHN_UNDEF`, `DT_HASH`'s chain count); the psABI's process entry (RSP 16-byte aligned at `_start`, no return address), which `_start` aligns again rather than trusts. Read in the repository's existing citations only: no new external document, and no other system's source (CLAUDE.md §3). |
| Required dependencies | `land/s4` at `5ea97ef` (S4-0's 64-bit handles, `user-abi` 0.7); candidate 1 accepted (item 197); `onbf-pack`'s ELF reader; the generator as it is today (`oidl generate`, full mode); `origin-abi`'s `start::decode`; `origin_frame::random`. No new crate from outside the workspace (CLAUDE.md §5). |
| Security invariants | (1) No segment is ever both writable and executable, at any moment: each page is mapped once with its final permissions, and the bytes and relocations are written through its frame before the first thread exists. (2) RELRO pages are read-only to the program from the start. (3) The base and the stack are drawn from `origin_frame::random`; with no generator the launch is refused and nothing is placed at a predictable address. (4) The system-call range is exactly the program's one read-execute segment at its base (A6); a description with no read-execute segment, or more than one, is refused. (5) A relocation slot outside a writable or RELRO segment's file-backed bytes, misaligned, or one whose value plus the base overflows, refuses the launch; nothing in the launch path indexes by a value it has not bounds-checked, and none of it can panic. (6) A refused launch gives back every frame and handle it took (today's launch rule). (7) No `unsafe` in the kernel outside `framekernel/frame/`: `launch.rs`, `program.rs` and `program_proof.rs` forbid it. In ring 3, `unsafe` only in the files the gate lists: the runtime's entry and its `mem*` functions, and the proof's deliberate faults; every other ring-3 file holds none, and each ring-3 crate root denies it. (8) No ring-3 crate asks whether its target is Linux or Unix (§11's lint, which then covers `.cargo/userspace.toml` too). (9) The description is the same bytes from the same ELF. (10) Nothing the program reports is trusted by the kernel beyond the boot proof's own comparison. |
| Performance constraints | None set (§53, item 115). The launch of the first program is timed from the first frame mapped to the thread started, and printed as a QEMU figure beside today's launch figure, with no budget. |
| Required tests | The ledger's exit tests for S4-1. **Host:** `onbf-pack carried` refuses each of ONBF §6.1.2's hard errors, one fixture each: a writable and executable segment, a text relocation, `PT_INTERP`, a non-empty `.init_array`, an undefined symbol, and a relocation type other than RELATIVE (with GLOB_DAT and JUMP_SLOT also refused in S4-1, since there is nothing to bind until S4-2); it gives the same bytes twice from the same input; a description's addends land in its bytes and its slots are exactly the reader's; the program's real ELF converts. The launch path's page building and relocation arithmetic, on the host, against hand-made descriptions, including each refusal of invariant (5). §11's lint passes over the new crates and fails on one planted `cfg(target_os = "linux")` in them. The `unsafe` gate fails on one planted `unsafe` in an unlisted ring-3 file. **Boot (one new Refuse result, "a Rust program in ring 3"):** the program runs in ring 3 at a random base, and two launches' bases differ, and their stacks; it reads its start record from RDI, header and entries, and reports them, and the kernel compares them with what it wrote; it uses a handle from the record through the generated stubs (a message on BOOTSTRAP that names it as the writer); it reads data only through relocated pointers and reports what it read; every relocated slot in its pages holds its addend plus the base; its code is read-execute and its data writable and never executable (a write to its code and a jump into its data each end it with a page fault at that address, with the error code's write and instruction-fetch bits); a panic ends it with the runtime's exit code; a normal run ends with its own exit code; every process and all they held are gone afterwards. **Negative controls**, each a temporary edit run once and put back byte for byte: a program linked with a writable code segment is refused at build; a launch path that skips one relocation fails the boot line; a fixed base fails "two launches differ". |
| Actual deliverables | The build, the three crates, the conversion, the description compiled into the kernel, the launch path, the proof and its row, the gates' new lists, and the records. Small commits that each build. Nothing pushed. |
| Acceptance conditions | On the task's final commit: `cargo xtask check`, `cargo xtask boot-test` and `cargo xtask boot-matrix` pass, every boot run as `cargo xtask exclusive -- <command>`; every exit test above passes and every negative control fails the boot or the build; STATUS, this file, provenance, the requirements rows and the threat-model rows touched are current. The report names exactly what needs independent review: the launch path's relocation and mapping code, the runtime's entry and `mem*` functions, and the conversion's new check. Nothing here runs on physical hardware: QEMU on the development Mac only. The matrix rows it moves (named after TASKS item 25's template, 2026-11-12): S11-03 and A11-L1 to `built`, S11-04 and A05-G6 to `partial`; none to `done`, since their scope is hardware or their evidence QEMU's. |

### S4-2. `origin.core` and the per-call pin (TASKS item 47)

Sent by the coordinating session on 2026-11-13. Written here on 2026-11-13, before any code. It is task S4-2 of ADR-0011 N14 step 4 (`docs/research/2026-11-11-n14-step4-scope.md`, section 4, whose exit tests were written before this), and it builds decision items 198 to 205 (ADR-0011 Amendment 2, L2 to L8; ADR-0005 Amendment 8, G1) and items 272 and 273 (ADR-0005 Amendment 9, H1; ADR-0011 Amendment 3, L13), with item 274 (L14) recorded where the runtime states its exit codes. It closes row E8 of the system-call threat model for every process that is not one of the kernel's proof processes.

Two scratch probes were made before this was written, outside the repository, with the pinned compiler (`rustc 1.99.1 (58cf8f9c3 2026-10-13)`, LLVM 23.1.1, host `aarch64-apple-darwin`, for `x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu`). They show that the plan below can be built; they prove nothing about what is built. (1) Two functions written with `#[unsafe(naked)]` and `naked_asm!` compiled on stable and linked with `rust-lld` as a shared object: its code held the two stubs and nothing else (the crate's panic handler was discarded), it had no relocation, and its only writable bytes were the linker's own dynamic table. (2) A position-independent program linked against it held no `syscall`, called each import through a slot of its own in its read-only-after-relocation data, and named each import in one `R_X86_64_GLOB_DAT` relocation.

| Field | |
|---|---|
| Task ID | S4-2, of ADR-0011 N14 step 4; TASKS item 47, on branch `claude/dreamy-bose-c5c3e3`. The worktree was cut from `main` at `2bb4388`; on the coordinating session's word it was fast-forwarded to `land/s4` at `e1b5c33`, and `docs/decisions-271-275` at `c03e574` was merged in (`1ed35f4`), with the two rows that merge adds placed in the roadmap as proposed (`3e2dbc0`). |
| Objective | A program that is not one of the kernel's proof processes reaches the kernel only through `origin.core`, and each call number is accepted only from its own stub. (1) **The generator** writes each entry point of a `syscall` library as a naked function, once, in a tree of its own: the L13 extension of every parameter narrower than 64 bits, in parameter order (zero for an unsigned integer, a `bool` and a bits type; the sign for a signed integer; an enum as its underlying integer, zero where that is unsigned and the sign where it is signed: corrected on 2026-11-13 to L13's sentence as decision item 277 words it, which the owner accepted that day; this contract first said zero for an enum, as L13 then did); the fourth argument from RCX to R10, extended on the way where it is narrow; the load of its own number; one `syscall`; and a return, or `ud2` for a method that never returns. It also writes, for the kernel, the pin as comparisons, and a list of the entry points with each one's number and the minor its method arrived in. (2) **`origin.core`** is a new crate, `runtime/core`, that holds the generated stubs and nothing else. xtask links it once a build as a shared object named `origin.core`, and `onbf-pack` describes it for the kernel: its code, its exports by name with each one's minor, the offset of each call's `syscall`, and the release fingerprint of the definition it was generated from. A description is refused if the image has any relocation, any writable byte besides the linker's dynamic table, more or less than one read-execute segment, an export the list does not name or a missing one, or anything but one `syscall` in each stub and none elsewhere. (3) **The kernel** makes one set of frames for `origin.core` at boot, written once, and maps them read-execute into every process that is pinned per call, at a random place, once, before its first thread; it records that place for the process as it maps it. A call from such a process is accepted only when the address of its `syscall` instruction is the recorded base plus the offset the build gives for its number; any other call, and any number no stub has, stops the thread as a fault does, with a record of the number and the address, and no status comes back. The kernel's proof processes keep ADR-0005 A6's range and its `access_denied`. (4) **The launch path** binds each carried program's imports: every import slot gets the address of the export it names, written through the frame of a page that is read-only to the program from the start. (5) **`origin-rt` and the first program** move onto `origin.core`, and a second carried program, the pin proof's, is added. (6) **Two gates** in `cargo xtask check` read what was linked. (7) **H1**: a program reads RFLAGS as the first instruction of its first thread, and a boot proof judges the eight bits H1 names. **Not in this task:** ADR-0005 P8's helper and anything else in `origin.core` (L4); the EXE and LIB profiles, sealed objects, the Code Integrity Table and the Loader Service (M6); thread or process creation (S4-3, G2); the exception channel (P10: a stopped thread ends as a faulting thread ends today); any change to `origin.kernel.oidl`, to a call number, a C signature, a wire format, `origin-abi`'s layouts or the start record; moving the kernel's assembly proof programs onto `origin.core` (L8); a CET shadow stack (row E11); N8's measurement (S4-6). |
