Neither `CLAUDE.md`, an ADR's decisions nor `OWNER_DECISIONS.md` is touched. |
| Architecture contract | ADR-0011 N5, N6, N13 and Amendment 2, L1 to L9, and Amendment 3, L13 and L14; ADR-0005 P4, P6, P10 and P12, A5, A6, B2, C4, C7, D3, D5, E1, E2, Amendment 8 G1, G2 and G6, and Amendment 9 H1; `docs/spec/OIDL.md` §10 (registers, register decoding, the C entry points), §11, §13 (the release fingerprint) and §18; `docs/spec/ONBF.md` §6.1 (imports bound eagerly, full RELRO, "syscalls are not ABI"), §6.1.2 (the hard errors), §8.2 and the note to §8.3; ADR-0004 H1; ADR-0013 R1, R6 and R7; ADR-0016 R8 (every random place from `origin_frame::random`; with no generator nothing is launched); ADR-0017 Z9 and Z10 (the pin is comparisons, with no table indexed by the number; the image gate's rule on jump tables holds for it); ADR-0018 V1, V3, V4 and V7; CLAUDE.md 7.7 §3, §10, §11, §13, §14, §17, §42, §48, §75 and §78. **No ABI or format decision is made here.** What this task chooses, each the kernel's or the build's own and none an interface: the form of the two kernel-private descriptions; where `origin.core` goes (a random page of the programs' region, `[0x1000_0000, 0x1000_0000_0000)`, clear of the program and its stack, until the Loader Service decides it at M6); the record kept of a stopped call (kernel-internal, read by the boot proof, until P10's exception channel exists); the names of the generator's outputs. **Three readings this contract makes, put to the coordinating session with it:** (a) "a carried program holds a `syscall`" means an instruction in the linear disassembly of its code; a `0F 05` byte pair inside another instruction is counted and printed, not refused, since a jump into one is exactly what the pin stops and a byte scan would fail a correct program by chance. In `origin.core` itself the byte pair is the rule: exactly one for each stub, and none anywhere else. (b) The gate excepts the kernel's proof programs by name (L8), and for each name it holds the exact number of `syscall` instructions that program is meant to have: the pin proof's program has one, in the function that makes the forbidden call, so a stub inlined into it is still caught. (c) H1's proof is a boot result of its own, Refuse by ADR-0013 R1(b): it is a register read once, with no measurement, and every program's string instructions rest on the direction flag. Whether the owner must choose that class is the coordinating session's to say. `origin-rt`'s `_start` stops clearing the direction flag, which Amendment 9 leaves to its builder: H1 gives it, and the new proof holds H1. |
| Relevant specifications | The System V x86-64 psABI, version 1.0, at commit `e1ce0983`, as `docs/research/2026-11-12-psabi-revision.md` records its rows R1, R2 and R5 (read by the coordinating session on 2026-11-12; this session does not open the document). The ELF gABI and the psABI's relocation types as `onbf-pack`'s reader already cites them, with `R_X86_64_GLOB_DAT`, `R_X86_64_JUMP_SLOT`, `DT_NEEDED` and `DT_SONAME` read from the same documents on the day the reader is changed, and recorded in provenance with the date (CLAUDE.md §16). Intel's SDM (Volume 1's section on the flags register, and Volume 2's entries for SYSCALL, MOVZX, MOVSX, MOVSXD, MOV and PUSHFQ) and AMD's APM Volume 3, at the revisions current on the day they are read, recorded with the date: for the two bytes of `syscall` the description and the gate look for, the forms the stubs use, and the bit numbers H1 names. The Rust reference's text on naked functions at 1.99.1, read before the stubs are written, with what it says recorded. **No source of any other operating system is opened** (CLAUDE.md §3, decision item 92): the ledger records what an earlier session learned from the published pages on Zircon's vDSO and OpenBSD's `pinsyscalls`, and a gap there goes to the coordinating session. |
| Required dependencies | `land/s4` at `e1b5c33` (S4-0's 64-bit handles and S4-1's build, runtime, description and launch path, with the answers to its review); `docs/decisions-271-275` at `c03e574` (CLAUDE.md 7.7; items 271 to 274). Candidates 2 to 9 accepted (items 198 to 205). The pinned toolchain's `rust-lld` and `llvm-objdump`, as the existing gates use them. No crate from outside the workspace (CLAUDE.md §5); xtask gains the first-party `oidl` library. No owner answer is awaited to start; the three readings above, and the roadmap placement of A05-H1 and A11-L13, are with the coordinating session. |
| Security invariants | (1) **One `syscall` a number.** `origin.core`'s linked code holds exactly one `syscall` for each method of `origin.kernel`, inside that method's stub, and no other `0F 05` byte pair anywhere; the description is refused otherwise, and the gate checks it again from the disassembly. (2) **Each stub is its own.** Before its `syscall` a stub holds only the L13 extensions of its own narrow parameters, the move of the fourth argument, and the load of its own number, in that order; the gate works out that sequence from the definition and compares it with what was linked, instruction for instruction. (3) **The kernel's offsets are the image's.** The offset the kernel compares for a number is the offset of that number's `syscall` in the linked image the kernel carries: the description takes it from the image's bytes, the gate from the disassembly, and the two must agree; the kernel does not compile if the release fingerprint of `origin.core`'s definition is not its own dispatcher's. (4) **Nothing but comparisons.** The pin is a `match` on the number whose arms compare the offset with a constant; no table is indexed by the number or by the address (Z9, Z10). The return address is compared and never dereferenced. (5) **Fixed when made.** A process is pinned per call or holds an A6 range from its creation, and never changes. A pinned process whose base is not recorded has no call accepted. The base is recorded once, by the code that maps the image, and a second mapping is refused. (6) **A violation ends the thread.** A call from a pinned process that fails the pin, or names a number no stub has, returns no status: the thread ends as a faulting thread does, and its ending records the number and the address of the instruction. A proof process keeps `access_denied` and `not_supported`. (7) **`origin.core` is never writable.** Its frames are written once, at boot, before any mapping of them exists; every mapping is read-execute, and the framework's count refuses a writable mapping of a frame that is executable anywhere. It is no mapping `memory_unmap` or `memory_protect` can name, and a fixed `memory_map` over it is `invalid_args` (L2, D3, D5). (8) **One set of frames.** Every pinned process maps the same frames; none is copied. (9) **A random place.** `origin.core`'s base in each process is drawn from `origin_frame::random`; with no generator nothing is launched. (10) **Imports are bound by the kernel and read-only.** An import slot lies in the bytes of a read-only-after-relocation segment, 8-byte aligned, and is no relocation slot; it names an export that exists; it is written through its frame before the first thread, and its page is never writable to the program. A description that breaks any of this is refused before anything is mapped. A carried program's bytes hold no call number and no offset of `origin.core`. (11) **Every export is a stub.** `origin.core` exports exactly `origin_kernel_<method>` for each method, each with its minor, and nothing else. (12) **No `syscall` elsewhere.** No carried program holds a `syscall` instruction, but for the proof programs the gate names, each with its exact count. (13) **`unsafe` stays where the gates list it.** In the kernel, none outside `framekernel/frame/`: `core_image.rs`, `launch.rs`, `process.rs`, `program.rs`, `syscall.rs` and the proofs forbid it. In ring 3, the generated stubs (one `#[unsafe(naked)]` each), the client's generated imports, and the pin program's two listed files; every assembly block states why its options hold, and none declares `preserves_flags` unless every instruction in it leaves the flags alone. (14) **Nothing is trusted from ring 3** beyond the proofs' own comparisons; the launch path cannot panic on any description. (15) **H1.** A thread's first instruction finds DF, CF, PF, AF, ZF, SF and OF clear and IF set. |
| Performance constraints | None set (CLAUDE.md §53, decision item 115; ADR-0018 V3 and V6). Reported, as figures under QEMU on the development Mac and, when the batch runs there, as KVM guests in CI, each a trend and judged by nothing: the cost of one call through `origin.core` (a loop of calls that answer `bad_handle`, timed by two `clock_read`s in ring 3), as built and again with the pin check taken out of an exported copy (V7's pair; the same build that is the first negative control); the instructions L13 adds, counted from the definition for every stub and for the stub that is timed; `origin.core`'s size in bytes and pages; and the time to load and start a program, as S4-1 prints it, which now includes mapping `origin.core` and binding the imports. The pin's cost model, for the ledger row ADR-0018 V5 asks for: one load of the process's base, one subtraction, and at most as many compare-and-branch pairs as there are methods before the dispatcher's own. |
| Required tests | The ledger's exit tests for S4-2, all of them, and the two the coordinating session added. **Host, the generator:** for every method of `origin.kernel` the generated stub, compiled for the userspace triple and disassembled, is the sequence invariant (2) describes, with one `syscall` and its own number; a definition with one parameter of each narrow type gives each its own extension, in each of the six registers, the fourth through R10; a `never` method ends in `ud2`; the client tree holds no stub and no `syscall`. The pin: every number is accepted at its own offset and at no other number's, and an unknown number at none. The list of entry points names every method once, with its number and minor. **Host, the description (`onbf-pack corelib`):** a real linked `origin.core` converts, twice to the same bytes; each refusal of invariant (1), (3) and (11) and of "nothing to relocate, no writable data" has a fixture: a second `syscall` in one stub, a `0F 05` pair outside every stub, a stub with none, a relocation, a writable segment with bytes, two read-execute segments, an export the list lacks, a listed export missing, a release fingerprint that is not the list's. **Host, `onbf-pack carried`:** a program linked against `origin.core` converts, and each import's slot and export are the linker's; an import of a symbol `origin.core` does not export, a `DT_NEEDED` that is not `origin.core`, an import slot outside RELRO, and any `GLOB_DAT` or `JUMP_SLOT` when no library is named are each refused; the KRN conversion still refuses every import. **Host, the kernel's arithmetic:** `program::check` refuses each breach of invariant (10); `fill_page` writes the export's address into an import slot and nothing else there; the core image's own checks refuse a pin offset outside the code and an export outside it. **Host, the gates (`corestubs.rs`):** against listings and bytes made by hand, the `origin.core` gate refuses a second `syscall` for one number, a `syscall` at an offset the kernel's table does not hold, a stub whose number load is not its own, an instruction before the `syscall` that is neither an L13 extension of that method nor the R10 move, and an extension that is missing; the carried-program gate refuses any `syscall` in a program not excepted and a count other than the stated one in a program that is. **Boot, a new Refuse result, "origin.core and the call pin"** (`ipc_proof::prove_core_pin`), by the pin program, a script interpreter in Rust that makes each step through the import of its number: (a) a script that makes every one of `origin.kernel`'s calls through `origin.core` gets the status each must give; (b) the hostile scripts of "IPC and handles" (`hostile`, 124 calls) and of the memory calls (`hostile_memory`) run again through the stubs, and each call gives the status it gives today; a step whose status differs is named, and only L13's reason is accepted (below). The hostile steps of the events-and-ports proof are spread over several processes the kernel arranges by hand and are not run again here; (a) covers those calls with good arguments, and that limit is recorded; (c) a `syscall` instruction in the program's own code stops the thread, and its ending records that number and that address; (d) a jump to call A's `syscall` with call B's number in RAX stops the thread, and so does a number no stub has, each with its record; (e) a write to a page of `origin.core` ends the thread with a page fault at that address, a write to a present user page; `memory_unmap` and `memory_protect` of its range answer `invalid_args`, a fixed `memory_map` over it answers `invalid_args`, and its pages are then still mapped read-execute on the same frames; (f) two processes hold `origin.core` at different bases, each page of both on the same frame, the frames executable in two spaces and writable in none; making the image at boot took exactly its pages from the free frames, and no launch takes one more for it; (g) every import slot of each launched program holds the address of its export, read back through the child's tables before its thread starts, on a page mapped read-only; (h) L13: through the C entry point, a call whose narrow parameter has other bits set above it succeeds as the same call with them clear, for an unsigned, a bits and an enum parameter, and the same register values in a direct call by a proof process under A6 are `invalid_args`; (i) a proof process's call from outside its range is still `access_denied`, and its unknown number still `not_supported` (L8); (j) the cost figures; (k) every process and all they held are gone afterwards, and the heap and the free frames are where they were, `origin.core`'s own frames staying. **Boot, a second new result, "the flags at a thread's entry"** (Refuse; reading (c) above): the pin program's entry is one it supplies itself, whose first instruction pushes RFLAGS; the proof finds DF and the six status flags clear and IF set in what it reports. "A Rust program in ring 3" passes unchanged in its clauses, with the first program pinned and bound. **Negative controls**, each a temporary edit, run once and put back byte for byte, with what failed recorded in ADR-0013: (1) the pin check left out: lines (c) and (d) fail; (2) the base recorded one page off: every call stops, and the boot fails; (3) a stub in today's `asm!` form, inlined into a carried program: the carried-program gate fails the build; (4) A6's range kept for a pinned process, over `origin.core`'s code: line (d)'s jump fails; (5) a stub generated without its L13 extension: line (h) fails; (6) a thread started with the direction flag set (`NEW_RFLAGS` in the framework's `stack.rs`, a temporary edit of a file this task does not otherwise change): the flags result fails; (7) an import slot left unbound: line (g) fails; (8) `origin.core` mapped from frames of the process's own: line (f) fails. Each host refusal above is its own control: the fixture is the planted mistake. |
| Actual deliverables | The generator's new outputs and its tests; `runtime/core`, `runtime/kernel-client` and `tests/kernel/pin-program`; `onbf-pack corelib` and the imports in `onbf-pack carried`; the build order and the two gates in xtask; the kernel's core image, pin, binding and proofs; OIDL.md §10, §11 and §18 revised to what is built; ADR-0013's rows and controls; the threat model's rows; STATUS, this file, provenance (a table row for each new crate) and the matrix rows; a hand-over for the independent review. Small commits that each build. Nothing pushed, and no commit to `land/s4` or `main`. **Size, and a proposal:** this is the generator, a new image, two conversions, two gates, the launch path, the system-call entry and two proofs. It stays one branch, since the halves cannot be gated apart for long: a program cannot link without `origin.core`, and the pin cannot be shown without a program bound to it. The proposal is two review points on it. **R1, the build half:** the generator, `origin.core`'s image, its description and its gate, with the first program still under A6 and the naked stubs linked into it from `runtime/core` as a library for that stretch only; `check`, `boot-test` and `boot-matrix` pass there, and line (h)'s first half can already be shown. **R2, the kernel half:** the shared frames, the mapping, the binding, the pin, the programs moved over, the second gate and the proofs. If the coordinating session prefers one review at the end, the commits are the same and R1 is only a marked commit. |
| Acceptance conditions | On the last commit that changes code: `cargo xtask check`, `cargo xtask boot-test` and `cargo xtask boot-matrix` pass on the development Mac, every boot run as `cargo xtask exclusive -- <command>`; every exit test above passes; every negative control failed where it was aimed and is recorded; then records-only commits. The hand-over names what the reviewer must read, each invariant above with the file and the test that holds it, what the author is least sure of, and what rests on reading alone. An independent review, arranged by the coordinating session, has been answered on this branch: each finding with a commit, a recorded limit or a reason. **Not shown by this task:** anything on a physical machine or under KVM (QEMU's software processor on the development Mac only; CI's guests are the keeper's run); that a naked function can never be duplicated by the compiler (the gate holds the rule either way); what a processor does on a wrongly guessed pin comparison (row I2 stays "Partly", L9); a refusal of the launch with no generator, which no boot reaches. **Matrix rows, each with a criterion of its own, none to `done`, since their scope is hardware and the evidence is QEMU's:** **A11-L2** to `built`: one image a build, described with no relocation and no writable byte; in two processes its pages are the same frames at different bases, read-execute, and `memory_unmap`, `memory_protect` and a fixed `memory_map` over it answer `invalid_args` and leave it mapped. **A11-L3** to `built`: every import slot of a launched program holds its export's address before its thread starts, on a page read-only to it, and a slot left unbound fails the boot. **A11-L4** to `built`: the linked image's code is the generated stubs and padding, and the description is refused for anything else. **A11-L5** to `partial`: the exports are exactly the generated entry points, each with its minor, and the gate refuses one more or one fewer; `user-abi` is at major 0, so nothing is frozen, and no EXE or LIB image carries the version yet (M6). **A11-L6** to `built`: each stub is a naked function, and the gate finds one `syscall` for each method in the linked image, at the offset the kernel holds, after the load of its own number, and none in a carried program that is not an excepted proof. **A11-L7** to `built`: the offsets the kernel compares are read from the linked image by the build, the kernel does not compile against another definition's fingerprint, and a base recorded one page off stops every call. **A11-L8** to `built`: a pinned process's call from anywhere but its stub stops its thread while a proof process's is `access_denied`, and A6's range kept for a pinned process fails the boot. **A11-L13** to `built`: through the C entry point a narrow parameter with bits set above it is taken as the same call with them clear, a direct call with that register is `invalid_args`, and a stub generated without the extension fails the boot. **A11-L14** (a rule): `origin-rt` documents its three exit codes as its own convention, with no code reserved; its state changes only if the gate's rules for a `rule` row allow one here. **A05-B2** to `built`: lines (c) and (d) pass, and with the pin check left out they fail. **A05-G1** to `built`: a refused call returns no status, the thread's ending records the number and the address, and a number no stub has ends it the same way. **A05-H1** from `not-verified` to `built`: the criterion the row already has, shown under QEMU. **A11-N5** to `partial`: every carried program that is not a proof reaches the kernel through `origin.core` alone and is pinned per call; the kernel's proof processes are not, by L8. **A11-N13**: stays `partial`, with one more of its gates in place in the form the build can check (no `syscall` instruction in a carried program's image but `origin.core`'s); its source-level form, over crates, is still owed. **A05-A6**: its limitation now says which processes keep the range. Rows of the directive that this touches (the userspace build, private call numbers) keep their state unless the gate shows otherwise; any that moves is named in the result. |

**Result (2026-11-13): built and gated on the development Mac; not landed. Both reviews, of the build half and of the kernel half, are answered.** The evidence is in `SOURCE_PROVENANCE.md`, under "Task S4-2", and the controls in ADR-0013 (CP-1 to CP-13). Under Rust 1.100.0, on `b1b25c1`, the merge of `main` at `803755c` into code as at `56c0dc0`: `cargo xtask check` passed in 478 s (1,783 host tests passed, none failed, 27 ignored; the image gate's pin rule, the names gate, both gates of `core-gate`, the reproducible-build gate and Loom among its steps); `cargo xtask boot-test` passed 11 of 11 in 146 s; `cargo xtask boot-matrix` passed 124 scenarios with none failed in 2,676 s, every kernel boot with a generator ending 65 of 66 and the one without ending "58 of 60; 6 did not run". Under Rust 1.99.1, on `88fcfcc`, the same three passed. Every exit test of the contract passed, and every negative control failed where it was aimed. Where it differs from the contract above:

- **The install at boot came with the build half** (`cdd2e30`), not the kernel half: the reproducible-build gate requires that the kernel image hold the bytes of everything it carries, and a description used only at compile time left `origin.core`'s code out.
- **`.cargo/userspace.toml` no longer names an entry** (`ac22d57`). It reached `origin.core` too, which has none. A program's entry is the linker's default, `_start`, and the pin program is given its own by `xtask`.
- **`R_X86_64_JUMP_SLOT`, `DT_JMPREL` and an `R_X86_64_64` against an import are refused, not read.** The userspace flags bind at link time, so the linker writes only `R_X86_64_GLOB_DAT` slots. The last was found building the pin program: a table of the entry points' addresses needs it, so a program that keeps an entry point's address in data does not build.
- **The release fingerprint is checked where the kernel is compiled.** The linked `origin.core` carries none; the generator's list beside it does, the description takes it from there, and `core_image::check` compares it. The contract's fixture "a release fingerprint that is not the list's" is that function's host test.
- **L13 and enumerations.** The contract, like L13's sentence then, said a narrow enumeration is zero-extended. The generator extends it as its underlying integer, which is what the decoder accepts for a signed one. `origin.kernel` passes none by register. The coordinating session put it to the owner as decision item 277, accepted on 2026-11-13; the contract's objective (1) and OIDL §10 now carry the accepted sentence, and a test definition with enumerations over `i8`, `i16` and `i32`, each with a member below zero, shows the sign extension on assembled stubs.
- **Line (h) at boot shows bits types only.** `origin.kernel` has six narrow register parameters, all 32-bit bits types, in the second, third and fourth registers. The contract named an unsigned and an enum parameter too; those, and the other registers, are shown on the host, on stubs assembled from a test definition.
- **Line (b) leaves one step out.** The hostile IPC script's step that asks a call number no method has cannot be made through a stub, so 123 of its 124 calls are run. Line (d) covers that number.
- **Reading (a) went further than the contract**, on the coordinating session's conditions: the gate on carried programs fails on bytes that do not decode, and places every `0F 05` pair of the carried code against the listing, so a pair in bytes the listing does not cover fails too.
- **The proof's order is the controls'.** The binding is judged first and the cost printed before the pin's clauses (`ecc86d5`), and the binding clause judges slots alone while the image clause judges the frames of three processes (`ce970a8`). The second came from control CP-8 failing one clause early.
- **Control 6 edits the user arm of the first frame**, not `NEW_RFLAGS` itself, which the kernel's own threads start from too. It has three forms, since a set direction flag now fails the first program before the flags' proof is reached.
- **The cost pair resolves nothing** (ADR-0013): boots of one build differ by more than the two builds do.
- **Clause (c) judges the exact address** since `aef9f47`: the pin program reports where its own `syscall` is, and the ending must name that address. Until then it accepted any address in the program's code (the review of the kernel half, K3 and S3).
- **Clause (f)'s "making the image at boot took exactly its pages from the free frames" is no longer judged** since `cca0faa`: the frames' records are on the heap, which may take a frame of its own, and other processors run meanwhile, so the count could fail a correct install. The install's read-back of every word judges it instead, and the fall in free frames is printed as a figure (the review of the build half, F2; of the kernel half, S2).
- **Clause (i)'s "still `access_denied`" is shown as far as a process outside its range can show it**: the boot shows that the call comes back and the thread goes on to its own fault; that the status is `access_denied` is read from `syscall.rs`, since such a process cannot report what it got (S2).
- **Invariant (10)'s "a carried program's bytes hold no call number" is true of the first program and not of the pin program**, on purpose: its scripts are written in call numbers, and it maps each to its import. The names gate in `cargo xtask check` lists that one ring-3 file as the only one that may name `origin_abi::syscall` (the review of the kernel half, K12 and S4).
- **Invariants (4) to (6) are held by one host-tested function** since `aef9f47`: `pin::judge`, with `CallOrigin` and its one change, `mapped_at`; `syscall::run` asks it through `pin_verdict`, which is never inlined so that the image gate can read the pin as compiled (`c676eef`), at the cost of one direct call and its return a system call (K2, K4).
- **Who may make a range process is a gate**, not a convention: `Process::proof` and `Process::proof_child_of`, renamed from `new` and `child_of`, may be named only in the proof files the names gate lists (`3a078b9`; K5).
- **The kernel's own write path refuses `origin.core`'s frames** (`Process::write_page`, named `write_frame` until `56c0dc0`; `aef9f47`), and the names gate lets the framework's raw `write_frame`, which does not refuse them, be named only in the wrapper's file and the address-space proofs (`56c0dc0`); the framework's own rule is TASKS item 51 (K1).
- **The pin is kept out of line, at a cost stated as "Unmeasured, owed"** (ADR-0018 V3): one direct call and one return a system call, so that the image gate can read it; ADR-0013 gives the model and what would lift it (K4).
- **An import carries its export's name, and each pin must lie in its own method's stub** (`ffb4cbf`; K6).
- **One rule of the description was too weak, and its fixture wrong** (`c60d338`). `onbf-pack corelib` refused writable data that had bytes in the file, and passed a writable segment of zero-filled memory, which is what a `static mut` that starts at zero makes; a fixture called that the linker's padding, which the real linker puts elsewhere. Found by reading that check again after naming it, in the build half's hand-over, as the one its author was least sure of. Now refused, on a fixture and on a library linked by the pinned toolchain.
- **No framework change was needed.** Shared frames, their mapping as a run and the count of writable and executable mappings were S3-5b's.
- **Matrix rows:** as the contract says, with A11-L4, L5 and L6 at `host` (their evidence is the gate and host tests), and A11-L14 left `not-started`: it is a rule that reserves nothing, the runtime's text states it, and nothing checks it.
- **Not done here, by the coordinating session's rulings:** a trap after a stub's `ret` (ADR-0017 holds the kernel to `harden-sls-ret` and says nothing of ring 3; TASKS item 49); a rule in the framework against writing a frame that is executable anywhere (TASKS item 51); `image_inputs::remove_linked` and a `cdylib`'s second copy under `deps/` (forwarded to the session that owns the file). The roadmap placement of A05-H1 and A11-L13 was accepted as decision item 276; `main` at `4097413`, merged into this branch at `e0622a0`, carries the accepted paragraph. TASKS items 49 and 51 are the coordinating session's numbers, and their entries are its to write: this file does not hold them yet.

**The review of the build half and its answers (2026-11-13).** One AI reviewer, started by the coordinating session, read `255c1b3..ac22d57` from an export and from built bytes: `docs/engineering/REVIEW_S4-2-R1_2026-11-13.md`. No defect that lets anything but the stubs into `origin.core` or gives the kernel another call's offset; one medium finding, seven low, nine informational, fifteen records. Each is answered in the report's "Resolution", with its commit. What changed:

- **The install** (F1, medium; F2; F11): it reads every word back, fills the rest of the page with the linker's `int3`, is judged by what it returns, and its failure line fails the boot test; the proof's last clause reads the frames again at its end. Controls CP-9 and CP-10.
- **`onbf-pack`** (F3, F6, F14, F17): the one writable segment is the dynamic table with zero fill to the end of its page, by the coordinating session's reading of L2, and nothing follows its `DT_NULL`; four fixtures; a program with no `_start` and a program with no `DT_NEEDED` are refused.
- **The generator** (F4, F10, F12, F13): call number 1295 is refused where the definition is read; the list of exports is in number order; an enumeration over a signed integer is shown sign-extended (decision item 277).
- **The gate and the build** (F5, F9, F13): the gate is seen to refuse a really linked image with a planted fault; the build refuses a second `syscall` interface with its reason; the gate's limit (spellings, not bytes) is recorded.
- **Recorded, not built**, by ruling: F7 (TASKS item 51), the trap after a stub's `ret` (TASKS item 49), F16.

**The review of the kernel half and its answers (2026-11-13).** One AI reviewer, not the build half's, started by the coordinating session, read `ac22d57..f6f0fce` from an export and from a kernel built from it: `docs/engineering/REVIEW_S4-2-R2_2026-11-13.md`. No way found for a launched program to have a call accepted from anywhere but that call's own `syscall`, nor to write, unmap, re-protect or map over the image, nor to change its kind; in the built kernel the pin is compares alone. No high or medium finding; six low (K1 to K6), seven informational (K7 to K13), nine records. Each is answered in the report's "Resolution", with its commit or its control (CP-11 to CP-13); the coordinating session ruled on the five questions and reads the answers itself, with no third reviewer.

### 52. The compiler moves from Rust 1.99.1 to 1.100.0, by CLAUDE.md §15 (decision item 278)

Assigned by the coordinating session on 2026-11-13, after the owner's instruction of that day, as that session passed it on: "Updated Rust language to latest available version 1.100.0, use it" (decision item 278; its record in the decision sheet is the coordinating session's). Written before any of it was built: before the toolchain was installed with our targets, and before the pin changed. It lands as a pull request of its own as soon as §15's list passes, outside decision item 70's 100 commits: the owner's answer to decision item 279, "Own pull request", on 2026-11-13, after this contract was first written. Items 278 and 279 are in the decision sheet on `docs/decisions-271-275` at `fccddd9` (pull request 25, not yet on `main`). Until it lands, every other branch stays on 1.99.1.

**Checked before anything else, on 2026-11-13** (01:49 UTC):

- *The release.* `RELEASES.md` at the tag `1.100.0` of `rust-lang/rust` opens with "Version 1.100.0 (2026-11-12)". The channel manifest `static.rust-lang.org/dist/channel-rust-1.100.0.toml` is dated 2026-11-12, names Cargo `0.101.0 (3acc79c0c 2026-11-05)`, and has the SHA-256 `3051e859570f64a8c5acb9d09e87425db52c7d97287c67e8f6d57d7d34ef9258`, which is what the `.sha256` file beside it says. The coordinating session read the compiler's commit (`0ef1bc386059596dada8fb65b50425aab2ea637a`) and its LLVM (23.1.5; 1.99.1 has 23.1.1) from a host-only install. This task reads both again from `rustc -vV` of the toolchain it installs. The release is one day old.
- *What pins the compiler today.* One version in four places that `verify.sh` holds together: `channel` in `rust-toolchain.toml`, `rust-version` in `Cargo.toml`, `EXPECTED` in `verify.sh`, and the commit in `developer-tools/toolchain/rustc-commit-hash`. CI pins by that version and that commit (`.github/ci/install-rust.sh`). It pins no archive's hash: rustup checks each archive against the manifest. The manifest's hashes for what the Mac and CI download go into the research note, as provenance.
- *Who reads Cargo's build directory by its layout.* Three places: `image_inputs::remove_linked` (`deps/<name>-<16 hex>`), the signing branch check in `xtask`'s `main.rs` (`release/deps/onbf_sign-*.s`), and `verify.sh`'s `wcslen` test (`release/deps/liborigin_boot_uefi-*.rlib`). The release notes say Cargo has a new layout for intermediate files and that the layout is no stable interface.
- *The flags.* The kernel's target gets `-C jump-tables=no` and `-C target-feature=+harden-sls-ret`, no SSE or AVX feature and no `target-cpu`. Ring 3 gets `-C target-cpu=x86-64-v3`. `expected_warning.rs` matches the five lines of the compiler's warning about the second flag exactly.
- *The kernel's image names its compiler.* `onbf-pack` writes the compiler's release and commit into the KRN manifest, so every kernel image changes with the compiler by design, whatever the code generator does.

**The release notes' lines that can break a build, change an image, or change what a record says.** The research note goes through every line of the notes; these are the ones that touch Origin, with what each is checked by. The first nine are the coordinating session's starting points, read again by this task in the notes themselves; the rest are this task's own reading.

| Line of the notes | What it can do here | Checked by |
|---|---|---|
| Cargo: a new layout for intermediate build artifacts (cargo #17354) | The three readers above find nothing, or the wrong file | Each reader is moved to something Cargo documents (its JSON messages, `cargo clean -p`, or an output path the tool names itself), then run for real. The relink path gets its run on a directory made stale on purpose. |
| Cargo's own lints, `[lints.cargo]`, `cargo::unused_dependencies` (cargo #17298) | New warnings in every build | Fixed, not allowed away. An unused dependency is removed. |
| "Rust" ABI on x86 uses SSE registers where they are always available (#161583) | Ring-3 code generation may change; `extern "C"` must not | The pull request is read for what it changes on x86-64. §10's FFI-bool test, the OIDL suite and the ring-3 boot proofs. |
| An error for SSE or AVX on an x86 soft-float target (#160302) | None today: the kernel enables neither | The kernel builds. Recorded for decision item 123: the kernel's integer features must be named one by one, never through `target-cpu`. |
| The never type is stable; `Infallible` is `!`; the fallback is `!` on every edition (#155499) | New errors or changed inference | The workspace builds and its tests pass; every change is listed. |
| Some `Box` impls generalised over allocators (#161946); custom allocators stable (#156882) | New inference errors. Comments that say an API "is unstable on Rust 1.99.1" may be out of date | The build; each such comment is checked against 1.100.0's documentation and corrected or left. No new API is used in this task. |
| New and stricter lints: repeated `repr` (#157036), `aligned_fields_in_packed` (#162160), `drop_in_place` (#160229), `ambiguous_glob_imported_traits` (#161805), rustdoc's `invalid_markdown_table` (#159583) | New warnings, which the gates treat as errors | Fixed in the code. A new `allow` only with its reason beside it, and listed in the record. |
| volatile accesses to memory outside the abstract machine may trap (#160564) | Words for the MMIO contract; no code change expected | The pull request is read and the driver contract's record says what it means for MMIO. |
| LLVM 23.1.5 and newly built `core`, `alloc`, `compiler_builtins` | Every image's bytes change. ADR-0017 Amendment 1's figures change (113 returns, 33 without an INT3, 2 jump tables, in the kernel of 2026-11-10) | The image gate passes for the code the build compiles. The libraries' new figures are reported, and the amendment's wording is proposed to the coordinating session. The reproducible-build gate, and the comparison across two hosts. |
| Closures capture more precisely in `if let` (#154210); dyn-compatibility checked in more places (#154992); cycle errors for enums with explicit discriminants (#157562); `let`-`else` and `macro_rules` grouping (#158515); the next solver in the orphan check (#160668) | New errors | The build. |
| `str::escape_debug` always escapes grapheme extenders (#158303); the standard library uses Unicode 18.0.0 | A host test that compares `{:?}` output or classifies characters may change its result | The host tests. A test whose expected text changes is listed with why. |
| `Iterator::min` and `max` use other `Ord` methods (#160203) | None for a consistent `Ord`; an inconsistent one would show | The host tests and the models. |

**Not in the notes, verified again by §9 on the installed toolchain:** that `-C target-feature=+harden-sls-ret` is still accepted, with which warning; that `-C jump-tables=no` still holds; that `cf-protection` and `stack-protector` are still absent from `rustc -C help` (the notes list `-Cprofile-sample-use` as the one option stabilized); that lld still reads `LLD_VERSION`; that the compiler still names library sources by the toolchain's path with `rust-src`, so that item 32's remap is still needed and still enough.

**When to stop a part and bring it to the coordinating session with the evidence**, because it is the owner's decision and holding the pin at 1.99.1 until a point release is a possible answer: the hardening feature is refused or no longer does what Z10 needs; a gate can no longer hold; one of §10's three regressions is back; an image is no longer the same bytes from two builds or on two hosts and the cause is in the toolchain; `extern "C"` or the system-call path changes; a boot proof fails for a reason in the compiler.

| Field | |
|---|---|

| Objective | The tree is pinned to Rust 1.100.0 in all four places; everything builds with it and with nothing else; every gate that held on 1.99.1 holds; and each kind of evidence CLAUDE.md §15 lists is recorded with its result. The eleven files of the reproducible-build gate are again the same bytes on the Mac and in CI. **Not in this task:** `CLAUDE.md`, the decision sheet and the matrix's quotes of the directive (revision 7.8 is the coordinating session's, on a records branch this branch merges before its final gates); any ADR's text (proposed wording goes to the coordinating session); any use of something 1.100.0 makes stable (the never type, the allocator API, `extern "custom"`); the kernel's x86-64-v3 integer features (decision item 123); any ABI or format change; TASKS 46 and TASKS 50 beyond what "Required dependencies" says. |

(7) Every image is the same bytes from two builds and on two kinds of host, holds no path of the machine that built it, and has the fixed line for the linker in `.comment`. (8) `unsafe` code is reviewed wherever a fix touches it or the compiler's behaviour changed under it, and the record says who reviewed it: an AI session, and no person, unless one does. (9) Warnings are errors as before; a new `allow` is named with its reason. (10) Nothing is pushed without this session's user's word. |

Each is planted in the working tree and undone before the next commit. |
