The coordinating session checks the result. |

**The order of work.** (1) This contract. (2) The research note, from the notes and the linked pull requests. (3) The toolchain installed; `rustc -vV` and `cargo -V` recorded. (4) Fixes that 1.99.1 also accepts, one commit for each cause, found by building with 1.100.0 in the working tree without committing the pin. (5) The pin in its four places, in one commit, with whatever cannot build on both compilers. (6) The readers of Cargo's build directory. (7) `verify.sh` for real, and `VERIFICATION.md`. (8) The gates and the boots on the Mac. (9) The records branch merged; the gates again. (10) The user asked for the draft pull request; CI's results and the comparison across hosts recorded.

**Result (2026-11-13, before CI).** The account is `docs/research/2026-11-13-rust-1.100.0.md`; the provenance entry is "The compiler moves from Rust 1.99.1 to 1.100.0".

- **The pin:** `c4e9861`, in its four places. `rustc 1.100.0 (0ef1bc386 2026-11-09)`, LLVM 23.1.5.
- **What the tree needed.** Before the pin, each accepted by 1.99.1 as well: `a991096`, `62710d9`, `0c7155e`, `0ac1a6e`, `817cd16` with its correction `74c1e49`, `fa0f3e0`. With it: the hardening warning's new text. After it: `b008319`, the hardening option as a check of its own in `verify.sh`. No compile error, and no change to any line of `unsafe` code.
- **What the release notes did not say:** the compiler now announces that `-C target-feature=+harden-sls-ret` will become a hard error (rust-lang/rust#162235, no release named). The flag is accepted and the image gate passes. It went to the owner: decision items 280 and 281, ADR-0017 Amendment 2.
- **`817cd16` was wrong, on both compilers,** and the stale-directory run this contract owed found it: its `cargo clean` named no package, so Cargo removed the host's `release` directory and left the stale file. The build stopped; it could not pass. `74c1e49` is the correction. I had told the coordinating session the first form was TASKS 50's first half before any run had exercised it.
- **§15's list, on the development Mac** (Rust 1.100.0, `5b65ce0`, nothing uncommitted): `cargo xtask check` passes (1,695 host tests, 0 failed, 27 ignored; Loom 53 models, 78 controls); `verify.sh` 21 of 21; `boot-test` 11 of 11; `boot-matrix` passes; the image that booted is the reproducible-build gate's. Every image has new bytes, each the same from two builds. The six changes before the pin changed no image on 1.99.1.
- **The negative controls:** the pin moved in two of its four places: with the commit's file left at 1.99.1's, `verify.sh` and `install-rust.sh` both fail, naming both commits; with `rust-version` left at 1.99.1, `verify.sh` fails and `install-rust.sh`, which reads only the channel and the commit, does not. So the contract's "both scripts, for each of the four" is more than they do: `rust-version` and `EXPECTED` are held by `verify.sh` alone, in CI's `toolchain` job. And a target directory made stale on purpose is linked again, for the program and for the kernel alone, on both compilers, and the result is the gate's bytes; `expected_warning.rs`'s tests hold that another warning and 1.99.1's five lines pass through uncollapsed. Four for the new check: the option out of the configuration, commented out, or under the loader's table, and a bare return in the probe.
- **TASKS 46** is in this branch (`8455b75`), since its trial passed both runs. **TASKS 50** keeps `image-graph-gate`'s label for `origin-core`, each linked file with its own package, and the stale-directory run for `origin.core`, after S4-2; the relink step names no file of Cargo's any more.
- **The records branch** (`docs/records-rust-1.100.0`, CLAUDE.md 7.8 and ADR-0017 Amendment 2) is merged at `a4e93fb`, after `main` at `f77e1a3`. The booted tree and the final tree differ in documents alone.
- **The last `check`,** on `6a89251` (the review's answers and these records), twice, each with the §59 assembly file removed first: passed both times, 1,695 host tests, 0 failed, 27 ignored; the §59 counts 3, 0, 1, 3. The second build of that check was not reached (review F8).
- **The last `check` before CI,** on `9b72748`, the tip pushed to pull request 28, nothing uncommitted, the development Mac, 05:35 to 05:42 UTC: passed, 1,695 host tests, 0 failed, 27 ignored; the requirements gate on CLAUDE.md 7.8.
- **CI (2026-11-13):** Pull request 28 (draft), run 43374286164 on `9b72748`, every job passed: `check` (1,696 host tests, 0 failed), toolchain verification (21 of 21), the macOS job (rustup installed 1.100.0; all 22 files the same on a Linux and a macOS runner), and the boots under QEMU's software CPU and under KVM on two AMD EPYC 9V45 runners. The boot step took within about a minute of Rust 1.99.1's on runners with the same processor. The research note's §6.4 has the jobs and the paired times.
- **The independent review** (`docs/engineering/REVIEW_T52_2026-11-13.md`, one AI reviewer; no person has reviewed this change): no high or medium finding, five low and six informational. Each is answered in its Resolution, with its commit and the planted fault that shows it (research note §6.5).
- **The macOS job** stays an ordinary job of the workflow, so its failure fails the run: the coordinating session's ruling of 2026-11-13. It has three runs on GitHub's runners (a pass and an aimed failure on 1.99.1, a pass on 1.100.0). If it fails for the runner's own reasons twice in any ten runs, that goes to the coordinating session before anything is changed.
- **Not shown:** a physical machine; a release candidate; a third kind of host; how often the macOS runner fails for its own reasons; a person's review. The owner's approval is the merge of pull request 28.

### 53. The real-CPU boot jobs' step time beside their processors, in CI's summary

Assigned by the coordinating session on 2026-11-13, after `main`'s run 43340136715 was taken for stuck (TASKS item 53 above; `PERFORMANCE_TRENDS_2026-11-13_ci-boot-step.md`). Written before the task's code was committed; the two scripts were tried in a scratch directory first.

| Field | |
|---|---|
| Task ID | TASKS item 53, on `land/s4-part2`, after the trends record's commit `ac86367`. |
| Objective | The job "processors tested on" prints, beside each real-CPU boot job's processor, how long that job's `cargo --locked xtask boot-matrix` step took, read from the run's own job list through GitHub's API, with a sentence saying that the figures are KVM guests on GitHub's runners and a trend that claims nothing. **Not in this task:** any threshold, and any failure of a slow run; the job on QEMU's software processor; keeping the figures from one run to the next; the weekly processor sweep; any job of `ci.yml` but the summary job. |
| Source ownership | This task alone edits, in `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, the `processors` job only: its `permissions:` block and its steps. It edits `.github/ci/processor-coverage.sh` and writes `.github/ci/boot-step-durations.sh`. Its own records: this contract and its entry, the `.github/` section of `SOURCE_PROVENANCE.md`, and row S53-12. TASKS item 46 edits other jobs of `ci.yml` and lands with TASKS item 52 in a pull request of its own; the batch merges `main` once after it. |
| Architecture contract | CLAUDE.md 7.6 §53 (what CI measures is recorded as a trend that claims nothing; decision item 115), §1 "Measure reproducibly" and "Qualify the declared environment", §74 and rule 30; ADR-0018 V9 (no claim of speed). §80: no spending. Not an ABI or format decision (§84). |
| Relevant specifications | GitHub's REST reference, "List jobs for a workflow run attempt", read on 2026-11-13: anyone with read access to the repository can use it, and each step has `name`, `status`, `conclusion`, `number`, `started_at` and `completed_at`, the last three of which may be null. GitHub's documentation of a workflow's `permissions:`: a job's block replaces the workflow's for that job. |
| Required dependencies | The trends record, `ac86367`. `gh` and `jq`, which GitHub's Ubuntu runner images carry. No new action and no new crate (§5). No owner answer is awaited. |
| Security invariants | (1) **Permissions.** The workflow's block is `contents: read` and stays so. Before, the `processors` job inherits it. After, it has a block of its own: `contents: read` restated, and `actions: read`. Nothing writes, and the only credential is the run's own token, passed to `gh` as `GH_TOKEN` in one step; no other secret is used. A pull request from a fork gets a read-only token, which is all the job asks. (2) **What the API returns is data.** A job's label names a file only if it matches `[A-Za-z0-9._-]+`; a duration is digits or nothing; a conclusion keeps letters, underscores and spaces only; nothing returned is run or put on a command line. (3) **The step never fails the job.** A list that cannot be read, an answer not in the expected form or a run that is not named gives a warning and the column says "not recorded". (4) The durations are written under `$RUNNER_TEMP`, not into the checkout. |
| Performance constraints | None set. Performance cost: none, by construction: no image, gate or boot changes; one request to the API in a job that already runs. |
| Required tests | No harness covers `.github/ci/`, so the scripts are tried by hand in a scratch directory. **`boot-step-durations.sh`:** `main`'s run 43340136715 (5,787 s and 5,776 s, success); pull request 21's run 43284416104 (3,189 s and 3,294 s); a cancelled run, 43341053868 (cancelled); a run that does not exist; a file that is not JSON; a file that is missing; no run named. The last four warn and exit 0. Planted jobs: a label `../../etc` (skipped, and no file outside the directory), a job without the step ("no such step") and a step still running (no time, `in_progress`). **`processor-coverage.sh`:** durations for some records and none for others; no second argument; a durations directory that does not exist; no records. **In CI:** the batch's pull request's run shows the column, with the token the job's block grants. |
| Actual deliverables | The new script, the edited script and job, this contract and its result, the provenance section's line and row S53-12's limitation. Nothing is pushed by this task alone: it goes with the batch. |
| Acceptance conditions | `cargo xtask check` passes on the task's last commit. On the batch's first CI run, "processors tested on" shows each real-CPU job's step time beside its processor, equal to what the API gives for that step, and the run's other jobs are unchanged. Row S53-12 stays `partial`. **Not verified by this task:** a pull request from a fork, since none can be opened without a second account. It rests on GitHub's statement that a fork's token is read-only, which is all the job asks, and on the step never failing the job. |

**Result (2026-11-13): built, checked on the development Mac, and run in CI.**

- **The code** is `a4fb3f7`, with `83ee792` after the review below. Only the `processors` job of `ci.yml` changed. Its permissions block is `contents: read` and `actions: read`, and the run's own token is in one step's environment.
- **Tried by hand,** every case the contract lists, with the results it expected: `main`'s run 43340136715 gives 5,787 s and 5,776 s, and pull request 21's run 43284416104 gives 3,189 s and 3,294 s. The summary job, run on the first of them with its two processor records, prints "96 min 27 s" and "96 min 16 s" beside the two EPYC 7763s.
- **Where it differs from the contract:** the review found that the label's pattern lets `.` and `..` through. Writing to either only failed, since both are directories, but the script then printed a time for them. `83ee792` refuses both by name, as it refuses any other label that cannot name a file. Security invariant (2) now reads: a label names a file only if it matches `[A-Za-z0-9._-]+` and is not `.` or `..`.
- **The gate:** `cargo xtask check` passes on `1e2908b`: 1,694 host tests passed, 0 failed and 27 ignored; Loom ran 53 models and caught 78 negative controls. `83ee792` changes only a script that no gate reads. The document, requirements, provenance, roadmap and progress gates pass on it.
- **Review:** the coordinating session, an AI, read `a4fb3f7` in full and accepted it, with the remark fixed in `83ee792`. No person has reviewed it.
- **In CI:** run 43358268915 of pull request 27, on `edb0e32`, passed every job. The log of "processors tested on" shows the token it got, `Actions: read` and `Contents: read`. Its new step printed 5,786 s and 5,813 s for the two real-CPU jobs, the same as the jobs API's start and end of each job's `boot-matrix` step. Both runners had an AMD EPYC 7763, by their processor records. The summary page shows job summaries only to a signed-in viewer, so the table was rebuilt from the same script at `edb0e32` and the same inputs: "96 min 26 s" and "96 min 53 s" beside the two EPYC 7763s, each with the sentence that the figures claim nothing.
- **Still open:** a pull request from a fork.

### 50. `origin.core` in the relink step and in the image-graph gate

Assigned by the coordinating session on 2026-11-13, once task S4-2 had landed in the batch `land/s4-part2` at `793299a`; written before any of it was built. It finishes what TASKS item 52 left of the relink step (its review, finding 4) and what S4-2 brought: `origin.core` is a shared library, `liborigin_core.so`, linked with the fixed linker line like each program, and no target directory holding a stale copy of it has ever been made on purpose.

**What the tree does today** (read at `793299a`): `image_inputs::remove_linked` learns which packages to clean from the stale file's target alone (`linked_packages`): the kernel for the kernel's target, and every package `ring3::carried_packages()` gives for the userspace's, `origin-core` among them. So a stale `liborigin_core.so` is cleaned with every carried program, and a stale program with `origin-core`; each is relinked, but more is rebuilt than was stale, and the choice rests on two lists being kept in step. `image-graph-gate` labels `origin-core` "the carried program `origin-core`", which it is not.

| Field | |
|---|---|
| Task ID | TASKS item 50, on branch `claude/t50-origin-core-relink`, cut from `land/s4-part2` at `793299a` (which holds S4-2's `79355d6` and `main` at `803755c`). |
| Objective | Each linked file the relink step is given comes with the package that links it, from the caller, which knows it: `framekernel` for the kernel, `origin-core` for `liborigin_core.so`, each program's own package for its file. The clean for a stale file names that package and no other, and the choice by target goes. A stale `liborigin_core.so` is then cleaned by `cargo clean --package origin-core` and linked again, and that is shown by a run on a target directory made stale on purpose. `image-graph-gate` names `origin-core` as what it is. **Not in this task:** any change to what an image is built from or how; S4-2's gates; task S4-3 (TASKS item 55), which starts from the same base and edits `framekernel/` only. |
| Source ownership | `developer-tools/xtask/src/image_inputs.rs`: the relink step's signature and its tests, and `images()`' label. Three call sites pass the package beside each path, one argument each: `ring3::origin_core` and `ring3::programs` in `developer-tools/xtask/src/ring3.rs`, and the kernel's build in `developer-tools/xtask/src/main.rs`; nothing else in those files. Records: this contract and its result, the provenance entry, `docs/research/2026-11-11-toolchain-path-in-images.md` for the run. |
| Architecture contract | CLAUDE.md 7.8 §75 and §60; TASKS items 44 and 52 for the relink step; ADR-0011 Amendment 2 for `origin.core`. Not an ABI or format decision (§84). |
| Relevant specifications | None outside the repository. Cargo 1.100.0's `cargo clean` as measured in TASKS item 52 (research note §6.5): with `--package`, it removes that package's files for the target named, its linked file under its outer name included for a binary. Whether it does so for a shared library's outer name is what the run shows. |
| Required dependencies | `793299a`. No new crate or tool. Rust 1.100.0, the pin. |
| Security invariants | (1) No `cargo clean` is put together without a package, and none with an empty target directory (unchanged). (2) A clean names exactly the package that links the stale file. (3) A stale file can never pass: after the clean and one more build it is read again, and one still stale is an error (unchanged). (4) A file the clean did not remove is said to be still there, with the package, never said to have been removed and linked again (unchanged). (5) No image's bytes change: the reproducible-build gate's digests are the same as at `793299a`. |
| Performance constraints | None set. Nothing at run time: no image changes. A stale file costs one compilation of its own package, where it cost every carried package's. |
| Required tests | **Host tests:** the arguments of the clean for each kind of linked file, with its package; a linked file given with no package refused; the stale and the fresh cases of `relink_stale` as today, with the package carried through. **Gate:** `cargo xtask check` under the machine's turn, and the reproducible-build gate's digests compared with `793299a`'s. **The real run, under the machine's turn:** (a) the linker's text changed and changed back in `.cargo/config.toml`: `origin.core` and each program are cleaned by their own packages and linked again, and the results are the gate's bytes; (b) `liborigin_core.so` alone made stale, by one byte of its linker line changed in every copy Cargo keeps: `cargo clean --package origin-core`, linked again, the gate's bytes; (c) a further run links nothing again. The host's `release` directory is checked after each step. No boots: no image is built differently. |
| Actual deliverables | The code and its tests; the run's record; the provenance entry; this contract's result. Not pushed: the final commit goes to the coordinating session for the keeper. |
| Acceptance conditions | The host tests and `check` pass; (a), (b) and (c) pass on the development Mac under Rust 1.100.0; the digests are unchanged. If (b) shows that `cargo clean --package origin-core` leaves the outer `liborigin_core.so`, the step's check (invariant 4) stops the build, and that goes to the coordinating session with the evidence before anything else is changed. |

**Result (2026-11-13).** The code is `be7980e`; the run is the ledger's §19 (`docs/research/2026-11-11-toolchain-path-in-images.md`).

- **The callers pass the package beside each path,** as the coordinating session accepted on 2026-11-13; `linked_packages` is gone, and the clean names the stale file's own package alone. `image-graph-gate` names `origin-core` as the library the kernel carries.
- **The host test** holds that only the stale file's package is cleaned (`framekernel` and `first-program` when both are stale, `first-program` alone when it alone is), the clean's arguments for the kernel, `origin.core` and each program, and the refusals as before.
- **The real run for `origin.core`, the first:** with the linker's text changed and changed back, `origin-core`, `first-program` and `pin-program` were each cleaned by their own package and linked again, and the results are the gate's bytes; with `liborigin_core.so` alone made stale, one clean, `--package origin-core`, and the library is the gate's bytes again; a further run linked nothing. `cargo clean --package origin-core` removes the library under its outer name, so the step's check that the file is gone did not stop the build. The host's `release` directory was there after every step.
- **The gate:** `cargo xtask check` passes on `be7980e`, 1,807 host tests, 0 failed, 27 ignored, no warning line. **No image changed:** the gate's twenty files are, all forty from both builds, the same bytes as those CI built from `793299a` (run 43421978012), by `cargo xtask reproducible-compare`. No boot: nothing is built differently.
- **Not shown:** a stale file of a package that is neither the kernel, `origin.core` nor a carried program; the relink step is given no such file. No person has reviewed this.

### 55. The kernel-wide bound, and O2 for step 4 (task S4-3)

Sent by the coordinating session on 2026-11-13. Written here on 2026-11-13, before any code. It is task S4-3 of ADR-0011 N14 step 4 (`docs/research/2026-11-11-n14-step4-scope.md`, section 4, whose exit tests were written before this), and it builds decision items 206, 209 and 210 (ADR-0005 Amendment 8, G2, G5 and G6). Items 207 and 208 (G3, G4) are drafts for step 5 and the Loader Service, and are not built here. It closes row K5 of the system-call threat model, and gives row O2 the citation its closure for step 4 needs.

| Field | |
|---|---|
| Task ID | S4-3, of ADR-0011 N14 step 4; TASKS item 55, on branch `claude/elegant-benz-1983ff`, cut from `main` at `803755c` and, on the coordinating session's word, reset to `land/s4-part2` at `793299a`, which holds S4-2's final commit `79355d6`. |
| Objective | No set of processes can make the kernel hold more memory than the kernel set aside for them at boot. (1) **The figure.** A set of limits is worth a figure in bytes: the most kernel memory and frames those limits let a process make the kernel hold, kind by kind, from each kind's largest kernel object as the build measures it (G5). The figure is the sum of a fixed part for the process itself (the process, its account and its address space's own object, and the page tables up to the framework's bound of 32 a space), and of one part for each kind its limits name: its handle table at the size its `handles` quota lets it reach; each channel, as the kernel allocates one; the queued-message bytes, which are already charged as the heap rounds them (`ipc::message_cost`); the committed bytes, with each memory object's own allocations; the mapping records; each event, port and binding, a binding with its slot in the port's index (row K7, as G5 asks); and each thread's kernel stack, FPU area and kernel objects. Every allocation is rounded as the kernel heap rounds it, and a table that grows is counted at the most it can hold during a growth. Arithmetic that would overflow makes no figure, and a launch with such limits is refused. (2) **The root.** At boot, once the loader's memory is reclaimed and before the first process exists, the kernel sizes the root: the bytes its free frames hold then, less a reserve for the kernel's own use, and prints both. Until it is sized the root holds nothing, so a process made earlier is refused. (3) **The payment.** Making a process pays its figure out of the root before anything of it is allocated: no process ID is drawn, no address space or table is made. A process the root cannot pay for is refused with `limit` (G5), and the root, the frames and the heap are as they were. The payment is held by the process's account and given back when the account goes: after the process, its threads, its address space and every object charged to it are gone, so the figure covers each of those for as long as it exists. No charge made by a running process changes (G5). (4) **Threads.** A process's limits name the most threads it may have started and not yet reaped (`threads`), which the figure needs for "a kernel stack and an FPU area for each thread" (G5). The kernel's launch path is the only starter (G2); a start past the count is refused. This is no call and no interface: it is the count G3's draft names, held inside the kernel, and its shape for a call is G3's, decided with step 5. Each limits value in the tree names it explicitly (G6): one for each carried program, and for each proof process the threads its proof starts in it. (5) **O2.** The threat model's row cites `origin.kernel` at its version, which has no method that returns a new thread or process; a host test over the definition holds that, so the row reopens by a failing test when such a method is added. **Not in this task:** the thread and process calls and their quotas (G3, G4, step 5 and the Loader Service); the Loader Service's defaults (G6, M6). **What the bound does not cover, written down:** (i) **The slab table.** The kernel heap keeps blocks of up to 2,048 bytes in at most 1,024 slabs (4 MiB, `origin_frame::memory`), a fixed capacity apart from the frames: the root bounds bytes, not slab slots, so processes' small objects can still fill the slab table while frames remain. Recommended as a framework task: a slab table that grows. (ii) **A port's index after its binders have gone** (row K7): its slots are counted in each binder's figure, but the index never shrinks while the port is open, so slots made by a binder whose payment has been given back stay until the port closes. (iii) **The frames a stalled shootdown never reuses** (`THREAT_MODEL_ADDRESS_SPACES.md` T3): kept on purpose, and not given back to the root. (iv) **Slab fragmentation**, at most the slab table's 4 MiB, which the reserve covers. Each goes to the threat model's rows as found, and to the coordinating session as a follow-up. |
| Source ownership | This task's session, on `claude/elegant-benz-1983ff`. **New, owned by it:** the bound's boot proof, `framekernel/src/ipc_proof/kernel_bound.rs`. **Shared files, and how far:** `framekernel/src/quota.rs` (the figure, the costs, the purse and the payment, host-tested; `Limits` and `MemoryLimits` move here from `process.rs`, which re-exports them, so every `crate::process::Limits` stays as it is); `framekernel/src/process.rs` (the payment at creation, the threads count, the costs as the build measures them); `framekernel/src/launch.rs` (a refused payment is `limit`, not `no_memory`; nothing else of it changes); `framekernel/src/bootproof.rs` (one row and the counts); `framekernel/src/kernel.rs` (sizing the root, one call and its line); `framekernel/src/sched.rs` (the call that runs the new proof); `framekernel/src/ipc_proof.rs` (a module line and the proof's entry); `framekernel/frame/src/userspace.rs` (`MAX_TABLES` made public, so the figure reads the bound and does not copy it: one word, the framework's file); each file that writes a `Limits` value (`aspace.rs`, `user.rs`, `ipc_proof.rs`, `ipc_proof/core_pin.rs`, `ipc_proof/directory.rs`, `ipc_proof/memory_objects.rs`, `ipc_proof/events_ports.rs`, `program.rs`, `program_proof.rs`): the new `threads` field, one line each, and nothing else; `developer-tools/xtask` (the boot markers; the new proof file in `listed_names.rs`'s list of files that may make proof processes, with its reason); the OIDL test that holds O2 (in `developer-tools/oidl`'s tests, reading `sdk/interfaces/origin.kernel.oidl`, which is not changed). Records: `THREAT_MODEL_SYSCALLS.md` rows K5, K7 and O2 and its list "What must be closed"; ADR-0013's table and controls; `PERFORMANCE_BUDGETS.md` (one ledger row); `SOURCE_PROVENANCE.md`, `STATUS.md`, this file, the requirements rows. `launch.rs` is this task's in the ledger's order (S4-2 before, S4-4 after). |
| Architecture contract | ADR-0005 P3, P6 and P11, C7, D4 and its note (item 142), F7, Amendment 8 G2, G5 and G6, and G3 as a draft only; ADR-0011 N14 and Amendment 2 L8 (proof processes keep A6; the launch path is the only way a program starts); ADR-0013 R1, R6 and R7; ADR-0016 R8 (a launch draws its places from the kernel's generator; the payment comes before any draw, so a refusal needs no generator); ADR-0018 V3, V4, V5 and V8; CLAUDE.md 7.8 §13, §28, §29, §53, §58 and §78; the system-call threat model's rows O1, O2, O3, O5, K5, K6, K7 and K11. **No ABI or format decision is made here:** nothing a program sees changes; `user-abi` and `origin.kernel` stay as they are. What this task chooses, each the kernel's own and none an interface, put to the coordinating session with it: **(a) the reserve:** the larger of 64 MiB and one eighth of the free memory at sizing (on the development Mac's 512 MiB machine, 64 MiB), a hypothesis like a budget (§53, item 115), since the kernel's own use after boot (drivers, caches, the launch path's own pages) is not measured; **(b) where the payment is held:** in the account, given back when the account goes, the latest of everything the figure covers; **(c) the pages a launch maps itself** (a program's image, its stack and its start record) are the launcher's own use and not in the figure, which G5 makes of the limits: in step 4 they are the kernel's, out of the reserve; with the Loader Service they are its own committed memory (G4's draft). **Two readings, put with it:** "destroyed" in G5 is read as "its account is gone", for the reason in (b); and "each kind's largest kernel object" is read, for a kind kept in a table that grows, as the table's footprint at its quota, growth included. |
| Relevant specifications | None outside the repository: the figure is arithmetic over the kernel's own types, and the heap's and Rust's `Vec` growth rules are measured by host tests, not read from a document. **No source of any other operating system is opened** (CLAUDE.md §3). |
| Required dependencies | `land/s4-part2` at `793299a`, with S4-2's per-call pin and launch path and Rust 1.100.0. Items 206, 209 and 210 accepted on 2026-11-12. No owner answer is awaited to start; the choices (a) to (c) and the two readings are with the coordinating session. No crate from outside the workspace (CLAUDE.md §5). |
| Security invariants | (1) **Paid before made.** No process exists, and no kernel memory is allocated for one, before its figure is paid; a refusal leaves the root, the free frames, the heap and the next process ID as they were. (2) **Never more out than in.** The root's balance never goes below zero and never above its size: a payment and its check are one atomic step, from any number of processors; a repayment cannot be made twice (the payment is moved into the account and dropped once). (3) **Covered while it exists.** A process's payment is out of the root from before its first allocation until its account is dropped, which is after its last thread is reaped, its address space is gone and every object charged to its account is gone. (4) **The figure is not less than what the limits allow.** Each kind's part is at least what the kernel can allocate for it under the quota, as the heap rounds it; a table that grows is counted at its most during a growth. Where a kind's allocation outlives its payment, that is written down (below) and not hidden. (5) **No default.** `Limits` has no constructor that fills a kind; every value in the tree names every field, `threads` included (G6). (6) **No new lock and no new hot path.** The root is one atomic word; the threads count is checked under the lifecycle lock the start already takes. No system call gains a check. |
| Performance constraints | None set (CLAUDE.md §53, decision item 115; ADR-0018 V3 and V6). **None, by construction,** on every system call and on every charge a running process makes: no call gains a check, and G5 leaves each charge as it is. **Unmeasured, owed,** at each process's creation and end, with this model: the figure's arithmetic (about thirty checked multiplications and additions), one compare-and-swap loop on the root's one word at creation and one atomic add when the account goes; and at each thread's start one comparison under the lock it already takes. A ledger row in `PERFORMANCE_BUDGETS.md` names it, with the memory it costs: every process holds its worst case, not its use, so fewer processes fit than memory would hold. Reported as trends, judged by nothing: the time to load and start a program, which S4-1's proof prints, before and after; the root, the reserve, and the most of the root paid out at once during the boot. |
| Required tests | The ledger's exit tests for S4-3, all of them. **Host (`quota.rs`):** a set of limits' figure, kind by kind: each kind alone adds exactly its part, the parts add, and a figure that would overflow is none; each kind's part from its cost, with the costs a test gives; the heap's rounding against the real heap's (`origin_frame::heap`, the bytes it counts in use for each size and alignment); a growing table's bound against Rust's `Vec` (its capacity after growing one at a time and by several, up to the quota, never more than the bound counts); paying and repaying to the unit: a payment of exactly what is left succeeds and leaves zero, one byte more is refused and takes nothing, a payment's drop gives back exactly what it took, and charges from several threads never take the balance below zero; a root that is not sized refuses every payment; sizing it twice is refused. **Host (O2):** a test over `origin.kernel.oidl` finds no method whose results name a `process` or a `thread` handle. **Boot (a Refuse proof under ADR-0013, "the kernel-wide bound"):** (a) the root's size, the free memory it came from and the reserve are printed when it is sized; (b) the costs as the build measured them are printed, kind by kind; (c) after every proof before it, the root is back at its size; (d) with a proof process holding most of the root, a program whose figure is more than what is left is refused by `launch::load_program` with `limit`, and nothing is made: the root, the free frames, the heap in use and the next process ID are as they were; (e) once that process is gone, a process with the same limits is made, and its payment is its figure; (f) after the proof, the root is back at its size; (g) the most of the root paid out at once during the boot is printed. **Negative controls**, each planted in an exported copy, seen to fail where aimed, and recorded in ADR-0013: the payment left out (line (d) fails); the repayment left out (line (f) fails, or (c)); and, beyond the ledger's two, the threads count not checked (a host or boot line that starts one thread more than the limit fails), and the figure's thread part left out (the host test of the parts fails). |
| Actual deliverables | The figure, the costs, the root, the payment and their host tests in `quota.rs`; the payment and the threads count in `process.rs`; the root sized in `kernel.rs`; the boot proof and its markers; the O2 test; ADR-0013's row and controls; the threat model's rows; the ledger row; STATUS, this file, provenance and the matrix rows (A05-G2, A05-G5, A05-G6, A05-P11, A05-F7 and A05-D4 where the gate expects them). Small commits that each build. Nothing pushed, and no commit to `land/s4-part2` or `main`. |
| Acceptance conditions | On the last commit that changes code: `cargo xtask check`, `cargo xtask boot-test` and `cargo xtask boot-matrix` pass on the development Mac under Rust 1.100.0, every one run as `cargo xtask exclusive -- <command>`; every exit test above passes; every negative control failed where it was aimed and is recorded; then records-only commits. The matrix rows it moves, each taking its condition from here: **A05-G5** to `built` (the figure, the root and the payment as above, under QEMU only; scope `both/hw`, so not `done`); **A05-G2** to `built` (O2's row cites `origin.kernel` at `user-abi` 0.7, and the definition test holds it); **A05-G6** stays `partial`, with every limits value naming `threads`; **A05-P11** and **A05-F7** keep their state, with their limitations saying the kernel-wide bound exists. The hand-over names what the reviewer must read and in what order, each invariant with where it is held, what the author is least sure of, and what rests on reading alone. An independent review, arranged by the coordinating session, has been answered on this branch. **Not shown by this task:** anything on a physical machine or under KVM (QEMU's software processor on the development Mac only; CI's guests are the keeper's run); that each kind's cost is the most it can be, beyond the reading of its allocations and the host tests of the heap's and `Vec`'s rules: no boot fills every quota of a process and compares the kernel's use with the figure; the Loader Service's defaults (G6, M6); the high-water marks G6 asks each program's limits to come from, which stay with row A05-G6. |

**Rulings of the coordinating session (2026-11-13), which change the contract above.** The contract was sent at `da6827f`, with an inventory of the kernel's allocations after it. The coordinating session ruled on each point and sent nothing to the owner: (a) the reserve's size is the kernel's own budget figure, not an ABI, format or scope decision; (b) the `threads` count is within G5; (e) a growing table is counted at its most during a growth. The rest change what is built:

- **(b) Threads and step 5.** `threads` is in `Limits` inside the kernel, with no call and no ABI change; each step-4 program gets the least it needs by G6, which is 1. When step 5 builds G3's call, the count's charge and refund follow G3 as decided again then.
- **(c) A host test for the refund's timing.** One host test has an object charged to an account outlive the process object, and the refund waits for it; the boot proof shows the same with a channel charged to a process that is dropped first.
- **(d) The launch's own pages are the creator's, paid out of the root, not the reserve.** Reading (c) of the architecture contract is replaced: the image, the stack and the start record a launch maps for a child are memory the creator spends for it, so in step 4 the root pays them, as the figure is paid. The payment at a launch is the figure of the limits plus the pages the launch maps, recorded and printed apart, and both come back when the account goes. `launch::load_program` pays them before the child is made, the start record at its largest (`origin_abi::start::MAX_SIZE`), and gives back what the record did not use once it is written; a launch the root cannot pay for in full is refused with nothing made. A proof process's pages, which the kernel's proofs map one at a time, are paid as each is mapped. Each private page costs its frame and its share of the space's two tables of page records; each page of `origin.core`, whose frames every process shares, costs its share of the records. The reserve stays the kernel's own use. This changes no figure G5 defines.
- **A port's index.** G5 asks that it be measured per binding and counted: each binding's figure counts the most index it can make the port grow by, eight slots (a host test holds that bound). Measured, it showed what G5 did not foresee: the index never shrinks while its port is open, so slots a binder's bindings grew stay after its payment comes back. That piece is with the coordinating session; until it answers, the figure counts eight slots a binding and row K7 says what stays uncovered.
- **The space's page records.** When `mappings` is above zero, the space's two per-page record tables are counted at the most pages its tables can map, derived in code from `MAX_TABLES` and the table layout (`origin_frame::userspace::MAX_USER_PAGES`). **Performance cost, stated with it:** this is an overestimate on purpose, about 2 MiB for each process that may map, which lowers how many processes the root admits. Charging the record tables per mapping as they grow would tighten it.
- **A fix to S3-5b's code, found by this task.** `UserSpace::prepare_run` reserved room in both record tables for a whole run before it checked the space's bound, so a refused `memory_map` of a large shared object could leave that room behind, and a process's holdings depended on the largest object in the system rather than on its own limits. It now refuses with `ChangeError::Limit` a run whose records would pass the space's bound, after the already-mapped check and before any reservation. No status, order or call changes. One commit of its own, with a host test that measures both tables' capacity before and after, and one that `invalid_args` for a page already mapped still comes first.
- **The framework's read-only sizes.** `ObjectRef::ALLOCATION`, `userspace::{MAX_TABLES, MAX_USER_PAGES, PAGE_RECORD, SHARED_FRAME}` (`MAX_TABLES` was private: one keyword) and `ports::{Reservation::ALLOCATIONS, Port::INDEX_SLOT, Port::FIRST_INDEX_SLOTS}`, each computed from its type, in one commit of their own. No `unsafe` is added or touched. Beside them in the kernel: `HandleTable::ELEMENTS`, `Mappings::RECORD`, `ipc::channel_cost` and `sched::THREAD_STACK` made `pub(crate)`.
- **Not covered, each recorded in the threat rows with why it is bounded:** the heap's fixed slab table (an open row, TASKS item 56, a framework task with a table that grows as its starting point); frames a stalled shootdown never reuses (T3) and slab fragmentation, which the reserve covers; a thread object held by a handle after its thread is reaped; the concurrent growth of one port's index; undelivered messages between their refund and `reap_closed`.
- **Performance cost (ADR-0018 V3):** **None, by construction,** on every system call: no call gains a check and no running process's charge changes (G5). **Unmeasured, owed,** at a launch and at an account's end: the figure's arithmetic and one compare-and-swap on the root's one word at creation, one more for each page a proof maps, a give-back once the start record is written, and one atomic add when the account goes; the memory each process holds at its worst case, the overestimate above among it. A ledger row names it.
- **The boot's count.** A boot with a generator runs 67 proofs; under QEMU only "speculation controls" fails (decision item 154), so the verdict is "degraded: 66 of 67".

**Result (2026-11-13): built and gated on the development Mac; not landed and not pushed. An independent review is to come.** The evidence is in `SOURCE_PROVENANCE.md`, under "Task S4-3", and the controls in ADR-0013 (KB-1 to KB-6). Under Rust 1.100.0, on `f5f1829` (code as at `8ca93c5`), in one turn of the machine: `cargo xtask check` passed in 434 s (1,825 host tests passed, none failed, 27 ignored); `cargo xtask boot-test` passed 11 of 11 in 166 s; `cargo xtask boot-matrix` passed 124 scenarios with none failed in 2,886 s, every kernel boot with a generator ending "66 of 67; failed: speculation controls" and the one without ending "59 of 61; 6 did not run". Every exit test of the ledger passed, and every negative control failed where it was aimed: the ledger's two (KB-1, KB-2) and four more (KB-3 to KB-6). What the boot printed on QEMU's 512 MiB machine: a root of 447,248 KiB from 512,784 KiB free less 65,536 KiB; the first program's figure 155,776 bytes and its launch 295,040 bytes ahead, 72,064 kept for its 17 pages; the proofs before the bound's had at most 72,105 KiB of the root out at once. Where it differs from the contract and its rulings:

- **A port's index** is counted per binding, eight slots, as G5 asks, and that is met for live bindings; it is not met for the slots their growth leaves behind once their payments come back, since the index never shrinks while its port is open. Decision item 282, accepted on 2026-11-13, is ADR-0005 Amendment 10 (I1): a port's creator pays for its index through a `bindings_per_port` limit. TASKS item 57 builds it; this task does not. Row K7 stays open on that point alone.
- **The refusal clause** refuses the launch alone. The contract also named a proof process with the program's limits; once ruling (d) made a launch pay its pages besides its figure, such a process fits in what is left, so the clause shows the launch and its snapshot only.
- **Two clauses beyond the ledger's:** a process given no threads cannot start one, the refusal taking nothing (for control KB-3); and a channel that outlives its creator keeps the payment out until it goes (ruling (c)).
- **The last line** prints the busiest moment of the proofs before this one, not of the whole boot: this proof's own holder takes nearly the whole root.
- **`user_pages` is `MAX_USER_PAGES + 1`** in the costs: a single page's map reserves room for one record before its tables refuse it, so a record table can grow for that one.
- **`ipc.rs`, `handles.rs` and `mapping.rs`** each gained a read-only size, as the rulings list; `sched.rs` makes `THREAD_STACK` `pub(crate)`.

**Answers to the independent review (2026-11-13).** One AI reviewer read `793299a..11028bc` (`docs/engineering/REVIEW_S4-3_2026-11-13.md`): no High, two Medium, two Low, six informational. The coordinating session ruled on its four questions, none for the owner. Each finding, with the commit that answers it, is in the review's "Resolution". What changes in the contract and the result above:

- **B1, counted:** each thread's part of a figure gains the most one system call can hold that no quota charges while it holds it: a message's bytes at `MAX_MESSAGE_BYTES` and the larger list beside them, 69,632 bytes on the development Mac (`da52a27`). The first program's figure is 225,408 bytes and its launch pays 364,672 ahead. The calls are not reordered: charging `message_bytes` before the copy would put `limit` before `bad_handle`, against D5's order. `SOURCE_PROVENANCE.md`, under "Task S4-3: the answers to its review", lists what each of the twenty calls can hold while it is preempted: only `channel_write` (69,632 bytes) and `channel_read` (66,560) hold anything no quota charges, and `call_temporaries` counts the larger.
- **B2, reordered:** security invariant (3) did not hold for another creator's memory object mapped in a process at its end. Now the objects a process's mappings held are dropped at its last reap, after its address space has gone and before `TERMINATED` (`da52a27`), and the boot proof shows it (`5bad1b8`). S3-5b's quota clause reads the account at `TERMINATED`, the moment the charge is now promised back; `ending()` turning `Some` earlier promises nothing to a program.
- **B3, B4, B10:** the proof counts each page a launch maps apart from what it paid, refuses a second thread to a process given one, and settles the machine before each snapshot (`5bad1b8`); controls KB-7 to KB-9 and, for B1, KB-10.
- **Wording corrected:** "nothing is made" is "nothing is left", which is what a snapshot shows, nothing allocated before the payment being shown by reading `Process::with_origin`. Invariant (6), "no system call gains a check", leaves out one comparison: the fix to `prepare_run` adds a run's records against the space's most pages to `memory_map`'s path (CL37 says so). The fix changes one corner's status the right way (B6): a run past the space's pages now gets `limit` whatever memory is left, as item 142 says.
- **Records:** O9 is proven for what the proof shows, with each kind's cost resting on reading; O11 gains kept and retired kernel stacks, a retired handle slot, and the smaller cases of B2; B19's model names everything the reserve takes.
- **Gates after the answers,** under Rust 1.100.0, on `45bae7f` (code as at `5bad1b8`, the last commit that changes code), in one turn of the machine, a clean tree before and after: `cargo xtask check` passed in 387 s (1,827 host tests passed, none failed, 27 ignored; Loom, 53 models and 78 controls caught; every gate); `cargo xtask boot-test` passed 11 of 11 in 159 s; `cargo xtask boot-matrix` passed 124 scenarios with none failed in 3,037 s, every kernel boot with a generator ending "66 of 67; failed: speculation controls" and the one without ending "59 of 61; 6 did not run". The quick gates ran first.

### 57. ADR-0005 Amendment 10, I1: a port's creator pays for its index

Sent by the coordinating session on 2026-11-13, after task S4-3 was merged into the fourth batch. Written here on 2026-11-13, before any code. It builds decision item 282 (ADR-0005 Amendment 10, I1), which the owner accepted on 2026-11-13 after task S4-3 measured a port's index and found that it never shrinks while its port is open. It closes row K7 of the system-call threat model, which S4-3 left open on that point alone, before S4-4's directory, the first long-lived service whose clients come and go.

| Field | |
|---|---|
| Task ID | TASKS item 57, on branch `claude/elegant-benz-1983ff`, fast-forwarded to `land/s4-part2` at `1b8688a` (the merge of task S4-3) and with `docs/decision-282` at `31249b7` merged in (`00f9854`), with ledger row CL38 added in a commit of its own (`3ea50d5`), in the coordinating session's text, until pull request 29 reaches the batch. |
| Objective | No slot of a port's index exists uncounted. (1) **The kind.** `quota::ObjectLimits` gains `bindings_per_port`: the most bindings armed at once on any one port the process creates. Every `ObjectLimits` value in the tree names it (G6); `ObjectLimits::NONE` gives zero, so a port whose creator was given none accepts no binding. (2) **The bound.** A port is made bounded by its creator's value: the framework's `Port` gains a bound on its bindings armed and reserved at once, set when it is made, and `Port::reserve` refuses a reservation that would pass it, under the port's lock where `reserved` already changes, so concurrent binds cannot pass it together. The refusal comes before the binding's two nodes are allocated, so `limit` comes before `no_memory` in D5's order, after the binder's own `bindings` quota (F2, I1). `port::bind` answers it `limit`, with the binder's charge given back: no binding, no charge, no slot. (3) **The figure.** Each port's part of its creator's figure (G5) is the port's block and its index at the bound: the most slots the index can hold at that bound, its old block during a growth, each rounded as the heap rounds it. Worked through from the index's growth rule: while bindings armed and reserved stay under a bound n ≥ 1, the index grows only from a capacity under n, to at most max(4, 2n − 1) slots. The framework states that rule in code beside the index, a host test drives the real index against it, and the kernel's build checks that the figure's copy of it agrees. A binding's own cost no longer counts slots of the index: the port's creator pays them. **Not in this task:** the right value for a real service (I1's "what stays unknown"); a call that changes a port's bound after it is made; anything of S4-4. |
| Source ownership | This task's session, on `claude/elegant-benz-1983ff`. **Shared files, and how far:** `framekernel/frame/sync/src/ports.rs` (the framework's: the port's bound, its check in `reserve`, the refusal in `Reservation::try_new`, the index rule, and their host tests; a Loom model if the coordinating session asks for one, since no lock is added and the check sits under the lock that `reserved` already takes); `framekernel/src/quota.rs` (the kind, the figure's port part, host tests); `framekernel/src/port.rs` (the port made bounded, the refusal as `limit`); `framekernel/src/process.rs` (`costs`: the binding without index slots, the index slot for the port's part, the build's check of the rule); each file that writes an `ObjectLimits` value (`ipc_proof/core_pin.rs`, `ipc_proof/events_ports.rs`, and the proofs that use `ObjectLimits::NONE` unchanged): the new field, set to the most bindings armed at once on any port that process makes, as its proof arms them; `framekernel/src/ipc_proof/kernel_bound.rs` (a clause); xtask's markers. Records: the threat rows K7, K5, O9, O11; ADR-0005's Amendment 10 status line; ADR-0013's row and controls; `SOURCE_PROVENANCE.md`, `STATUS.md`, this file, the matrix row A05-I1. |
| Architecture contract | ADR-0005 Amendment 10, I1 (decision item 282); Amendment 8, G5 and G6; Amendment 7, F2, F7 and F15; D5's order; ADR-0013 R1, R6 and R7; ADR-0018 V3, V5 and V8 (ledger row CL38); CLAUDE.md 7.8 §13, §28, §30, §53, §58 and §78. **No ABI or format decision is made here:** no call's shape, status or order changes beyond what I1 decided; `user-abi` stays 0.7. Choices of the kernel's, put to the coordinating session with this contract: the bound counts bindings armed and reservations in flight together (the index holds a slot for each), which is what keeps the index within the rule; a port's bound is fixed when it is made. |
| Relevant specifications | None outside the repository. **No source of any other operating system is opened** (CLAUDE.md §3). |
| Required dependencies | `land/s4-part2` at `1b8688a`, whose gates the keeper is running, or the head that replaces it; `docs/decision-282` at `31249b7`. No crate from outside the workspace (§5). No owner answer awaited. |
| Security invariants | (1) **Bounded at all times.** A port's bindings armed and reserved at once never pass its bound, from any number of processors: the check and the increment of `reserved` are one step under the port's lock. (2) **A refused bind changes nothing:** no binding, no charge to the binder, no slot, no allocation. (3) **Every slot counted.** While a port exists its index never holds more than the figure's port part counts, growth included; when the port goes, its index goes with it. (4) **No default.** Every `ObjectLimits` names `bindings_per_port`; zero accepts no binding. (5) **Order.** The binder's own `bindings` quota is checked before the port's bound, and both before any storage (F2, D5). (6) **No new lock, and nothing on a hot path but one comparison at a bind** (CL38). |
| Performance constraints | None set (CLAUDE.md §53, decision item 115). **Unmeasured, owed,** with the model in CL38: one comparison under the port's lock at each bind, and a larger figure for each process given the kind (the index at its bound, about 2n slots of the index's size each port), which lowers how many processes the root admits. **None, by construction,** on every other call. Reported, judged by nothing: the figure a port adds at the bounds the proofs use. |
| Required tests | **Host (framework, `ports.rs`):** a port bounded at n takes n reservations or bindings armed and refuses the next, with nothing taken; one given back makes room for exactly one; a bound of zero takes none; the index's capacity never passes the stated rule, for bounds 1 to a few hundred and any order of reserve, bind, fire, cancel and close; concurrent reservations from several threads never pass the bound. **Host (`quota.rs`):** the port part of a figure, kind by kind, at bounds 0, 1, 2 and many, and the binding's part without index slots. **Build:** the figure's copy of the rule agrees with the framework's for every bound up to a few thousand. **Boot (a clause of "the kernel-wide bound"):** a port whose creator was given `bindings_per_port: 2`, bound by another process: two binds are armed, a third is `limit` and leaves the binder's charge, the port's armed count, the heap and the frames as they were; once one binding fires, a bind is accepted again; a port whose creator was given none refuses the first bind. **Negative controls**, planted in an export and recorded in ADR-0013: the bound not checked in `reserve` (the third bind accepted); the bound counting armed bindings and not reservations (a host test of concurrent reservations fails); the port part left out of the figure (a host test fails); the refusal mapped to `no_memory` (the clause fails on the status). |
| Actual deliverables | The bound in the framework with its host tests; the kind, the figure's port part and their host tests; the bounded port and the refusal in the kernel; the explicit field in every limits value; the boot clause and its markers; ADR-0013's row and controls; the threat rows, with K7 closed for what is built; the matrix row A05-I1; STATUS, provenance and this file. Small commits that each build. Nothing pushed. |
| Acceptance conditions | On the last commit that changes code: the quick gates first (`cargo fmt --check`, `cargo xtask progress`, the document gates), then `cargo xtask check`, `boot-test` and `boot-matrix` once, under `cargo xtask exclusive`, on the development Mac under Rust 1.100.0; every test above passes and every control fails where aimed. **A05-I1** to `built`: a port's bindings armed at once never pass its creator's `bindings_per_port`, a bind past it is `limit` with nothing changed, and the index at that bound is in the creator's figure, shown under QEMU only (scope `both/hw`, so not `done`). An independent review if the coordinating session arranges one. **Not shown:** anything on a physical machine or under KVM; the right bound for a real service. |

**Rulings of the coordinating session (2026-11-13), on the contract above.** It accepted the design, (a) to (e) as sent with the contract, and the framework change, and asked for a Loom model. None goes to the owner; the coordinating session tells the owner how I1 was read.

- **Reservations in flight count with bindings armed.** A reservation holds a slot of the port's index, so the figure can bound the index only if the bound counts every slot. The consequence, written here and in row K7: under a race, a bind can be refused with `limit` while fewer than the bound are armed, because another bind is in flight; a retry after that one completes or fails can succeed. The status, and its place in D5's order, are unchanged.
- **The cost statement:** CL38 stays as the coordinating session wrote it, since the keeper adds the identical row when pull request 29 reaches the batch. Beside it, here: a binding's own cost no longer counts slots of the index, so a binder's figure falls by eight slots a binding, and a port's creator's figure rises by the index at its bound.
- **Loom:** one model of two reservations racing for a port bounded at one, beside `origin-sync`'s other port models so that `cargo xtask check` runs it: at most one holds a place and the other is refused. Its negative control removes the check and must be caught.

**Result (2026-11-13): built and gated; not landed and not pushed. An independent review is to come.** The evidence is in `SOURCE_PROVENANCE.md`, under "TASKS item 57", and the controls in ADR-0013 (KB-11 to KB-14, and the Loom control `reserve_ignores_the_port_s_bound`). Code in `149a9f4`, `5629cbb` and `5e5ead9` (the index rule's host test with cancels and the port's close, which the contract asked for and its first version left out). Gates on `1253fee` (code as at `5e5ead9`, the last commit that changes code), under Rust 1.100.0, in one turn of the machine, a clean tree before and after: `cargo xtask check` passed in 432 s (1,832 tests passed, the 54 Loom models among them, none failed, 27 ignored; 79 Loom controls caught; every gate, the cost gate with 38 ledger rows and the provenance gate with 26 crates among them); `cargo xtask boot-test` passed 11 of 11 in 165 s; `cargo xtask boot-matrix` passed 124 scenarios with none failed in 2,947 s, each of its 21 kernel boots with a generator ending "66 of 67 proofs passed; failed: speculation controls" (decision item 154) and the one without ending "59 of 61 proofs passed; 6 did not run". Where it differs from the contract:

- **The Loom model's control** is in `cargo xtask loom`, beside the other port models' controls, not in ADR-0013's table of boot controls.
- **A binding's cost** is its two nodes, 192 bytes on the development Mac, down from 448: the eight slots of a port's index it counted are its port's creator's now.
- **The values given:** each S3-6 and S4-2 proof process at the most bindings armed at once on any port it creates, as its script, or another process's, arms them: 4, 2, 1 or 0 (`SOURCE_PROVENANCE.md` lists each). Every such proof passed with them.

**The independent review (2026-11-14) and its answers.** One AI reviewer read `3ea50d5..6cb8bd6` from an export: no High, one Medium, six Low, five informational (`docs/engineering/REVIEW_TASKS57_2026-11-14.md`, with the Resolution). The coordinating session's rulings: one grower of a port's index at a time, with a Loom model and its control (T1); a bind that would queue at once is refused by the same reading as the race, since it holds a place (T3; the coordinating session takes it to the owner); the shared-port case recorded for S4-4 (T7); CL38 corrected, no lock-free change (T6). Code in `d4bdaf1` (framework) and `597f174` (kernel). Where the answers differ from what the contract said: invariant (6) is one more acquisition of the port's lock at a bind, not one comparison; invariant (2) holds of the outcome, and under a race a refusal can leave the index grown within the rule, or come after the two nodes were made and freed; and KB-11, "the bound not checked in reserve" in the contract, was planted as a port with no bound at all, the check in `reserve` alone being the Loom control's. Gates after the answers: on `0d59c85` (code as at `597f174`, the last commit that changes code), under Rust 1.100.0, in one turn of the machine, a clean tree before and after: `cargo xtask check` passed in 432 s (1,835 tests passed, the 55 Loom models among them, none failed, 27 ignored; 81 Loom controls caught; every gate, the cost gate with 38 ledger rows and the provenance gate with 26 crates among them); `cargo xtask boot-test` passed 11 of 11 in 164 s, the kernel boot's port clause ending "to the byte: true (160 bytes, 160 counted)" and every S4-2 proof passing with the every-call process given 1; `cargo xtask boot-matrix` passed 124 scenarios with none failed in 2,949 s, each of its 21 kernel boots with a generator ending "66 of 67 proofs passed; failed: speculation controls" (decision item 154) and the one without ending "59 of 61 proofs passed; 6 did not run".


### 40. Identity maps for firmware-reserved memory (TASKS item 40)

Written 2026-11-12, before any code, by the session that builds it (sent by the coordinating session the same day).

| Field | |
|---|---|
| Task ID | TASKS item 40. It closes ADR-0007 questions 4 and 8's deadline (decision items 8 and 12; item 229 keeps it for rented servers) and TASKS item 11's "IVMD semantics and placement". |
| Objective | A device the firmware names in an RMRR (VT-d) or IVMD (AMD-Vi) can be attached: its domain identity-maps every range named for it, with the permissions the firmware gives, and nothing else changes for any other device. A range the kernel cannot trust is not mapped, and its devices stay refused, as today. |
| Source ownership | This task's session: `framekernel/frame/src/iommu.rs`, `iommu/amd.rs`, `dmar.rs`, `ivrs.rs`, `vtd.rs`'s IOVA window, a new pure module for the ranges, `vm.rs`'s memory-map check, `framekernel/src/kernel.rs`'s call of `iommu::init`, `framekernel/src/storage.rs`'s new proofs, the test fixture (a new test-only UEFI crate under `tests/emulator/`), xtask's scenarios; the research ledger, threat T14, STATUS, SOURCE_PROVENANCE, the matrix rows A07-I4, A07-I11, A07-A9 and those the work changes. The coordinating session: ADR-0007's text for R1–R5 once answered, the decision sheet, and the merge into the third batch. |
| Architecture contract | ADR-0007 I2, I3, I4, I11, I12, A2, A3, A9 and questions 4 and 8; R1–R5 as answered (until then, their recommended options). ADR-0011 N6 and N12 (IOMMU programming is the framework's). No ABI or format changes: all of it is kernel-internal (ADR-0007, "Migration consequences": identity maps are additive). |
| Relevant specifications | VT-d Rev 5.20 (D51397-019) §3.15, §3.16, §8.4, §9.8; AMD 48882 Rev 3.11 §2.2.2.1 (EX), §5.2.2, §5.2.2.2 (Tables 111–113), MMIO 0020h and 0028h, Table 3. Both checked on 2026-11-12 as the vendors' latest. |
| Required dependencies | `land/s4` at `5871257`. The coordinating session's answers to R1–R5 before the landing; none needed to build on the recommended options. |
| Security invariants | (1) A domain maps only its own DMA frames and the reserved ranges the firmware names for its device; no other device's domain gains a page. (2) An identity-mapped range lies wholly in memory the memory map calls Reserved, RuntimeCode or RuntimeData, outside the interrupt range (and AMD's special range), clear of every platform register range, within the domain's address width, at most 4 GiB long; otherwise it is not mapped. (3) No IOVA the window hands out falls on an identity-mapped page, and `unmap` never removes one. (4) An IVMD's permissions are kept: a read-only range is never writable by the device. (5) A never-map IVMD's range is never mapped for the devices it covers. (6) The exclusion registers are never written. (7) Unattached devices keep default deny (I2): no context entry, a blocking DTE. (8) No new `unsafe` outside files the unsafe gate lists; the fixture's `unsafe` is test-only and listed. |
| Performance constraints | None set. One page-table entry per 4 KiB of reserved range per named device, made once at attach. |
| Required tests | The ledger's §6: host tests for every rule above at its boundaries, the window and unmap rules, the IVMD flags and placement, and the fixture's table rewrite parsed back by the kernel's own parsers; boot proofs under VT-d and AMD-Vi through NVMe with the fixture's tables (DMA both ways at the range's physical address; the NIC's domain without it; a bad range refused by name; under AMD-Vi a read-only range's write blocked and recorded, and 20h and exclusion ranges in every attached domain). Negative controls, each a temporary edit booted once and put back byte for byte: no identity maps; maps one page off; the memory-map check skipped; (AMD) read-only mapped read-write. Each must fail the boot. |
| Actual deliverables | The ranges module and its host tests; the identity maps in both drivers; the fixture crate and the xtask scenarios that boot it; the proofs; the records (ledger, T14, STATUS, provenance, matrix rows). Small commits, each of which builds and passes its tests. Nothing pushed. |
| Acceptance conditions | On the task's branch: `cargo xtask check` (the requirements and unsafe gates included), `cargo xtask boot-test` and `cargo xtask boot-matrix` pass; every new proof's controls are caught; T14, STATUS and the matrix rows follow. Then the coordinating session reads the piece, runs the gates on the merge and lands it in the third batch. Nothing here runs on physical hardware. |
